# SGVue Desktop — decisions

Every decision taken while building SGVue, in the order it was taken, with the reasoning and
the measurements that settled it. Moved out of `CLAUDE.md` unchanged, because it is paid for
in every session; `CLAUDE.md` keeps the one-line index, and a new decision is a line there and
a full row here.

> **Coordinates in this repository are synthetic.** Every coordinate quoted for the reference model — in these rows, in
> `PROGRESS.md`, in the parity READMEs and in test literals — was shifted by one whole-metre delta before the first push
> (2026-09-20), so the arithmetic is intact and the real site is never identified. The file's SHA-256 is likewise replaced.

> **Commit hashes** cited in these notes refer to the project's history before it was published on 2026-10-05, and do
> not resolve in this repository.

| Decision | Choice | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Desktop shell | **Electron 44** (bundled Chromium) | Node is installed, Rust is not. Same WebGPU/WebGL2 renderer on both OSes, so the design renders identically. |
| Build tooling | electron-vite 5 + **Vite 7** (electron-vite excludes Vite 8) + TypeScript 5.9 | Standard, one config for main/preload/renderer. |
| UI | React 19 + Zustand 5; renderer module in plain TypeScript | The design's logic class needs a real state library; markup/CSS ported verbatim. |
| 3D | **three 0.186.0** (`three/webgpu` + `three/tsl`), **WebGL2 by default**, **merged geometry per slot** | One mesh per element is ~12 fps on a real 144 MB model (Aquila); a `BatchedMesh` is one *driver* draw per instance and floods the GPU process (see 2026-09-17 below). A few dozen merged meshes are 87 draws a frame at 48 fps. |
| IFC parser | **web-ifc 0.0.77** (MPL-2.0), single-threaded build in a Web Worker, read-only adapter; **IfcOpenShell as ground truth in tests** | Client-side, IFC2X3/IFC4/IFC4X3, metres out. Lighter than bundling IfcOpenShell and far easier to package on Windows. |
| File access | `sgvue-file://` read-only protocol with single-use tokens + native drag-drop; parser reads a Blob in chunks (`OpenModelFromCallback`) | Main streams and never holds model bytes; no 600 MB IPC copy. |
| Power query for AI | **sql.js 1.14** in its own worker, `PRAGMA query_only`, SELECT-only gate, 10 s timeout by worker restart | Arbitrary joins for the assistant, no native module, cannot damage the model. |
| AI | **@anthropic-ai/sdk 0.125** in the Electron main process only, `claude-opus-5`, adaptive thinking, effort `medium`, manual streaming loop, key in `safeStorage` | Per-token text, tool-start narration for the designed busy row, explicit cache breakpoints, abort. Key never reaches the renderer. |
| Persistence | JSON in `userData` via main (settings, sessions, recents); exports via save dialog | Enumerable writers; sessions carry file paths. |
| Share link | `sgvue://s=<base64url>` registered scheme | Keeps the design's "copy link" affordance and "reopens the exact view" behaviour on desktop. |
| Packaging | electron-builder 26: macOS `.dmg` (arm64 + x64), Windows NSIS `.exe` (x64), unsigned; `asarUnpack` `.wasm` | Signing/notarisation owed. |
| Reuse | Port parser/georef/property modules from Marumi, Aquila and ifcgref | Proven on real files; their pitfalls are recorded below. |
| **2026-09-16 — Renderer sources live directly under `src/renderer/`** (not electron-vite's `src/renderer/src/`) | Matches the plan's repository layout, so `styles/`, `state/`, `viewer/` and `ai/` sit where the plan says. `@renderer` alias and `tsconfig.web.json` point at it. |
| **2026-09-16 — Dev CSP is produced from the production CSP by string replacement** in `electron.vite.config.ts` (`apply: 'serve'`), not by a second hand-written policy | One source of truth. The production meta tag is the real policy; dev adds only `'unsafe-inline'` (Vite's react-refresh preamble) and `ws://localhost:5173` (HMR). |
| **2026-09-16 — The Phase 0 screenshot tool lives in `scripts/screenshot.cjs`, outside `src/`** | Writing a PNG is a disk write. Only the enumerated writers may write — the plan's `settings.ts` and `sessions.ts`, with a third, `exports.ts`, reserved (not created then; it arrived 2026-09-25 with Schedules phase 4) — and a dev screenshot must not become another. |
| **2026-09-16 — One `import 'web-ifc'` serves the worker and the Node tests** | web-ifc's own `exports` map resolves the browser ESM build under Vite and `web-ifc-api-node` under vitest, so `ifc-source.ts` needs no environment branch. Only `locateFile` differs, and the worker injects it (Vite's `?url` asset). The fixture harness therefore runs the same code the app runs. |
| **2026-09-16 — `ReadOnlyIfcSource` exposes its sealed write stubs as a map** | The read-only guarantee has to be *testable*. `sealedWriteApi` lets a test call every forbidden method and see it throw, without ever handing out the `IfcAPI` object or spelling a forbidden name outside `readonly-list.ts`. |
| **2026-09-16 — `IfcPreDefinedPropertySet` values are indexed from the set's own attributes** | `IfcDoorLiningProperties`, `IfcWindowPanelProperties` and their siblings carry values as attributes, not as `IfcProperty` lines. IfcOpenShell reports them; skipping them lost `PanelOperation`, `LiningDepth` and the rest on every door. A set with no populated attributes is still recorded, so the index matches the ground truth exactly. |
| **2026-09-16 — `sample-model.js` is copied byte-identical into `src/renderer/dev/`** | `design-reference/` is the read-only specification and cannot carry the `.d.ts` the TypeScript build needs. `tests/unit/mock-adapter.test.ts` compares the two files byte for byte, so the copy cannot drift. |
| **2026-09-16 — Storey `h` is derived from the next storey's elevation, and absent on the topmost** | No IFC file declares a storey height. Deriving it from consecutive elevations is a stated derivation; inventing one for the top storey would be a placeholder. |
| **2026-09-16 — `sgvue-file://` must be registered with `corsEnabled: true`** | The renderer is loaded from `file://`, and Chromium refuses to fetch a custom scheme from it unless the scheme is CORS-enabled — the request never reaches the handler. Proved by `scripts/verify-worker.cjs`; set in `src/main/index.ts` in Phase 1b, ready for the real handler in Phase 8. |
| **2026-09-17 — Geometry is a second worker request, not part of the parse** | The tree, the storey list and the property card are usable the moment the index lands; geometry is another 4 s on a 138 MB model. Splitting them lets the shell fill in first, and lets a model be re-streamed (a new federation offset) without re-parsing. |
| **2026-09-17 — Vertices stay in each geometry's local space; the part matrix carries the placement** | It is what makes deduplication by `geometryExpressID` possible at all — 35 119 unique geometries for 67 364 parts on the reference model. Marumi transformed every vertex and could not instance anything. |
| **2026-09-17 — Geometry deduplication tables are per chunk** | `GeometryRecord` offsets point into *that chunk's* buffers, so a geometry used in two chunks is written twice. Making it model-wide would mean one unbounded buffer instead of ~1 M-vertex chunks, which is the memory rule. The summary still reports the model-wide unique count. |
| **2026-09-17 — Mirrored placements keep their winding** | A negative-determinant placement leaves triangles wound the other way. Marumi reversed the indices per placement; that is impossible on a shared instanced buffer, and unnecessary here because the reference renderer draws every element `side: DoubleSide` (`viewer-core.js:73`). |
| **2026-09-17 — A part's `bbox6` is the AABB of the transformed local AABB** | Exact per-vertex bounds would have to be recomputed for every one of the 67 364 parts, defeating the instancing. The conservative box is what three.js uses for instanced meshes, and picking filters by box before it walks triangles anyway. |
| **2026-09-17 — `GeometryChunkHeader.index`/`total` is a progress fraction, not a chunk number** | web-ifc's own stream counters restart at every IFC type, so they saw back to zero. The worker counts meshes against the product count read up front; the mock adapter, which has everything in hand, counts chunks. The contract states that the producer picks the unit. |
| **2026-09-17 — A federation slot is found by free-slot search and kept for the model's life** | Marumi's worst bug: after a delete, `models.length` repeats and the next model inherits the removed one's ids. `removeModel` therefore leaves every surviving id untouched and returns the dropped ids to prune, rather than remapping five separate maps. |
| **2026-09-17 — `guardSql` leaves `EXPLAIN` unwrapped** | `SELECT * FROM (EXPLAIN …)` is not valid SQLite — a query plan cannot be a subquery. `SELECT` and `WITH` are wrapped with `LIMIT 201`; `EXPLAIN` runs as written and the worker stops reading at the same 201 rows. |
| **2026-09-17 — The geometry contract is IFC Z-up metres, and `geometry-streamer.ts` is the only place the frames meet** | web-ifc's `flatTransformation` folds a Z-up → Y-up swap into every placement; IFC, the design's renderer, its mock federation and every annotation in this repository are Z-up. One constant rotation `(x, y, z)_Yup → (x, −z, y)_Zup` is composed on the left of the placement and the federation offset is chosen in the Z-up frame. The reference model's offset is `[12520, 23186, 4] m` and its height now spans z, −4.9…56.0 m, matching the storey ladder read from `IfcBuildingStorey`. |
| **2026-09-17 — ~~Per-instance RGBA on a `BatchedMesh`~~ → merged geometry per slot** (plan §3.7's other option). **This supersedes the four `BatchedMesh` decisions below it, which are kept only so the route is not taken again.** | **A `BatchedMesh` costs one *driver-level* draw per visible instance.** WebGPU has no multi-draw in three 0.186 at all, and on macOS ANGLE/Metal emulates `WEBGL_multi_draw` by replaying per draw with its own state — so `renderer.info` reporting "44 calls" was measuring the wrong thing. At 67 364 instances, doubled by the shadow pass, it floods the Electron GPU helper process: **three kernel panics on this Mac on 2026-09-17** (`watchdog timeout: no checkins from watchdogd`, compressor 100 %), every panic report's `processByPid` naming the GPU helper at **~168–170 GB**. The third was on **WebGL2**, after the first safety attempt, so it is not a backend problem. Measured under a bounded guard: flat at 634 MB through parse and streaming, then **634 → 5 461 MB inside one 0.5 s poll** the instant the batches were uploaded and the first frame drawn. So each slot's parts are now baked into federation space with their own `matrix16` and concatenated into one `BufferGeometry` per slot and family (`batches.ts`), drawn by one `Mesh` — **87 draws a frame** on that model, GPU footprint flat at 1.74–1.79 GB across 48 s of orbiting, 41–51 fps, hover 3.3 ms. The price is deduplication: 5 754 413 stored vertices expand to 14 621 800 (×2.54). Design parity moved by at most 0.15 of 255 on every 2a state. |
| **2026-09-17 — Per-part colour and state live in one `DataTexture`, not in the geometry** | A merged mesh has one material for tens of thousands of parts, so the state travels with the vertex: every vertex carries its part's index in a `partIndex` attribute and the material reads RGBA for it with `textureLoad` (`texelFetch`, core WebGL2, no filtering). `part-state.ts` is 1.1 MB for the reference model's 67 364 parts, and a write is two array stores plus a dirty flag — the frame loop turns any number of writes into at most one upload. |
| **2026-09-17 — The part alpha channel carries state as well as opacity** | 0 is hidden; below 0.5 the part is see-through; above 1 it is opaque and glowing by the excess; `+4` on top of any of those tags the hovered part. That is what keeps the reference's `selMat` (emissive ×0.35) and `hlMat` (×0.15) without a second table. |
| **2026-09-17 — Every solid slot has a second `Mesh` over the *same* `BufferGeometry` for the see-through pass** | `depthWrite` belongs to a material and a merged mesh has one material for thousands of parts, so a see-through part inside an opaque mesh either hides what is behind it or stops the opaque ones occluding. Two meshes over one geometry split them with three's own `maskNode`: the solid material discards anything below 0.5 alpha, the see-through one discards anything at or above it, so a part is rasterised by exactly one of them; the solid mesh stays genuinely opaque and writes depth, the see-through one blends over it afterwards writing none. `maskNode` also reaches the **shadow** pass (`Renderer._getShadowNodes` uses `maskShadowNode \|\| maskNode`), which is what stops a hidden part casting. Two earlier attempts are recorded because they are the obvious ones and both fail: `material.depthNode` renders the scene black under an orthographic camera in 0.186, and pushing a see-through fragment's clip-space `z` to `w` stops it occluding but also stops it tinting anything opaque in front of it. |
| **2026-09-17 — The renderer no longer reaches past three's public API anywhere** | The `BatchedMesh` ghost twin borrowed eight private fields because there is no supported way to draw one batch's instances under two materials. Two ordinary `Mesh`es over one `BufferGeometry` need none of them. `tests/unit/batches.test.ts` asserts every one of those eight names is absent from `batches.ts`, and that it never constructs a `BatchedMesh` again. |
| **2026-09-17 — Slot caps are vertex and index counts, not an instance count** | Merging bakes the placement in, so a geometry used by three parts is written three times and a chunk that fitted one batch may become two slots. The caps (65 k parts / 1 M vertices / 3 M indices) only decide where an oversized chunk splits; the 137.9 MB model lands in 35 slots. |
| **2026-09-17 — Picking has no acceleration structure at all** | The Marumi rule stands — filter by each element's world bounding box, sort by entry distance, then walk triangles, stopping when nothing nearer is possible — and with the placement baked in, the precise phase is a direct world-space walk of one part's contiguous index range: no instance matrix, no inverse, no scale conversion. A `MeshBVH` over a **merged** slot cannot be restricted to one part's range at query time, so it would have to test the whole slot and reject by lookup, and building one per ~300 k-triangle slot is a multi-hundred-millisecond stall on the main thread. Measured on the 137.9 MB model: **3.3 ms median** per hover (min 2.7, max 5.3) against Marumi's 222 ms with `Mesh.raycast`. three-mesh-bvh is no longer imported by the renderer. |
| **2026-09-17 — Every scene constant is the reference's value × (radius / 27.5 m)** | The design's ±48 m shadow frustum, 600 m ground, 240 m grid, zoom clamps and sun stand-off were authored for its own mock federation, whose bounding sphere is 27.5 m. Expressed as a ratio, the mock renders with the reference's exact numbers (the camera matches its `debug()` to the last digit) and a 425 m model gets proportional ones. `normalBias` scales with them because it is a world-space offset against a shadow texel; `bias` is in depth units and does not. |
| **2026-09-17 — The solid family is genuinely opaque** (`transparent: false`) | Once the see-through mesh draws every ghosted part, the solid mesh only ever draws parts at alpha 1 — so it renders in the opaque pass and writes depth exactly as the reference's ordinary materials do, with no blending and no sorting. It also removes the `shadowSide` workaround an earlier draft needed: the shadow pass copies `transparent` from the source material and renders a `DoubleSide` *transparent* material twice. |
| **2026-09-17 — The hemisphere-light difference against the design is an upstream bug r170 had and 0.186 fixed; we keep 0.186's** | `HemisphereLightNode.setup()` differs by one line: r170 (`src/nodes/lighting/HemisphereLightNode.js:45`) computes `normalView.dot( lightDirectionNode )`, 0.186 (`:76`) computes `normalWorld.dot( … )`. `lightPosition()` is byte-identical in both and returns a **world**-space position (r170 `src/nodes/accessors/Lights.js:20–26`, 0.186 `:84–90`), so r170 dots a view-space normal with a world-space direction and its hemisphere term depends on where the camera is. Measured on the design's own mock: the prototype renders the *same* white ground plane at 220, 210 and 200 of 255 from the plan, iso and section cameras; ours renders 218 / 218 / 217. Reproducing it would mean a wall that changes brightness as you orbit, forever, against no reference but r170. `tests/parity/phase2a/README.md` records what it costs us. |
| **2026-09-17 — `fitBox` uses `sin`, not `tan`** | `viewer-core.js` L303 fits the bounding *sphere* to the cone of vision: `r / sin(fov/2) × 1.08`. `tan` pulls the camera 10 % closer — 63.7 m instead of 70.3 m on the design's own federation — which is visible in a side-by-side. |
| **2026-09-17 — The orbit and dolly pivot fallback is a copy, not the target itself** | `viewer-core.js` L276 and L296 write `pivot || t` and then mutate `t` in place against it, so with no pivot the two alias and the camera target collapses to the world origin. Its callers almost always pass a pick or a ground-plane point, so it is rarely reached; reproducing it would be reproducing a defect, not a look. |
| **2026-09-17 — `PCFShadowMap`, because three 0.186 removed `PCFSoftShadowMap`** | Both backends warn and fall back anyway. 0.186's PCF is a five-tap Vogel disk scaled by `light.shadow.radius`, so it is still soft — just not the r170 filter the design was authored against. |
| **2026-09-17 — `backend: 'auto'` means WebGL2; WebGPU is opt-in by name only** (`scene.ts`, `forceWebGLFor`) | It read WebGPU-first until the panics. WebGPU is the path with no multi-draw at all, it was where the first two panics happened, and nothing about the merged renderer has yet been measured on it at a real size. `scripts/lib/backend.cjs` refuses it to dev scripts unless `SGVUE_BACKEND=webgpu` **and** `SGVUE_ALLOW_WEBGPU=1` **and** the content is the 590-part mock. Phase 10 reconsiders it with the performance and packaging work; the Preferences dialog in Phase 9 gets a "Renderer" setting (auto / WebGL2) mirroring the design's prop. |
| **2026-09-17 — Electron is started only through `scripts/safe-run.cjs`, and every guard reads `phys_footprint`** | `app.getAppMetrics().memory.workingSetSize` and `ps rss` are **blind** to GPU memory: at the instant a run's GPU process went 634 → 5 461 MB, `getAppMetrics` still read 106 MB. `/usr/bin/footprint -p <pid>` is the number the panic report itself uses, and `kern.memorystatus_vm_pressure_level ≥ 2` is the state that precedes a watchdog timeout. `scripts/lib/electron-guard.cjs` polls both every 250 ms and `app.exit(2)`s over 2 500 MB GPU / 6 000 MB renderer / 25 s; `src/main/gpu-guard.ts` does the same in the shipped app at `max(3 072 MB, 40 % of RAM)` (see 2026-09-18 below), stopping the viewer and then crashing the renderer rather than the machine. `safe-run.cjs` refuses to start a second dev Electron and kills any survivor afterwards — it matches on the executable path, never `pgrep -f`, which also matches a shell whose command line mentions Electron. |
| **2026-09-17 — The SQL bridge keeps `db.export()`, the worker does not** | The ten-second limit is enforced by terminating the worker, because nothing can interrupt `sqlite3_step`. Keeping the bytes on the renderer side is what makes the rebuild afterwards cost an open rather than a re-parse. |
| **2026-09-18 — `tb.filter` lights for a live step as well as for the open card** | The design writes `on(s.card === 'filter' \|\| s.filterOn)` (`:1951`), and `filterOn` is one of the undeclared keys plan §3.5 defect 8 drops — nothing ever set it, so the prototype's button only ever lit for the open card. The control one place over settles the intent: `section: on(s.card === 'section' \|\| !!s.section.kind)`. "Live" is any step switched **on**, highlight steps included and regardless of whether its rules carry a value yet, which is how the temporary-state banner counts steps. It is the one visible difference in the Phase 5 parity set, and it is a 30 × 30 button in one state. |
| **2026-09-18 — the undo stacks are module state, not store state** | The design keeps them as instance fields (`this.undoStack`, `:1016`) for a reason: a snapshot must not re-render anything. `createUndoHistory()` in `src/shared/undo.ts` is that pair of arrays, and only `canUndo` / `canRedo` reach the store. `resetHistory()` exists so a test can start from an empty history, since the module outlives a `useShell.setState(INITIAL, true)`. |
| **2026-09-18 — every filter-stack operation is a pure function that returns the next stack** | The design's `addStep` / `updStep` / `moveStep` / `dropStep` each call `this.up(...)` themselves. Splitting the arithmetic out of the mutation keeps **one** place that snapshots undo, and makes the stack testable with no store, no viewer and no DOM. `moveStep` returns the **same array** when the move would leave the ends, which is how the store knows there is nothing to snapshot — the design returns before reaching its own `up()`. |
| **2026-09-18 — applying a saved set gives each step a distinct colour** | `applyFilterSet` maps over the saved steps calling `newStep`, which reads the **live** stack for the colours already taken (`:1671`, `:952`) — the live stack does not change during the map, so a three-step set restores with all three steps the same colour. The auto-pick is built from the stack **being assembled** here, so three highlight steps come back in three colours. The design's own intent (`:954`, "the next unused") and plan §4 Phase 7's acceptance ("three highlight steps show three colours") both say so; a one-step set is identical either way, which is every set in the parity capture. |
| **2026-09-18 — `restoreView` snapshots undo before it restores** | The design's `restoreView` writes all five `VIS_KEYS` with a bare `setState` (`:1731`), so a restored viewpoint is the one visibility change ⌘Z cannot walk back. Its own `activate` calls `pushUndo()` for exactly this reason (`:1051`). The same viewpoint also saved three keys — `filterOn`, `rules`, `filterMode` — its state had already stopped carrying, which is plan §3.5 defect 1; both are fixed together. |
| **2026-09-18 — a viewpoint's `active` and a restored undo snapshot are clamped to a loaded model** | Both can name a model unloaded since; `setPickable` on a key nothing matches makes the **whole** federation inert with no control left to undo it. `commitModels` already drops a dead `active`, and this is the same rule one layer down. |
| **2026-09-18 — the mock adapter merges each colour group into one part** | `viewer-core.js:119` builds one mesh per colour group with `mergeBoxes`, so the prototype's Geometry row counts groups: a wall split around its opening by `wallBoxes` is **one** solid. Mapping each box to a part made it read four, which was Phase 4's one open mock-only difference. Vertices are written in group-local space with the part matrix carrying the placement, so the dedup the real pipeline depends on is kept (the mock's federation goes 590 → 516 parts). Measured: the rendered frame is **identical**, mean 0.000 over the whole window in both themes. |
| **2026-09-18 — The assistant's tools are one catalogue in `shared/`, and every entry declares `kind: 'read' \| 'view'`** | The names have to be identical in three places — the schema the model is sent, the executor that runs it, and the guard that checks it — and a tool that exists in only two of them is a silent failure. `src/shared/tool-schemas.ts` is the single list; `tests/readonly-guard.test.ts` fails the build if a tool is neither kind, if a tool name contains a mutating verb (`write`, `edit`, `delete`, `rename`, `create`, `update`, …), if a tool takes a file-system-shaped input, or if a catalogue entry has no executor or an executor has no entry. There is **no file-system tool of any kind**. |
| **2026-09-18 — The design's tool schemas are verbatim but for two invisible corrections** | A tool schema is sent to the model, never rendered, so the fidelity contract's subject is the *behaviour* it produces. (1) **One `combine` enum**: the design writes `replace\|append` on `set_filter_stack` and `replace\|add` on `apply_visibility`, so the same word meant two things; both are `replace\|append` and the instruction line and description say `append` (plan §3.5 defect 3). (2) **`additionalProperties: false`** everywhere, which `strict: true` requires. |
| **2026-09-18 — `color_models` is the one tool that is not `strict`** | Its `map` is a free-form `{modelKey: hex}` object. Strict mode forces `additionalProperties: false`, which on an object with no declared properties rejects every key the tool exists to carry. Optional properties elsewhere stay optional: Anthropic's strict mode copies `required` through **as given** rather than expanding it to every property — checked against the SDK's own transform, `node_modules/@anthropic-ai/sdk/lib/transform-json-schema.js`, which pops `required` unchanged. That same transform demotes `enum` into the description, so if the API ever refuses a schema the gateway drops `strict` from every tool once per session and retries, rather than failing the turn. |
| **2026-09-18 — Main owns a byte-stable API transcript and rolls it back on anything but success** | The one state the transcript may never reach is a `tool_use` with no `tool_result` after it: every later turn 400s and the only way out is losing the conversation. So abort, refusal, `max_tokens`, the round cap, the wall clock and any thrown error all slice the transcript back to its pre-turn length, and a tool that fails comes back as `is_error: true` rather than as a dropped block. The renderer's display transcript is separate and keyed by `turnId`, so a failed turn leaves a message on screen and nothing in the API history. |
| **2026-09-18 — Three cache breakpoints, and the previous turn's is stripped as it becomes history** | Render order is tools → system → messages, and caching is a prefix match. Breakpoint 1 is the static contract, 2 is the model schema (rebuilt only when the federation changes), 3 rides on the **current user turn** — which is what pays inside a turn, because a twelve-round tool loop re-sends everything up to it on every round. The API allows four; leaving the old turn's breakpoint in place would mean five by the third turn and a 400, so `decache()` removes it on commit. The live view state deliberately sits **outside** the prefix, as a `role:'system'` message after the user turn. |
| **2026-09-18 — The live view state travels as a mid-conversation `role:'system'` message** | It changes every turn, so putting it in `system[]` would invalidate the whole cached prefix in front of it every time. `claude-opus-5` accepts `{ role: 'system' }` inside `messages` with no beta header (`BetaMessageParam.role` is `'user' \| 'assistant' \| 'system'` in the installed SDK); it must follow a user message and may not be `messages[0]`. If the API ever refuses the role, the gateway folds every system message into the preceding user turn as a trailing text block — once, for the rest of the session. |
| **2026-09-18 — Every tool runs in the renderer; main never holds model data** | Main has the SDK, the key and the transcript. It has no index, no geometry and no database. A `tool_use` becomes `ai:tool:exec` to that window, the executor runs against the frozen index / SQL worker / view store, and **only `forModel`** comes back — JSON with real counts, per-OR-group counts, capped samples with `truncated`, and `valid_values` when nothing matched. A tool that does not answer inside its budget (20 s, 60 s for `query_sql` and `clash_check`) is answered *for* it, because silence would orphan the `tool_use`. |
| **2026-09-18 — Tool results are structured JSON with the design's sentence kept as `message`** | Plan §3.5 defect 9: the prototype returned prose, so the model had to parse its own tool results back out of a sentence. Every result is now an object — and it still carries the design's exact reply sentence, because the reply the *user* reads is unchanged. |
| **2026-09-18 — Every view tool goes through the store actions the buttons call** | The design's own note (`SGVue.dc.html:1214`): an AI action is indistinguishable from a manual one and lands on the same undo stack. Nothing in `executors/view.ts` writes state directly, so the 5 % scope guard, the temporary-state frame, the per-turn snapshot and ⌘Z all keep working with no second implementation. The pending entry carries the **patch** — `applyPending` hands it straight to `up()` — never a label and a patch that can drift apart (`BUILD_PLAN.md` §6 pitfall 21). |
| **2026-09-18 — `clash_check` runs on a uniform spatial hash, and a test asserts it equals brute force** | Plan §3.5 defect 6. The prototype's nested loop gave up at 400 000 tested pairs, which two real discipline models reach long before the answer does. B's boxes are hashed by **centre**; an A box scans only the cells its own half-extent, B's largest half-extent and the tolerance can reach, so no pair is missed and none is invented. `clashPairsBrute` is kept solely as the thing the test compares against, on seeded random boxes at four tolerances and on the design's own federation (442 candidates, ARC × STR). |
| **2026-09-18 — The audit, the quantity totals and the suggestions are local arithmetic** | `SGVue.dc.html:1281`'s own comment: no model round-trip, no invented numbers. `seedAudit` greets a fresh federation as the first turn, and it is seeded on the batch that takes the federation from **empty** to loaded — a later add or removal must not wipe the conversation already on screen. |
| **2026-09-18 — The API key is encrypted by the OS and has no getter anywhere** | `safeStorage` ciphertext in `userData/settings.json`; `apiKey()` is main-only and is called from one place. The renderer learns `hasKey: boolean` and nothing else — there is no `getApiKey` on the bridge, in main's IPC table, or in the settings view, and both the unit test and the Electron smoke test assert it. `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in the environment wins, so a dev run never touches the stored one. When the OS has no usable secret store, **nothing is stored** and the dialog says why: losing a key silently is worse than refusing it. |
| **2026-09-18 — The disk-writer guard matches calls, not words** | The assistant's system prompt contains the design's own sentence "If asked to edit, rename, reclassify, fix or write anything into the model", and the bare-word regex reported `prompt.ts` as a disk writer. The pattern now requires the opening parenthesis (`writeFile(`, `rename(`, `mkdirSync(`, …), and the test additionally asserts that every permitted writer still matches it — so the tightening cannot quietly neuter the guard. |
| **2026-09-18 — Preferences is the one allowed new surface, and it opens only from the native menu** | The fidelity contract's allowed deviations put the API key, model, effort, renderer backend and "Data sent to AI" behind Preferences… (⌘,), because they have no designed home and no designed surface may gain a control. It is built from the design's own tokens and card idiom, sits at z-index 40 over everything, and `autoHideMenuBar` is now **false** so the menu is reachable on Windows. The Electron smoke test clicks the real menu item and reads the dialog back. |
| **2026-09-17 — `tests/fixtures/*.expected.json` is git-ignored** | The generated ground truth carries a real project's metadata — file name, storey names, GUIDs, site latitude and longitude, three elements' property sets — for the same reason `samples/` is ignored. Regenerate it locally with `scripts/expected-from-ifcopenshell.py`. |
| **2026-09-18 — The five designed upload stages are driven by real worker milestones under two rules** | The design's stages are a `setTimeout` chain over a mock; ours are a worker on a real file. `shared/upload.ts`'s `stageStep` reconciles them: a stage is never shown **ahead of** real progress, and never for **less than** the design's own 420 ms minimum (`420 + random×360`, whose lower bound is the floor). On the 137.9 MB model the minimum never binds and the row reports the parse (reading file 487 ms → parsing entities 2 771 → building geometry 5 680 → indexing properties 12 485 → federating 12 917); on a 5 KB fixture it does, and the animation reads as designed. |
| **2026-09-18 — The design's stage *order* is kept, and the renderer's work was reordered to match it honestly** | The design shows `building geometry` before `indexing properties`; our worker indexes first. Rather than mislabel either, each displayed stage is unlocked by a milestone that has actually happened: `indexing properties` is the renderer deep-freezing the whole index into the model store (a real walk over every element and every property set), which happens *after* the geometry stream. Nothing claims work that has not been done. |
| **2026-09-18 — `.ifczip` is unzipped in the worker; `.ifcxml` is refused by name** | The design's drop zone advertises all three extensions, so silently answering "not an IFC file" for one of them would be worse than saying what is true. `fflate` 0.8.3 (pinned) unzips the single `.ifc` inside; an archive with none, or with several, is refused rather than guessed at. The recorded SHA-256 stays the **archive's**, because that is the file on disk and what a session and a share link name. `ifcXML is not supported` is the reason `.ifcxml` shows — an XML parser is a second full implementation of the schema and is not in scope. |
| **2026-09-18 — A `sgvue-file://` path must be *admitted* before it can be minted, and a token is single-use** | Admission is the only entrance and it is always a user act: the native dialog, a drop, the recents list, a stored session or a share link. It applies `realpath`, the extension, a regular-file check and the 600 MB cap, and stores the **resolved** path — so a symlink is followed once, at admission, and the token is bound to the target. The handler re-runs all four checks at fetch time (a symlink can be repointed in between), spends the token on the first request, and answers everything else with a bare 403. |
| **2026-09-18 — The session and the recents list are two files under `userData/sessions/`, written atomically** | `last.json` is the design's own `sessionPayload()` plus one `{ key, path, name, sha256 }` per model; `recents.json` is up to six files, newest first. A temporary file plus one rename, so a crash mid-save cannot leave half a session, and a corrupt file reads as "no session" rather than as an error — losing a session is annoying, refusing to start is worse. |
| **2026-09-18 — The recents list is capped at six** | `SGVue.dc.html:1938` spells the "open all N" count in words up to six and falls back to the digit above that. A seventh pill would read "open all 7 as a federation" — legal, and a sentence the design never writes. |
| **2026-09-18 — Filter sets and viewpoints stay in `localStorage`** | They carry no paths and no model data, they are scoped to a *building* rather than to a session, and they are the design's own storage under the design's own key names. Moving them behind `main/sessions.ts` would add a writer's worth of surface and gain no property. Phase 5's note that Phase 8 would move them is superseded. |
| **2026-09-18 — A launch share link travels in the renderer's own `location.hash`; a link that arrives later comes over IPC** | `SGVue.dc.html:866` reads `[#&]s=` out of the hash at mount, so the hash is the design's own path. It is also the only race-free one: `did-finish-load` can fire before React's first effect has registered an IPC listener, and a link sent then is simply lost — measured, and it is what made the first share-link smoke test fail. `main/index.ts` therefore puts a launch link in the hash and `open-url` / `second-instance` while running go over `link:open`. |
| **2026-09-18 — The preload imports channel *names*, never the zod schemas** | electron-vite externalises `dependencies` for main and preload, so a sandboxed preload that imports `ipc-contract.ts` emits `require("zod")`, fails to load **silently**, and leaves the renderer with no `window.sgvue` at all. `shared/ipc-channels.ts` carries the channel names and nothing else; the schemas run on the two sides that can run them. |
| **2026-09-18 — `file:open` accepts an injected answer when `!app.isPackaged`** | A native Open dialog cannot be driven by an automated test, and the whole chain behind it — admit → token → protocol stream → worker → rows — is exactly what needs testing. `SGVUE_OPEN_PATHS` supplies the chooser's answer in a development build only; the paths still go through `admit`, so it grants nothing the dialog would not. Absent from any packaged build. |
| **2026-09-18 — `finishUpload` no longer waits for rejected rows** | `SGVue.dc.html:1186` waits for every row that is not `done`, and an **error** row never becomes `done` — so dropping one bad file alongside a good one left the good one parsed and unloaded until the rejection was dismissed by hand. Error rows are excluded. A prototype defect, in the class of plan §3.5. |
| **2026-09-18 — `hlColor` is a real store field now** | The design declares it (`:852`), saves it (`:1682`) and restores it (`:1701`), but nothing ever writes it — it is always `#35C4B6`. Rather than drop a key the session and the share link carry, `setStepColor` writes it alongside the `viewer.setHighlightColor(c)` the design already makes there (`:2002`), so the recorded value and the viewer agree. |
| **2026-09-18 — The SQL property index is built once per batch, inside `addBatch`, and is not fatal** | Phase 1b built it behind the temporary `openFile`, which Phase 8 deletes. It is built from the whole federation, so N models joining together cost one build. A worker that will not start logs and is skipped: the index is what the assistant queries (Phase 9), and a model renders, selects and reviews perfectly well without it — it must not turn a loaded model into a landing-page error. |
| **2026-09-18 — `src/preload/index.d.ts` became `api.d.ts`** (the plan's own layout, §3.8) | TypeScript treats a `foo.d.ts` beside a `foo.ts` as that file's *declaration output* and drops it from the program, so the `declare global { interface Window { sgvue } }` was silently absent from every project that compiles `src/preload/index.ts` — which is the Node project the tests run in. |
| **2026-09-18 — The parity backdrop is frozen with `animation: none`, not `animation-play-state: paused`** | Pausing freezes each side wherever its own 26 s survey-grid drift happened to be when the capture started, which is a different phase on every run. Removing the animation puts both at phase 0, which is what comparing pixels actually requires. The pointer is placed at one fixed point for the same reason. |
| **2026-09-18 — A grid is a *segment*, not the design's `{ axis, v }`** | `IfcGridAxis` is a curve, and the 137.9 MB reference model's grid has 39 axes in two families at **46.59°** and **136.59°** with **none** axis-aligned. So `ModelMeta.grids` carries two plan endpoints, the drawn line is the grid's own infinite line clipped to the padded footprint (which *is* the design's literal endpoints for an axis-aligned grid), the family is a direction group within 10°, the near-side bubble is the end further along the camera's plan direction, and a section along a grid is a plane through the segment. Each generalisation reduces to the design's branch exactly, and `tests/unit/annotate.test.ts` asserts that against the design's literal numbers. |
| **2026-09-18 — The section offset's direction is a *stated convention*, because the design has none that generalises** | `viewer-core.js` L240–241 moves the plane along **+x** for an `axis: 'x'` grid and **+y** for an `axis: 'y'` one — two perpendiculars of *opposite* handedness, so no single rotation of the line direction reproduces both. `planNormal` therefore takes, of a segment's two plan normals, the one pointing into the positive half-plane: `(1, 0)` and `(0, 1)` for those two cases, and well defined at 43°. |
| **2026-09-18 — `pad` and the dimension stand-off are *not* scaled by `radius / 27.5`** | Both are already footprint-relative — `max(2.5 m, 6 %)` and `max(0.12 m, 7 %)` — and their floors are absolute clearances between annotation and geometry rather than sizes taken from the design's own mock. Scaling the 2.5 m floor would make it 38.8 m on the 425 m model and it would start to dominate the 6 % term, which is a different rule. The bubble gap (1.1 m), the stem drop (0.7 m), the laser's reach (1 000 m) and the section margin (1.5 m) *are* scaled, as every constant in `scene.ts` is. |
| **2026-09-18 — The level rings use `pad`, not `BUILD_PLAN` §5's `max(6 m, 12 %)`** | `viewer-core.js` L215 uses the same `pad` the gridlines use, and the prototype is what parity is measured against: 6 m / 12 % would put every ring and tag visibly elsewhere. BUILD_PLAN's figure describes an earlier iteration of the design's own build. |
| **2026-09-18 — In an elevation the family that is *kept* is the one whose lines run along the view direction** | `viewer-core.js` L204 is `keepAxis = alongX ? 'y' : 'x'`, and `axis: 'y'` is the family whose lines run along **x** — so looking from the east it keeps the grids running east–west. That is standard drafting: an east elevation carries bubbles 1…4, not A…E, because those grids sit at different distances *across* the screen while A…E all project to one screen position and would overprint. Their own lines are end-on, which is what the lifted bubble and its vertical stem are for. |
| **2026-09-18 — The laser's self-hit window is per *element*, not per mesh** | The reference rejects `x.object === self && x.distance < 0.05`. A merged slot mesh here holds thousands of elements (`batches.ts`), so object identity would reject almost everything; the element the ray started from is what the 5 cm window is actually about. `picker.ray(origin, dir, far, near)` implements it as a raised near bound and a second cast, which is the same answer for a lower-bound rejection. |
| **2026-09-18 — The two CRS chips are read from the file** | The design writes `SVY21` (status bar) and `SVY21 · EPSG:3414` (Coordinate-system card) as literals — true of its own Singapore subject, a placeholder for any other file, and the same class of value as Phase 3's manufactured GlobalIds. `shared/georef.ts`'s `crsChip` keeps **both of the design's chips verbatim** for an SVY21 / EPSG:3414 file (the reference model's is), names the file's own CRS when it is something else, keeps the design's status chip once the user has typed a base point, and shows the design's em dash when there is neither. It is the one Phase 6 difference a reviewer may want to overturn for the *card* specifically. *(Amended 2026-10-08: the card is read-only, so there is no typed base point and no fallback for one; both chips are the CRS the **boot** file declares — the store's `bootGeoref` — or the em dash.)* |
| **2026-09-18 — Spot coordinates report the file's own coordinates, not the scene's** | The geometry pipeline subtracts whole metres so float32 resolves millimetres 33 km out; the label's `xyz` row and the E/N/Z rows add them back, which is the same correction the property card's Base / top and Centroid rows already make. `toMap` is one implementation shared by both readouts. |
| **2026-09-18 — The viewer keeps grids and storeys *per model* and re-unions them** | They used to be appended and never removed, so unloading a model left its gridlines on screen — invisible until Phase 6 actually drew them. The union is by name, as `shared/federate.ts` does it for the shell, so a grid named `C` that every discipline repeats survives one model being unloaded. |
| **2026-09-18 — The property card's Base / top and Centroid add the federation offset back** | `viewer.elementBox()` is in *scene* coordinates, with the whole-metre offset already subtracted so float32 resolves millimetres 33 km from the file origin. Every other row of the Geometry section is a difference and is unaffected, but those two are absolute: without the offset the base of a wall reads −4.9 m where the storey ladder says +0. The offset is now a store field, set once by the boot batch. |
| **2026-09-18 — The Centroid row shows the em dash unless the file is georeferenced** | The prototype's `coords` is a typed-in base point (`SGVue.dc.html:849`), so its Centroid is a placeholder for a mock that has no CRS at all. `IfcMapConversion.XAxisAbscissa`/`XAxisOrdinate` are optional, so a file that states a base point but no axis direction is read as no rotation — a stated schema default, not an invented value; a file that states no base point gets the design's own dash. |
| **2026-09-18 — `ctxItems` and the card's `sel` are pure selectors carrying an `action`, not a closure** | The design binds `run: () => this.hide(tgtIds)` into each item. Separating the data from the handler is what makes twelve labels, three separators, the `(n)` suffix, the "3 types" collapse and the "acts on the whole selection" rule testable without a DOM — the same split `selectors/tree.ts` already uses for a tree row. |
| **2026-09-18 — The context menu closes exactly where the design closes it, and nowhere else** | Every item's own action clears `ctx` (`hide`/`isolate`/`showAll` through `up`, `select` through `select`, `askAbout` directly), a pointer-down on the viewport clears it (`viewer/input.ts` → `on.context(null)`), and Escape clears it first in the `keys.ts` cascade. The design has **no** document-level pointer-down listener, so a click on the sidebar leaves an open menu open; adding one would be a behaviour change, not a port. |
| **2026-09-18 — The parity harness separates two clicks in one state by 300 ms** | The prototype's `psOpen.<k>.toggle` closes over the `propOpen` it was rendered with (`:1978–1981`), so two toggles in one tick both write from the same stale copy and the first is lost. The port reads the live store and is correct either way. Reproducing the race would be reproducing a defect, and no person can reach it at 300 ms apart. |
| **2026-09-18 — `CompositionType` is read for `IfcSite` / `IfcBuilding` / `IfcBuildingStorey`** | Phase 3 left the Spatial-structure card's two rows on a dash because `ModelIndex` did not carry the attribute. It is now read with the same `text()` reader `PredefinedType` uses, checked against IfcOpenShell in the fixture test, and still shows the dash when the file leaves the optional attribute unset. |
| **2026-09-18 — Inline styles are kept as the design's own declaration *strings*, parsed at render time** (`src/renderer/app/css.ts`) | The fidelity contract says the markup is translated element by element keeping the inline styles verbatim. Hand-converting ~400 declarations to camelCase is where a port silently loses a `padding-left`; with `s('display:flex;gap:9px')` every string in `app/` is byte-identical to `SGVue.dc.html` and diffs against it directly. Values stay strings, so nothing guesses a unit, and `--rlane` passes through as React wants it. |
| **2026-09-18 — `style-hover` / `style-focus` become one CSS class per distinct declaration set, with `!important`** (`styles/hover.css`) | The design's template runtime rewrites the inline style on hover; React has no such thing. A class rule loses to an inline `style` attribute, so the hover state has to out-rank it — which is exactly what the design's runtime does by other means. Each rule quotes the design line it came from, and the whole file is ten rules for every hover site in the port so far. |
| **2026-09-18 — A model with no sample-library entry takes its row name from the file name and its swatch from `MC[slot]`** | The design's rows come from a fixed `SAMPLE_FILES` table, so every model already has a label and a colour; a real IFC file declares neither, and the row still needs both (the swatch *is* the button that opens the override palette). The file-name stem is the same rule the design applies to an upload (`up.replace(/\.[^.]+$/, '')`). It is deliberately **not** the `IfcProject` name: on a real export that is the project number, it says nothing about which file this is, and it is already the header two rows above. |
| **2026-09-18 — A value the file does not carry shows the design's own em dash, never the prototype's constant** | `spatial()` manufactures a GlobalId from a hash (`SGVue.dc.html:1740`) and writes `MILLI.METRE · SQUARE_METRE · CUBIC_METRE` and `CompositionType: ELEMENT` as literals. The contract's "never a placeholder" forbids all three here, so the units line is composed from the file's own `IfcUnitAssignment`, the GlobalId is read from the index, and `coords` starts `null` and is filled from `IfcMapConversion` when the file georeferences. On the mock — which has no GlobalIds and no georeferencing — this is the whole of the `project-card` parity difference, written up in `tests/parity/phase3/README.md`. |
| **2026-09-18 — Element-tree groups of 200 rows or more are windowed; everything smaller renders the design's own DOM** | Expanding `IfcMember` (6 805 rows) on the 137.9 MB model cost **501 ms** from click to painted frame, and the cost is linear in the row count. The windowed path keeps a spacer at each end sized by a *measured* row height, so the scrollbar, the scroll positions and every visible pixel are unchanged; the threshold sits above the mock federation's largest group (124), so all 24 parity captures take the unwindowed path and are byte-identical before and after the change. `rowWindow()` in `state/selectors/tree.ts` is the arithmetic, unit-tested; `#rows=<n>` lowers the threshold in a dev build so the windowed path can be exercised on the mock. |
| **2026-09-18 — The temporary-state frame ships with Phase 3, not with the filter stack** | `SGVue.dc.html:304` is the designed answer to "something is hidden", and Phase 3 is what ships the controls that hide things — the storey, model, group and element eyes. Leaving it to Phase 5 would mean a phase whose own features have no designed feedback. Its filter-driven variant (accent, "N filter steps — M hidden") is written and stays dormant until `stack` fills. |
| **2026-09-18 — `viewer.addModel` never frames the camera; the caller frames the boot batch** (`frameExtents()`, `FederationController.addBatch`) | The design builds the whole federation and *then* creates the viewer (`SGVue.dc.html:1096`), so the fit happens once with every model present, and `setModels` (`:917`) reads the camera, rebuilds and puts it back. Streaming the models in one at a time silently loses that distinction: `addModel` framed on `store.elements.size === 0`, so a four-discipline boot ended up framed on whichever arrived first — `dist 63.73` against the prototype's `70.31`, and visible as a building filling the stage. The viewer cannot know what a boot batch is; the shell can. A batch that takes the federation from empty frames at the end, every other batch and every removal preserves the camera, and `tests/unit/federation-store.test.ts` is that rule. |
| **2026-09-18 — `three-mesh-bvh` is removed from `package.json`** | Phase 2b's merged-geometry picking made it unused: a `MeshBVH` over a *merged* slot cannot be restricted to one part's index range at query time, so it would test the whole slot and reject by lookup, and building one per ~300 k-triangle slot is a multi-hundred-millisecond main-thread stall. The element-box filter measures 3.3 ms on the 137.9 MB model. A dependency with no importer is a dependency that gets re-imported by accident. |
| **2026-09-18 — colour-by-property gets no manual control, only a store action** | The design has **no** UI entry point for it: `colorBy` is written by the assistant's `color_by_property` tool (`SGVue.dc.html:1547`) and by nothing else, and the legend's own × is the only way a person clears one. Adding a picker would be a visible addition, which the fidelity contract forbids. So the store exposes `setColorBy(prop, rules?)` / `clearColorBy()` for Phase 9 to call, and the parity harness drives each side through *its own* entry point — `window.__sgvueDev.colorBy` on ours, the prototype's own `colorBy` method on its (reached on the logic instance its template runtime keeps as `logic`). Every other control in the Phase 7 capture is a designed one clicked by its own copy. |
| **2026-09-18 — the viewer's colour precedence is a pure function, `elementColour` in `materials.ts`** | `BUILD_PLAN.md` §1.4 is five layers deep and the reference implements it as two closures inside `createViewer` (`baseFor` L91–96, `applyMat` L487–498), which cannot be reached from a test without a GPU. Lifting the *decision* out — element state in, `{ color, alpha, lift }` out — leaves the renderer writing what it is told and makes every layer pair a unit test, including "an override keeps the source opacity so glass stays glass" (`alpha: null`) and the reference's own branch order. `partAlpha` is the other half: the hover lift is a tag added to whatever alpha the decision settled on. |
| **2026-09-18 — `colorBy` is not a `VIS_KEY`, and is cleared on every federation change** | It changes no element's visibility, so ⌘Z must not walk it back — the design's `VIS_KEYS` (`:1013`) leaves it out and so does ours. But its groups hold **ids**, so `setModels` clears it (`:923`) rather than remapping them; `commitModels` does the same, next to the `hidden` and `modelColors` pruning it already did. A scheme whose property nothing carries returns `null` **without touching state** (`:1350`), which is what lets `color_by_property` answer "nothing carries X" without destroying the legend already on screen. |
| **2026-09-18 — the chat panel gets no stop control, no disabled state and no abort on close** | The design has none of the three. `chatSend` (`:1592`) guards re-entry with `if (!q \|\| this.state.chatBusy \|\| !this.model) return` and that is the whole of it: there is no companion stop button in the busy row, no `disabled` attribute anywhere in the composer, and `toggleChat` (`:2011`) writes `chatOpen` and nothing else. Each would be a visible addition, which the fidelity contract forbids. `abortChat` exists in `ai/bridge.ts` for `scripts/ai-acceptance.cjs` and for the gateway's own 120 s cap; no designed surface reaches it. *(Amended 2026-10-01: the owner's "Ask Vee" handoff has a stop control, and it is the specification for the assistant's panel since that day — the send button stops a running turn. The other two stand: nothing is `disabled`, and closing the panel stops nothing. The row "the chat panel has a stop control, and `You` stands on the left", below.)* |
| **2026-09-18 — `chatW` / `chatH` are transient** | They are declared at `:850` and written by `onChatResize` (`:1127`), and the design's `sessionPayload` (`:1677`) does not carry them — a resized panel is not part of the view a session or a share link restores. They are store fields rather than component state only because the resize handler reads them from outside React's tree. |
| **2026-09-18 — the resize clamp reads `selIds.length`, the panel's `right` reads the derived card** | `:1130` and `:2049` are the design's own two expressions for the same lane, and they disagree for a selected id the federation no longer carries: the panel would sit at `right: 12` while the drag clamped its width as though the lane were 344 wide. Both are ported as written, at the same two sites. Reconciling them would be redesigning, and the state that separates them also leaves the property card undrawn. |
| **2026-09-18 — the context menu clamps its top to its own measured height** | `SGVue.dc.html:1901` clamps to `innerHeight - 260` whatever the menu holds, and the element menu is **385 px** tall: a right-click low in the window left its last three items past the bottom edge, unreachable — and the same constant pushed the two-item menu empty space gets **180 px above the pointer** for no reason. `app/ContextMenu.tsx` measures `offsetHeight` in a layout effect (before paint, so nothing moves on screen) and `clampCtxY` lifts the menu by exactly what it overflows, keeping an 8 px edge gap. `clampCtx`/`CTX_BOTTOM_MARGIN` are gone; `clampCtxX` keeps the design's 220 px right margin unchanged. A §3.5-class prototype defect. |
| **2026-09-18 — the shadow map is rendered on change, not every frame** | `sun.shadow.autoUpdate = false` (`viewer/scene.ts`). The sun is aimed at the model's centre and stands off it by a fixed offset, so **the shadow camera cannot see anything different when the view camera orbits** — only the scene can change it. Five call sites mark it dirty: a model added or removed, the shadow toggle, the section plane, the theme, and any frame in which `partState.flush()` actually uploaded (a part's alpha is what the family materials discard on in the shadow pass too, so that covers hide, ghost, fade, selection and colour). Measured on the 137.9 MB model: **126 → 105 draw calls** a frame, and a section along a gridline **103.3 → 10.5 ms** from click to painted frame, because that path changes no part state and so skips the pass entirely. Colouring by a property is unchanged (97.2 → 96.5 ms) for the same reason — it *does* write part state. Design parity: the stage measures **2.77 / 5.68 %** against the prototype before and after, identically. |
| **2026-09-18 — accessibility is attributes, focus and keys only; not one designed pixel moves** | `role="tree"`/`treeitem`/`group` with `aria-expanded`, `aria-selected`, `aria-level`, `aria-posinset`/`aria-setsize` (the windowed rows need the last two), a roving `tabindex`, `role="menu"` with a focus trap, `role="dialog"` on Preferences and on the design's own unload confirmation, a `role="log"` transcript, and an `aria-label` + visually-hidden description for the canvas. The 44 px touch minimum `BUILD_PLAN` asks for lives in `@media (pointer: coarse)` in `styles/a11y.css`, keyed on the design's own `data-role="toolbar"` and `title="Show / hide …"`, so a mouse never matches it and no designed markup gains a class. The focus ring is the design's own `:focus-visible` rule (`:44`) — none was invented. Re-captured parity: chrome **0.02 / 0.03 %**, the chat panel **0.00–0.01**, exactly the figures Phases 3 and 9 recorded. |
| **2026-09-18 — the element tree's keyboard is a pure reducer** | `state/selectors/tree-keys.ts` turns a key, the flattened node list and the focused key into `focus` / `expand` / `collapse` / `activate`, and `app/Sidebar.tsx` only dispatches it and moves the DOM focus. The design has **no** keyboard tree at all, so every rule here is ours and has to be checkable without a window — including the one property windowing threatens: a node exists in the list even when its row is outside the rendered slice, so a walk crosses `IfcMember`'s 6 805 rows exactly as a scroll does. Thirteen unit tests, plus an e2e walk. |
| **2026-09-18 — the app icons are drawn from the design's own favicon, by a Python dev script** | `build/icon.icns` / `.ico` / `.png` were electron-vite's **scaffold icon — the Electron atom** — until Phase 10. `scripts/make-icons.py` transcribes the eleven numbers and three colours of `SGVue.dc.html:30`'s `<link rel="icon">` SVG, draws them with Pillow 16× oversampled (an SVG rasteriser is a system dependency this repository will not add for three strokes), insets the artwork to 824/1024 for macOS as every icon has since Big Sur, and runs `iconutil`. Five of the seven `.ico` sizes are byte-identical inside `SGVue.exe`, which is how we know the Windows resource edit worked. |
| **2026-09-18 — the macOS build sets `identity: null` explicitly** | With no `identity`, electron-builder signs with **whatever certificate it finds in the keychain** — on this Mac a personal *Apple Development* one, which is neither the unsigned build the plan asks for nor anything another machine will accept, and it spent minutes signing every `locale.pak`. `null` is the documented "do not sign". Windows needed no such care: `dist:win` edited the executable's icon and version resources on macOS **without wine**, so `signAndEditExecutable` stays at its default. |
| **2026-09-18 — the packaged app is guarded too, and enters through a share link** | `scripts/safe-app.cjs` is `safe-e2e.cjs`'s guard pointed at `/SGVue.app/Contents/` as well as `node_modules/electron/dist/`, because a packaged `SGVue Helper (GPU)` is invisible to every other guard here; `safe-e2e.cjs` matches both lists now, since `tests/e2e/packaged.spec.ts` sits in the directory it globs. The packaged test opens `tiny.ifc` through a **`sgvue://s=…` share link** rather than a file association: `SGVUE_OPEN_PATHS` is gated on `!app.isPackaged`, the link is a designed affordance the app already registers, and it needs no new main-process code. |
| **2026-09-18 — the renderer is minified** | esbuild, `electron.vite.config.ts`. 3.79 → 1.50 MB for the bundle and 5.98 → 2.59 MB for the parse worker. Nothing here depends on a name surviving — every test and every harness finds elements by `data-role`, `title`, `data-tip` or designed copy — and `window.__sgvueDev` is dropped by the `DEVTOOLS` constant before minification sees it, which `tests/e2e/packaged.spec.ts` asserts against the packaged bundle. |
| **2026-09-18 — there is no `src/main/exports.ts`, because no designed control produces a file** | The plan reserves a third writer for "a snapshot PNG, a CSV export". Walking the design: the toolbar has no export or snapshot control, and the panel's `copy csv` (`:470`, `:2029`) writes to the **clipboard**. So the module is not created, and `settings.ts` and `sessions.ts` remain the only two writers. `viewer.snapshot()` exists in the renderer's API and has no caller — worth deleting, but deleting it is not this phase's business. |
| **2026-09-18 — WebGPU stays opt-in by name, and the mock cannot settle it** | Measured under the guard on the only content the backend policy allows (`scripts/lib/backend.cjs`): the 590-part mock federation runs **120 fps on both backends**, 22 draw calls, GPU `phys_footprint` peaking at 1 007 MB on WebGPU against 1 037 MB on WebGL2. At that size the two are indistinguishable, so the measurement answers nothing about the case that panicked this Mac — 67 364 parts — and a real model on WebGPU is still not something a script may start. `auto` remains WebGL2, in `scene.ts` and in the Preferences "Renderer" setting. |---
| **2026-09-18 — the shipped GPU guard's limit is `max(3 072 MB, 40 % of os.totalmem())`, decided once at startup** | A fixed 3 072 MB would have crashed the viewer on a model the app is *meant* to open: the packaged app peaked at **2 242–2 353 MB** of GPU `phys_footprint` on the 137.9 MB reference model, against a stated design target of 50–200 MB files. A legitimate peak scales with the file, and the files a machine is asked to open scale with the machine — 40 % of RAM is 9 830 MB on this 24 GB Mac, and an 8 GB machine stays at 3 277 MB, barely off the floor, where a 3 GB GPU footprint genuinely is trouble. Raising the ceiling costs nothing against the case the guard exists for: a runaway does not creep up on it, it climbs by **gigabytes per second** (634 → 5 461 MB inside one 0.5 s poll, measured), so it is caught within a poll or two at any of these limits. `gpuLimitMB()` is the whole arithmetic, pure and unit-tested; the poll, the memory-pressure trip and the warn-then-crash behaviour are unchanged, and `scripts/lib/electron-guard.cjs` keeps its tighter fixed defaults — a bounded dev run may fail loudly, a user's session may not. |
| **2026-09-19 — the label occlusion sweep runs when its answer can have changed, not every fourth frame** | `viewer-core.js` L711 tests every occluding label on one frame in four whatever is happening, which is free on a 590-part mock and ruinous on a real model: **78 grid bubbles × one ray through 26 539 elements = 83.8 ms a sweep**, measured with the camera standing still, and it is the whole of the 24–27 fps default state (p95 125–159 ms). The answer can only change when the camera pose, the occluders or the labels change, so `viewer-core.ts` keeps `occDirty` and the design's every-fourth-frame cadence is kept for the one case it was written for — a camera in motion. At rest one sweep settles it. `overlay.updateOcclusion` takes `moving` and returns whether it swept; nothing else about it moved. |
| **2026-09-19 — a pick ray walks a uniform grid, and the grid is a *filter*, not an answer** | `pick-grid.ts` bins every element's world box into a uniform grid (≈ one cell per element, 64 per axis at most) walked with a 3D DDA, so a ray sees only the candidates along it. Every candidate then goes through the same `boxInterval`, the same sort and the same triangle walk as before, and a box is inserted into every cell it touches *plus a hair*, so the surviving set is **identical** to the flat scan's — with one addition, a secondary sort key on creation order, because the grid yields candidates in cell order and a tie in entry distance must still break the way it did. Measured: **0.099 ms against 1.098 ms a ray** on the 137.9 MB model, sweep 83.8 → 0.1 ms, hover js 4.3 → 1.1 ms. Proved rather than asserted: `tests/unit/pick-grid.test.ts` compares against `bruteForce: true` (the old scan, kept as the reference) over ~7 000 seeded rays on the design's federation including section-clipped and hidden cases, and `scripts/pick-parity.cjs` does it on the real model — the 78 real bubble rays at three poses and 2 000 random ones, same id and same distance to the last bit. The grid follows model add and remove (rebuilt only when the element *set* changes, which identity settles) and per-element visibility is a `live` byte, so a hide still costs one pass. |
| **2026-09-19 — the frame loop draws on demand, and the rule is one sentence: *anything that changes what is drawn must call `invalidate()`*** | Redrawing 14.6 million vertices and 2.7 million edge segments unchanged costs ~8 ms of GPU a frame for a picture nobody asked for. The *loop* is untouched — it runs at the display's rate, the clock ticks, the status bar's fps counts frame callbacks, `on.camera` fires — and only `renderer.render`, the grid view, the occlusion sweep, the labels and the cube are skipped. Two frames are drawn after the last invalidation, not one, so an upload landing between the frame and the compositor cannot leave a stale image. The camera answers for itself: `cam.tick` returns whether what it placed differs from what it placed last frame (covering easing, flights, drags, the wheel and a projection swap) and snaps onto its goal within a microradian, because `1 − exp(−dt·rate)` never arrives. The way this fails is silent, so it is tested by pixels: `scripts/frame-triggers.cjs` drives twenty-two classes of change with the camera at rest and compares what the **compositor** holds before and after (a capture is not a re-render), then checks that a still scene draws nothing for two seconds while the status bar still reads 120 fps. |
| **2026-09-19 — `forceSinglePass` on the glass material was measured and is NOT taken** | The see-through material has carried the flag since Phase 2a and the glass one does not, which looked like an oversight: a `DoubleSide` **transparent** material is rendered twice (three.js traps, below), so on the reference model the glass family costs **14 draw calls and 365 019 triangles a frame** through the vertex stage that the flag would remove — 105 · 5 573 656 becomes 91 · 5 208 637, worth **0.14 ms of an 8.4 ms frame**. It was set, measured and reverted, because the back-then-front order those two passes produce is part of the drawn result. Measured in a band of **pure façade** — no DOM bubbles in it, at a 4 s settle where the camera has genuinely arrived — the flag changes **about 300 pixels of the glazing by at most 4 of 255**: 298 px in `iso` light, 302 in `iso` dark, 396 and 374 in `highlight` where everything is ghosted and the ceiling is 1 level, identically on every run in both themes. Small, systematic, and the wrong way round under the fidelity contract for 0.14 ms. **A first measurement of this put it at 0.19 % of the frame and up to 90 levels; that was a misattribution** — those pixels are the grid bubbles' *glyph antialiasing*, which moves between two runs of the same build, and a whole-frame mean cannot see past them. The shipped state is two-pass, exactly as at Phase 2a; `profile-frame.cjs` condition `5b` turns the flag on for four seconds if the number is wanted again, and `tests/parity/phase2a/README.md` records both. |
| **2026-09-19 — hover is not evaluated while a camera gesture is running** | The design's own rule is `down.on` (`viewer-core.js` L566): during a drag a pointer move records `lastEvt` and does **not** set `needHover`. The port had that already; what it did not have is the two gestures that reach the same camera calls by another route — a pinch, whose second finger leaves `down.on` false, and the wheel, which a pointer move can interleave with. Both now call `host.hoverCancel()`, which drops a hover queued earlier in the same frame, and a pinch move returns before asking for one. Hovering resumes at the first pointer move that is none of the three, which is that move's own hover. A ray cost 8.6 ms of a frame a drag is trying to finish in 16; it costs 0.1 ms now either way, but spending it during a gesture is still spending it for nothing. |
| **2026-09-19 — the edges were measured and left alone** | 2 691 699 blended segments looked like the next 2.6–3.2 ms. With the occlusion sweep gated they are **0.11 ms of an 8.44 ms frame** (8.44 → 8.33 mean with the edge group hidden), and the experiment that would have justified doing something — `transparent: false` on the edge material, which could never ship because the design's edge is 0.45 alpha over the lit surface — made the frame *slower* (8.83 ms mean, p95 15.2 against 10.3). So the cost is line rasterisation, not blending, and there is nothing to win that is worth a pixel. Condition `3b` stays in `scripts/profile-frame.cjs` as the record of the measurement. |
| **2026-09-19 — a guarded run may kill only what it started** | `safe-app.cjs` and `safe-e2e.cjs` matched every process under `/SGVue.app/Contents/` and killed them all afterwards — including **the user's own SGVue**, which is exactly what was open (`dist/mac/SGVue.app`, four processes) when this work began. Both now snapshot the matching pids *and their `.app` bundles* before the run and never signal or measure either. The bundle matters as much as the pid: an open app spawns and replaces helper processes while the user works — measured mid-run on 2026-09-19 — and a pid snapshot alone would have killed that helper. The dev-Electron rule is unchanged: a dev Electron of this checkout still refuses the run outright. A pre-existing packaged app does not; it is simply left alone, and the run says so. |
| **2026-09-20 — the scene is the model's PROJECT frame, not the file's world frame** | The reference file is a Revit shared-coordinates export on the CORENET X convention: its `IfcMapConversion` is an **identity** and its real position *and rotation* sit on the spatial-root `IfcSite.ObjectPlacement` — location `(12 345 457, 23 456 766, 5 050) mm`, `RefDirection` turned **−43.4103°** about Z. So the file's world axes are SVY21 map axes and the building stands at 43° to them. Rendering world coordinates left everything the app draws axis-aligned wrong by that angle: level rings, grid-bubble clipping, laser axes, dimension lines, the N/S/E/W views, and the property card's Length and Width — measured, the sample wall read **87.489 × 82.787 m** where the file's own `Qto_WallBaseQuantities` says **120.150 × 0.300 m**. `geometry-streamer.ts` now composes `frame⁻¹` on the left beside the Y-up → Z-up rotation, and it is still the only place the frames meet. The frame is a federation constant exactly like the offset — the boot batch's first model chooses it, every later model is streamed through the same one, so models still federate by world coordinates. Two dividends: the offset falls from `[12520, 23186, 4]` to `[313, −76, −1]` and the **`precision` warning disappears** (the vertices are 400 m from the project origin, not 33 km, so float32 resolves ~0.05 mm instead of 4 mm). The mock federation states no site placement, so its frame is the identity and its code path is byte for byte the old one. *(Amended 2026-10-08: "so models still federate by world coordinates" no longer holds — the frame is the boot model's project frame expressed in map coordinates, P = M_boot ∘ Site_boot, and each model streams through its own M_i⁻¹ ∘ P, so the federation lines up in map space; for a boot model whose map operation is the identity, P is exactly this site frame. The 2026-10-08 rows below.)* |
| **2026-09-20 — `coordsFromGeoref` composes `IfcMapConversion` OVER the site placement, and the method is detected** | The old reading took a present-but-zero map conversion first, so the card was handed **0 / 0 / 0 / 0** for a file whose position was sitting on its site — and it assumed a site-placement file states no rotation, which this one does. The base point is now the map coordinates of the **project frame's origin** and the total rotation from project north to true north: `E 12345.457 · N 23456.766 · Z 5.05 · −43.4103`. `detectMethod` says which declaration is actually *in force* (`source` / `sources` still say what was *found*), by which transform is non-identity — a map conversion is identity when E, N and H are zero or absent and XAxis is `(1, 0)` or absent; a site placement is identity when it neither translates nor rotates. `TrueNorth` is read from the `Model` context and used **only** when nothing else states a rotation; a map conversion that disagrees with it wins, because it is what the E / N readouts are computed with. `Scale` is still not applied — it is map-units per file-length-unit (0.001 on a millimetre file) and everything here is already metres, so the factor is 1. *(Amended 2026-10-08: the base point is now `federationFrame`, P, read off the same `mapPlacement` the geometry uses — so E, N and H are multiplied by the map unit, and `Scale`, whose direction the exporters disagree on, stays unapplied; and a federation's base point is its **boot** model's, set once.)* Coordinates are kept to a millimetre and the angle to 1e-4°, which is 0.2 mm over 100 m; the card renders the stored number as the design does (`value="{{ coords.E }}"`), so it reads `12345.457` / `23456.766` / `5.05` / `-43.4103`. |
| **2026-09-20 — the spatial-root `IfcSite`, never the first by expressId** | The reference file carries **16** `IfcSite` entities — road-marking families exported as sites — and only the one `IfcProject` aggregates is the spatial root whose placement everything chains up to. `findNode(spatial, 'IfcSite')` already found it for the tree; the georeferencing reader, `corenetReadout` and `scripts/expected-from-ifcopenshell.py` now use the same one. On this file the root happens to be `#91`, which is also the lowest id — so the old code was right by luck, and the next file would not be. |
| **2026-09-20 — a level ring's height comes from the storey's own `ObjectPlacement`; the ladder keeps showing `Elevation`** | `IfcBuildingStorey.Elevation` is stated relative to whatever the storey is placed in, so on this export it is short by the site's own **5.05 m** — every ring and every level section sat that far below its own slabs. `Storey.placement` records the storey's world position and `metaOf` puts it through the project frame; the sidebar ladder and the 3D tag still display the authored `Elevation`, which is the number the model's author wrote. Measured on the real model: 10 of the 12 storeys now have a slab top at **exactly** the ring, and the two roof levels are 400 mm below their nearest roof slab, which is the model, not the frame. |
| **2026-09-20 — a session, a share link and a viewpoint record the frame their camera was taken in** | The camera is **scene** coordinates and the scene is the project frame minus the offset, so a payload written against another boot model — or by a build from before the frame existed — points it with numbers that meant something else. Each payload carries `frameKey(frame)` (`origin` to a millimetre, `rotationDeg` to 1e-4°, or `identity`). The rule is the simplest safe one: on a mismatch **everything else restores and the camera does not** — `applyRestore` calls `frameExtents()` instead, and `restoreView` does the same. An absent marker is a mismatch, so every session and link saved before today loses only its camera. *(Amended 2026-10-08: the key is P's, `frameKey(federationFrame(boot))` — the same string for a boot model whose map operation is the identity, a new one for a boot model placed by a map conversion, whose saved cameras then fall back as any other frame's do.)* |
| **2026-09-20 — the 3D level tag prints the storey's authored `Elevation`, not the height its ring is drawn at** | `viewer-core.js` L219 prints `s.elev` for both, because the design's mock federation is authored at the origin and the two are one number. On a real file they are not: the ring must sit at the scene height and the tag must read what the sidebar ladder two panels away reads, or they disagree by the federation offset — measured, the tag said `+1 000` for the storey the ladder calls `+0` (and `−4 000` before the project frame landed, so this is a Phase 6 defect the frame work only shrank). `ModelMeta.storeys` carries `authored` beside `elev`, the offset never reaches it, and `levelTagHtml(name, authoredElevation)` in `shared/annotate.ts` is the formatter — two arguments, so the two can no longer be confused. It is the only readout that formatted a scene `z`: `Base / top` is a project elevation, the spot label's `xyz` row is a project coordinate and its E / N / Z are map, and every other `signedMm` / `mmv` / `fmtMM` call site formats a difference. |
| **2026-09-20 — grid bubbles declutter greedily in axis order, per row, and it is a pure function** | 24 axes put 26 px bubbles ~20 px apart on the reference model. `declutterBubbles` (`shared/annotate.ts`) takes only the bubbles that would **otherwise be drawn** — an occluded or off-screen one is never passed, so it reserves no space — and keeps the first of each row, dropping any whose measured box comes within `BUBBLE_DECLUTTER_GAP` (2 px) of a kept one. A row is one family at one end, so the two ends of a grid are decided independently. Greedy **in axis order**, not in screen order, is what makes the survivors the same axes frame after frame: measured, 480 frames of a slow orbit on the real model changed **no** bubble's state, so the hysteresis the rule allows for is not built. It runs inside `overlay.update`, which is already the rendered-frame branch, so render-on-demand is untouched; the reads are batched because measuring between two style writes forces one layout per bubble (3.20 → 1.70 ms of main thread a frame, against a 0.90 ms baseline; the frame itself is unmoved at 8.30 ms median, 110.7 fps). Measured on the real model: 0 intersections among visible bubbles at every zoom, 32 → 38 → 60 drawn as the view dollies in, and by 2.2× the sweep hides no numbered bubble at all. |
| **2026-09-20 — the sidebar's vertical budget, and it engages only above the design's own storey count** | The design gives the element tree `flex:1;min-height:0` and makes everything above it rigid, so the tree is the only item that can give.
