| **2026-09-24 — the ground is two layers in the opaque list, and the veil is never a transparent object** | Asked for by the user in these words: *"The base canvas plane kind of hiding the model elements underneath it, when you orbit the camera to below the canvas plane only can see it."*; offered keeping it opaque, dropping it, or see-through, the user chose *keep the ground at level 0 but see-through, anything below dimmed, looks the same above ground*. **How:** `materials.ground` keeps its colour and shadows but writes no depth, and draws before every batch; after the batches `materials.groundVeil` — the same plane and geometry, the same lit and shadowed colour — blends over it at **0.6** and writes the depth the old ground wrote. Over plain ground that is ground over ground; over an opaque part below grade it is 0.6 ground + 0.4 part, so the part shows at **40 %** of its contrast. Because the veil leaves the old ground depth behind, every transparent pass after it meets exactly the depth it met before, so edges, see-through parts, glass, gridlines, level rings and the section sheet below grade stay hidden as they were, and nothing above grade can change. **Why not a transparent veil:** three 0.186's `_renderTransparents` draws the back-face pass of every double-sided transparent material (the glass family) *before* the whole transparent list, so a transparent veil at any `renderOrder` would blend over glass's back faces wherever only ground lay behind them. `CustomBlending` with `transparent: false` keeps the veil in the opaque list (`RenderList.push`) and keeps its alpha (`NodeBuilder.isOpaque()` is false for it); `blendSrcAlpha: One` keeps the canvas opaque. **Order** (`scene.ts`): grid helper −2, ground −1, batches 0, veil 10. The first attempt drew the helper at 0, after the ground: the veil then won the helper's far, grazing depth fight on samples the old ground had lost, and **62 628 plain-ground pixels of the mock changed, by up to 21 levels**. Drawing the helper and then the ground reproduces the old pair exactly, and the veil — the ground's own plane — wins and loses on the same samples. **Measured, guarded, before against after, same poses:** the mock framed with grids off, **945 469 of 946 000 pixels byte-identical** in both themes, the 531 others on its at-grade site slab (the helper, 5 mm below that slab, now meets it in a different order); on the structure model (942 of 4 374 elements below grade, to −13 m) the changed pixels are the below-grade columns and pile caps; from below, 0 and 19 pixels. **Cost:** a synchronous 30-render timing on the reference model at pixel ratio 1: 1.89–2.09 ms before, 1.89–2.11 after (noise); at pixel ratio 3 (9× the pixels): +0.12 ms framed, +0.22 ms in plan, +0.14–0.18 ms close. `profile-frame` is vsync-bound at 16.7 ms either way. **Not dimmed:** glass or ghosted parts below grade (hidden, as before). |
| **2026-09-24 — the camera frames the building box; the whole box keeps covering what is drawn** | Asked for by the user in these words: *"frame the camera on the building, not the site."* **The rule** (`shared/site.ts`, `isSiteLike`): an element is site when its class is `IfcSite`, `IfcGeographicElement`, `IfcCivilElement`, `IfcExternalSpatialElement` or `IfcSpace`, or it is an `IfcBuildingElementProxy` whose name or type matches `topograph`, `toposolid` or the word `site`. Read off the three real models: the 137.9 MB reference model's whole box is 454 × 429 m because of one `IfcGeographicElement` (`SITEBOUNDARY`, 454.7 × 429.4 m), while its walls span 330 × 154 m and every non-site class stays inside 342 × 173 m; the external-works model is roads and footpaths as `IfcCivilElement`, planting as `IfcGeographicElement` and landscape areas as `IfcSpace` (`AREA_LANDSCAPE`); the structure model has no site at all. Every `IfcSpace` is left out because a space never widens a building — its walls do — while a site-sized one (`ROADBUFFER`, `BUILDINGSETBACK`, `PLANTINGAREAS`, 329 × 153 m on the reference model) widens it to the site. The ids travel as `ModelMeta.site` (model-local `expressId`s, `federation-store.ts` `metaOf`); `viewer-core.ts` keeps them per model and re-unions **`frameBox`** on every add and removal, falling back to the whole box when nothing else is left. **Per consumer.** `frameBox`: boot (`frameExtents`), zoom extents, the six view presets and the rig's starting target (`createCameraRig({ bbox })`), the cube's click, a section's re-aim, a double-click on empty space, and — through `nearScale` — the near zoom limits (0.4 / 0.15 × scale) and `fitBox`'s floors, so a small element can be framed closer. `store.bbox` (the whole box): the scene scale and `restage`, the clip planes (0.1 / 3 000 × scale), the far zoom limits (900 / 400 × scale, so the whole site can still be reached), the ortho stand-off, the shadow frustum, the sun, the ground, the ground grid, `groundZ`, the section sheet and its margin, the occlusion sweep's reach, the laser's reach, the ground-plane pivot range and the gridlines' 1 cm lift — each of them either covers what is drawn or is tied to the ground. **Unchanged:** a session's, a share link's and a viewpoint's camera restore (`setCamera`), and `debug().bbox`, which still reports the whole box (`debug().frameBox` is new). **Measured** (guarded, 1440 × 860): the reference model boots at **1.86 px a metre, was 1.15**; the structure model is unchanged; **the design's mock is not** — its turf areas and street trees are `IfcGeographicElement`, so the boot frames the 25.6 × 19.6 m block instead of the 40 × 34 m site, 13.1 → 20.0 px a metre, **612 683 of 1 238 400 pixels (49 %) differ** at boot and 576 604 in plan. That follows from the owner's rule and was accepted; the prototype frames the whole mock. **The near plane:** the near clip stays on the whole box's scale (0.1 × scale), and the near zoom floors (0.4 / 0.15 × the building's scale, and `fitBox`'s) would pass inside it once the whole box's radius is more than four times the building's (1.6× on the reference model). So `createCameraRig` clamps the building's scale to at least a quarter of the whole box's (`max(nearScale, scale / 4)`): the nearest a dolly may come is then never less than the near plane, and on every model measured here the clamp does not bind. Unit-tested at scale 1 and at scale 60. |
| **2026-09-24 — the gridlines span the grids' own authored extent, else the building's footprint** | Asked for by the user in these words: *"I think the gridline bubbles are too far away from my uploaded real model."* Until now every grid's infinite line was clipped to the **whole** model's footprint plus `gridPad`, so the reference model's site put its bubbles **93–173 m** from the building's face and its grid's own authored extent (Revit writes it, `index-builder.ts` `axisSegment`) was thrown away. **Now** (`shared/annotate.ts`): `gridExtentRect` is the union of every grid segment's two ends, a segment shorter than `GRID_EXTENT_MIN` (2.5 m, the design's own clearance floor) ignored as a direction rather than an extent — an `IfcLine` of magnitude 1 is a metre long however big the building is; `padRect` grows it by the design's `gridPad` of its own size; with no usable extent it is the **building box's** footprint (`frameBox`), never the whole model's. Each line is still its grid's infinite line clipped to that rectangle, so a family's bubbles stay in one row. The bubble gap (`BUBBLE_GAP`), the stem drop and the elevation lift (`frameBox.max.z + pad`) are sized from the building box's scale; the level rings and their tags take the building box's padded footprint, so they always enclose the building even where a grid covers only part of it. Declutter, the grid dimensions (whose stand-off cap is the new pad) and the elevation rule are unchanged. **Occlusion changed with it:** bubbles close to the building land among the site's own trees, so **only building elements occlude an annotation** — the picker takes an `occludes` mask (`viewer-core.ts`: not a site element) that only `anyHit` reads; `pick` and `ray` still hit site elements, so hover, selection, the laser and snapping are unchanged, and `pick-parity.cjs` compares the two pickers with the same mask. **Measured** (guarded, 1440 × 860, before → after): reference model, bubbles **93.2–173.1 m → 31.4–36.5 m** from the building box (median 163.2 → 36.4), pad 27.3 → 21.3 m, bubbles shown at boot 18 → 20 and in plan 26 → 40, grid-dimension labels shown **6 → 9 of 37** at the default framing and **5 → 7** in plan, the north elevation 13 bubbles with stems; `pick-parity.cjs` identical (115 label rays at iso and top, 24 at north, 2 000 random). **The mock:** its bubbles move 10.9 m → 2.8 m off the block, under the crowns of its street trees; with the trees occluding, the plan showed 5 of 18 bubbles and 2 of 7 dimensions, and with only the building occluding it shows **18 of 18 and 7 of 7** (as before), the boot 11 of 18 and 4 of 7 (as before). |
| **2026-09-24 — a toolbar button toggles the canvas (ground) grid; the flag lives in the viewer** | Asked for by the user in these words: *"Add an option to toggle off canvas gridline."* The design has no control for it, so this is a new one, owner-approved: a button right after Levels in the same group, the neighbours' markup, `hv-step` class, 30 px box and on/off colours (`toolbarFlags`' `on()`), `data-tip="canvas grid"`, and `IconGroundGrid` — a framed 3 × 3 grid in the toolbar's own stroke, so it reads apart from the IFC gridlines' open `#`. It hides the fine `GridHelper` only; the IFC grids stay on `grids`. **Where the flag lives:** the helper is rebuilt on every theme change (`rig.setTheme`) and the whole rig on every rescale (`restage`), so the flag is `viewer-core`'s `groundGridOn`, handed to every `buildScene` and kept by the rig's own `gridOn` across `setTheme`; `setGroundGrid` calls `invalidate()`. The store's `groundGrid` (default on) is pushed on viewer boot beside `grids` and `levels`. **Not** saved in a session or a share link and **not** an assistant tool, to keep it minimal. *(Amended 2026-10-02: it **is** reachable by the assistant now — `toggle_display`'s `groundGrid`, through this button's own `setGroundGrid` — because the owner's direction of 2026-10-01, "assistant should possess everything user can do on the app", supersedes "to keep it minimal"; the 2026-10-02 parity rows below have it. It is still not saved in a session or a share link.)* **Tested:** `frame-triggers.cjs` 24/24 (off and on repaint the compositor); the e2e case toggles it on the fixture and compares the viewport's own pixels (about 44 000 change, and on-again is within a stray pixel of the first capture), then again after a theme switch. **A layout cost, fixed:** the toolbar is centred and one button (32 px) wider, and the view cube's 148 px canvas sits above it; at the default window (1 264 px inside on Windows) the theme button's right 22 of 30 px, centre included, fell inside the cube's canvas (were 6), over an empty corner that swallowed the click. So the cube canvas now takes a mouse's events only where something is drawn (`cube.ts`): a capture-phase `pointermove` on the window hit-tests the pointer against the 26 zones, the compass ring, the north kite and its `N`, and sets the canvas's `pointer-events` to `none` elsewhere inside its square. The browser picks an event's target before that listener runs, so the switch holds from the next event — a mouse always moves before it clicks — and until the first move the square behaves as before. **Touch is exempt** (a tap has no move before it, so a touch always restores the whole square and a tap on the cube always reaches it). Nothing drawn changes; the cube's hover, click and `title` work where the cube is. **Tested at 1 264 px:** the e2e case asserts the theme button's centre is inside the cube's canvas, moves the mouse there and clicks it for real (the icon swaps), then clicks the cube's centre, which un-lights the `3D` view button as a cube click does. Playwright's own `locator.click()` still reports the canvas as intercepting, because it asks what is under the point before it moves the mouse; the test moves first, as a hand does. |
| **2026-09-24 — "Original materials" off colours every element by its IFC class** | Asked for by the user in these words: *"When the material override toggle off, there are nothing different. The toggle off are suppose to be coloured in different ifcentity."* **Why it looked the same:** the design's `baseFor` falls back from a colour-by to the model's swatch to the file's colour, so with the toggle off and no swatch picked every element kept its file colour — the toggle changed nothing on screen. **Now:** `classColors(elements)` (`shared/colors.ts`) is `colorBy(elements, 'IfcEntity')` over the whole federation flattened to `id → hex`, pushed once per `commitModels` through a new `viewer.setClassColors`. `decide` resolves it against the toggle exactly as it resolves `modelColor` (absent while the toggle is on), and `baseColour` takes it last: **colour-by / highlight step (`elColor`) > the model swatch the user picked > the IFC class colour > the file's colour**. `alpha: null` as before, so glass and spaces stay see-through. Reusing `colorBy` rather than a new palette is the point: the toggle and the assistant's colour-by-entity give a wall the same colour. The hint's third string becomes `Coloured by IFC class`. **Rejected:** a separate class palette (two answers for one question); a legend (the owner asked for no new surface). **Tested:** `tests/unit/class-colours.test.ts` (precedence, `classColors` ≡ `colorBy('IfcEntity')`); the e2e toggles it on the demo and counts changed viewport pixels. |
| **2026-09-24 — a double-click renames a saved viewpoint** | Asked for by the user in these words: *"allow double click to edit saved view name."* **Build:** `ViewsCard.tsx` swaps the name `<span>` for an `<input data-role="view-rename">` whose style string is the span's own (`NAME`) plus `display:block;width:100%;height:1.3em;box-sizing:content-box;padding:0;border:0;outline:none;background:transparent` — the e2e compares the row's box before and during the edit and finds it identical, and the field's computed font is 13 px / 500 / 16.9 px. `autoFocus` + `select()` on focus; Enter blurs, blur commits; Esc sets a ref and blurs, so the blur that follows commits nothing. The global key handler already ignores keys in an `INPUT`, so Esc does not close the card. **Store:** `renameView(id, name)` over the pure `renameViews` — trimmed; blank, unchanged or an unknown id returns the same array, and the action then writes nothing — stored through `storeViews` where `saveView` / `dropView` store (localStorage, per building). **Click vs double-click:** the row's `onClick` returns when `ev.detail > 1`, so the double-click's first click restores the view (as a single click always has) and its second does nothing: one restore, one undo entry. **Repeated names:** the viewpoints list has no repeated-name rule (the filter sets have one, the viewpoints never did), so none is applied. |

The `#mock&hostile` prompt-injection fixture moved to `dev/hostile.ts` (`HOSTILE_OPTIONS`, passed through a new `MockOptions.element` / `buildingSuffix`), imported only by `App.tsx`'s dev-tools block — `grep` finds none of its text in the production `out/`. **Files:** none — never `addRecent`, never in `library`; `sessionFiles` drops path-less models, so a session or share link cut from the demo names no files, and opening one shows the designed "That session had no models in it" banner. |
| **2026-09-24 — `IfcSpace` is a third batch family, see-through; a pick takes a space last** | Asked for by the user in these words: *"the ifcSpace, make them transparent by default. They are space anyway."* **Before:** a space went to `solid` or `glass` by its file alpha; on the reference model (2 062 spaces) they were drawn like any element of their file colour and alpha — lilac volumes wrapping the whole building in the captures — an opaque one cast shadows, all drew grey edges, and a space won every click inside its room. **Family:** `batches.ts` splits `part.isSpace` (the worker already tags it) into `space` slots: `materials.space` — `MeshStandardNodeMaterial`, transparent, `depthWrite: false`, `DoubleSide` with `forceSinglePass` (one flat tint, so the back-then-front order that glass keeps changes nothing — the `ghost` reasoning), opacity `select(a > 1, SPACE_MARKED_ALPHA, a × SPACE_ALPHA)`, the usual `maskNode` hidden test and the same colour / glow nodes; `castShadow` and `receiveShadow` false; `renderOrder` 2.5 (after the see-through twins, before glass: a room is inside the envelope). A space part's alpha is stored as 1, so every state alpha scales `SPACE_ALPHA` (ghost 0.1 → 0.008, hidden 0 discards) and a colour-by keeps it translucent. **Opacity chosen from screenshots:** 0.15 a face stacked six faces deep in a storey section (three storeys of floor-area spaces, two faces each) and tinted the plan ~60 % lilac; **0.08** leaves ~40 % there and ~15 % for one room seen from outside. Selected / highlighted: 0.3 a face with the glow, plus the accent outline. **Edges:** `drawsEdge` excludes spaces (a room's outline is its walls', doubled), `drawsSelEdge` does not. **Pick:** `picking.ts` keeps a `solid` mask (`live` minus spaces) and casts `pick` and `ray` over it first, then over `live` only on a miss and only when a space is hittable — so a space is picked only when it is the one thing on the ray, and because the rule is inside the picker the brute-force reference (`bruteForce`, `pick-parity.cjs`) applies it identically. `anyHit` unchanged (spaces are site, 2026-09-24). **Measured** (reference arch model, 135 MB, 15 892 elements, guarded, 1512 × 982, vsync-capped at 60 Hz both): frame 16.70 → 16.70 ms median, JS 3.6 → 3.9 ms (noise band), 97 → 96 objects, 4 823 993 → 4 808 461 triangles, 2 261 649 → 2 193 120 lines (renderer.info); hover 0.1 ms both; pick-parity 115 / 115 / 24 grid rays and 2 000 random rays identical. |
| **2026-09-24 — a network or device path is refused before the file system is touched, unless the user reached it themselves** | `realpath` or `stat` on `\\host\share\x.ifc` makes Windows open an SMB session to that host and offer it the user's NTLM credentials — before a single check has run — and a share link is a thing a person is handed by someone else. `file-protocol.ts`'s `isRemotePath` names any path whose first two characters are separators (`\\host`, `//host`, `\\?\…`, `\\.\…`, mixed), and `inspect` refuses it first. **Not refused everywhere, on purpose:** a Windows mapped drive's real path *is* its `\\server\share` form (libuv's `realpath` resolves it), so a blanket refusal would have broken every model opened from a network drive at the fetch-time re-check, and every recents pill for one after a relaunch. So `admit(paths, trusted)` lets a network path through when the user reached that host already — `'any'` from the native Open dialog (and its dev stand-in), the paths on main's own recents list for `file:admit`, and any path admitted earlier this session — and the protocol handler's re-check of an admitted path allows it. A share link, a stored session or a made-up path naming another machine is refused as "that file has moved". **The recents list cannot be used to launder one in:** `recents:add` refuses a network path that was not admitted this session (`isAdmitted`) and answers the list unchanged, so a compromised renderer naming `\\attacker\share\x.ifc` there gains nothing at the next `file:admit`. A drop whose path is a literal `\\server\share\…` is refused by `admit`, still loads from the `File` the drop handed over, and is not remembered (next row); a drop from a mapped drive letter is admitted like any local path. Tests: `file-protocol-remote.test.ts` wraps `realpath` and `stat` in spies and proves neither is called; `recents-ipc.test.ts` drives the real `recents:add` handler. |
| **2026-09-24 — a drop main did not admit loads, but is never remembered** | 2026-09-21 made every path the renderer stores or compares one that passed `admit()`. A drop main refuses — a literal network path since S1, or anything `admit` turns down — still loads from its `File` (the bytes are in hand), but its path is only the drop's own spelling, and it used to be sent to `recents:add` anyway: stored un-canonicalised, and after S1 refused there for a network path. `upload-pipeline.ts` now marks such a pick `unadmitted` and skips `addRecent` for it. What the user sees: the model opens as before, and does not appear under Recent — to have it there, open it through the Open dialog. `session-paths.test.ts` drops one refused and one admitted file and checks only the admitted path reaches `addRecent`. |
| **2026-09-24 — one size cap on the assistant's JSON from the renderer: 1 000 000 characters** | The contract said "JSON of a bounded size" and bounded nothing. `AI_JSON_MAX_CHARS` (`shared/ipc-contract.ts`) is measured as `JSON.stringify(value).length`, the way `sessions.ts` measures a session, in `main/ai/session.ts`: a tool result (or failure message) over it reaches the model as a short `is_error` — the transcript still gets its `tool_result` — and a turn whose `viewState` or `schema` is over it is refused with a `bad_request` event before any request. One million characters is ~250 000 tokens; every legitimate result is bounded at the source by 2026-09-20's caps (200 SQL rows, 50 colour groups, 40 valid values, 25 clash rows, 10-element samples, 2 000 ids) and the schema by 150 values a category, so nothing legitimate is near it. Two smaller bounds ride along: `SettingsPatch.model` is at most 200 characters, and `ai:turn:abort` carries its `turnId` and stops only that turn — an abort that arrives after a newer turn started stops nothing. |
| **2026-09-24 — the window never leaves the app's own page** | `window.ts`'s `lockNavigation`: `will-navigate` is prevented unless the target is the loaded page with at most a different `#hash` (Electron does not even emit it for a hash change; the check is belt and braces), `will-attach-webview` is prevented, and the existing open handler still denies every new window and hands `http(s)` to the OS browser. A dropped URL, a stray link or a `location.href` in a compromised renderer can no longer load another origin into the window that holds the preload bridge. Share links are unaffected — a launch link travels in the hash, a later one over IPC — and the e2e suite's share-link and launch-link cases pass. |
| **2026-09-24 — an `.ifczip` is read in two passes, so a refused archive inflates nothing** | The first pass reads the ZIP's directory only (a `filter` that answers `false` decompresses nothing), counting the `.ifc` candidates and their **declared** sizes; an archive with none, with several, or with one declaring more than 600 MB is refused there. Only then is the single entry inflated, and its real length re-checked against the same 600 MB. Before, every candidate was inflated before the count was looked at, so a crafted archive could make the worker decompress gigabytes to be told "contains 3 .ifc files". The two existing messages are unchanged; the oversize case gains the design's own phrase, `the .ifc file in the archive is larger than 600 MB`. |
| **2026-09-24 — the shipped GPU guard escalates: `wait` is not `none`** | `gpuGuardAction` answered `none` both under the limit and over it inside the grace period, and the watcher's `none` branch forgot the warning — so a renderer still over the limit was re-warned every second and **never crashed**, the one thing the guard exists to do. `wait` is now its own answer, and `gpuGuardStep` (pure, tested over consecutive polls) forgets the warning only on `none`. Over → `warn` → `wait` → `wait` → `crash` at 3 s, exactly as the header always described. |
| **2026-09-24 — `viewer.batch(fn)`: a run of setters repaints, rebuilds the edges and rebuilds the picker once** | Refactor pass 2, P3. One hide went through `applyVis` as `setVisibility` + `setHighlight` + `setElementColors`, each repainting every part and two of them rebuilding the edge index — 3 full repaints and 2 edge rebuilds a click, plus the fade's own; one `commitModels` added 5 repaints, 3 edge rebuilds and 2 picker rebuilds on top of `addModel`'s. **Mechanism:** inside `viewer.batch(fn)` (synchronous, nests) `requestPaint` / `requestEdges` / `requestPicker` only mark, and the batch's end runs each marked one once, in the order the setters ran them (paint, edges, picker). Outside a batch each runs at once, as before. All three are functions of the state the setters leave behind — `applyElement` is a pure decision per element, and a fading element keeps its fade paint either way — so the end result is what the sequence left. **The one exception is the pick list's fade rule** (`viewer-core.js` L509: during a crossfade the picker holds the set as it stood before it): `setVisibility` settles a picker rebuild already asked for *before* it starts a fade. **Rejected:** dirty flags consumed in `frame()` — a click, a context menu or `debug()` between the setter and the next frame would have read a stale picker, and a hidden window draws no frame. The shell wraps `applyVis`, `commitModels`, `step`, `restoreView`, `revertTurn` and `applySession`'s restore (`batched()` in `state/shell.ts`). **Measured** (134 MB model, 15 706 elements): hide 31.7 → 19.9 ms, show all 29.0 → 18.9, isolate 27.1 → 17.8, a commit 5.04 → 1.01 repaints; captures of nine states identical within the build's own run-to-run noise; `frame-triggers.cjs` 24/24. |
| **2026-09-24 — a session's element ids are renumbered onto the slots its files have now** | Refactor pass 2, P5. An element id is `slot × 1 000 000 + expressId`, and a boot hands slots out in batch order — which was the order the parses happened to finish in, so a two-file session reopened with the files the other way round hid the other model's elements. **Now:** `sessionFiles()` records each file's `slot`; `join()` orders a session's batch by it (`sessionOrder`, so a reopened session usually keeps its numbering; a payload from before slots were recorded uses its place in `files`, and files it does not name go last); and after the join `restorableIds` **renumbers** every id from its file's saved slot to the slot that file (matched by path) is open on now — `liveSlot × ID_STRIDE + id mod ID_STRIDE` — and carries `active` from the saved key to the key the file is open under. **The rule:** every one of the session's files must be open, or no id is restored (`hidden` empty, `active` unset); an id whose slot belonged to no file of the session is dropped; everything by name — storeys, models, the stack, the section, the camera — restores as before. So a session saved after a replacement (the replacement sat on a slot of its own, P10) restores on a fresh boot, a link opened with models already loaded restores onto the slots its files joined on, and a removal's gaps do not matter. *(First version, rejected in review: restore only when every file came back on exactly its saved slot — which dropped every id after a replacement.)* **Tested:** `session-codec.test.ts` (`sessionOrder`; `restorableIds` with slots 1 → 0, 0/2 → 0/1, after boot, a missing file, a payload without slots, a renamed key). |
| **2026-09-24 — a real pick while the demo building is on screen replaces the demo** | Refactor pass 2, P9; the owner prefers fresh loads. After the demo, a real file joined the demo's federation: the demo's offset (`[0,0,0]`), its project frame (the identity) and its `frameKey`, so the file's scene coordinates, the property card's Base / Centroid rows and every link cut from it were in the demo's frame. **Now** `begin()` treats a pick while the demo is shown exactly as a pick on the landing page (`freshStart`): no "Replace model?" question — the demo is not the user's model — the demo leaves (`resetUnshown`: models, offset, frame, coordinates, and the empty commit that prunes hidden ids, colours, `active`, colour-by and the selection), and what of the review still points into the demo goes too (`forgetDemoView`: storey and model switches, the filter stack, the section, the undo history, measurements and spot coordinates). The landing page's rows then load the file as a boot. A user preference — theme, grids, shadows — stays. The flag is set once the demo has joined and cleared by that fresh start or a dispose. **Tested:** `replace-pick.test.ts`. |
| **2026-09-24 — a batch whose join fails undoes itself, and a replacement streams in beside the model it replaces** | Refactor pass 2, P10. A throw in `viewer.addModel` mid-batch left the model in the indexes and the federation; a replacement dropped the old model *before* the new one joined, so a failure lost both; and after boot `join()`'s catch called `bootFailed`, which put the landing page over a loaded federation. **Now:** `viewer.addModel` streams a model whose key is already in the scene under a label of its own, removes the old one only once every chunk is in, and relabels the new one (`store.relabel` / `edges.relabel`); a stream that throws takes out whatever it put in. `joinOne` gives every model the lowest free slot and pins it through `federate` — so **a replacement takes a new slot**, not the freed one, and its ids differ from the old model's (the pruning of hidden ids, colours and `active` is unchanged). `undoBatch` closes the failed file in the worker, removes every model the batch joined before it, keeps one that replaced a model (its predecessor is gone), and ends the loading state — a commit when something changed, `ready` otherwise; a failed boot leaves no model, offset or frame. `join()` then shows the batch's rows as the designed error row when a model is on screen, and calls `bootFailed` only when nothing was booted; `openLibrary` follows the same rule. The part-state table only grows: a replacement allocates fresh rows and the old model's are not reclaimed until nothing is loaded (`forget` resets it then) — a few MB per replacement of a 60 000-part model. A side effect, in the owner's favour: replacing the only model used to rebuild the camera rig from scratch (read from the code — the removal emptied the store, and `restage(true)` carries no camera — not observed); the camera now stays, as the replacement row above intended. **Tested:** `replace-pick.test.ts` (a failed replacement keeps the old model, slot for slot; a later failure takes earlier joins out; a failed boot leaves nothing; the error row after boot). |
| **2026-09-24 — the macOS guards read `footprint(1)` with one parser, bound every call, and fail closed on a listing they could not make** | Refactor pass 4, G1 + G2. **G1:** `safe-e2e.cjs` and `safe-app.cjs` each carried their own `footprint` regex, `([\d.]+)\s*([KMG])B\s*\(?\s*phys_footprint`, which expects the number *before* the label — `footprint -p` prints `phys_footprint: 1,234 MB`, number after. It only ever matched by accident, across the newline, on the **`phys_footprint_peak`** line above: it read the peak, and of `2,048 MB` only the `048` after the separator (48 MB). Without a peak line it fell through to a `Total` fallback that does not match the `TOTAL` row either, and read **0 MB** (all three checked on a sample output). Both now use the tested `parseFootprintMB` the in-process guard and `src/main/gpu-guard.ts` already share (moved into the Electron-free `scripts/lib/mac-procs.cjs`; `electron-guard.cjs` re-exports it, so `gpu-guard.test.ts`'s agreement test still holds and a new test proves it is the same function). Their `/bin/ps`, `footprint` and `sysctl` calls are bounded at 5 s, 4 s and 2 s with `SIGKILL`, the pattern the Windows branch uses, because an unbounded `spawnSync` freezes the poll and `SGVUE_MAX_SECONDS` with it. An unreadable reading keeps its old meaning — footprint 0, pressure 1 — so no limit moved. **G2:** `safe-run.cjs` read an empty or failed `/bin/ps` as *no dev Electron is running* and started anyway, then printed `clean:` over a listing that never happened. It now fails closed exactly as its Windows branch does: the startup listing is retried once and, if neither attempt ran, the run is **refused with exit 69 before anything starts**; a finish whose listing failed prints `COULD NOT LIST` instead of `clean:`, the exit code unchanged. `readPsListing` is the pure judge — errored, killed at the timeout, non-zero or no rows at all is *could not list* (`ps -A` always lists itself). **The same fail-closed startup and finish went into `safe-e2e.cjs` and `safe-app.cjs`**, because adding a timeout to their `/bin/ps` without it would have created a new fail-open path: a timed-out startup listing would make the user's own packaged SGVue look like the run's to kill. Nothing else in the kill or exit flow changed. **Verified:** `tests/unit/mac-procs.test.ts` (the parser on real multi-line output with thousands separators and the `phys_footprint_peak` line above the real one; every could-not-list shape; the retry). The macOS path cannot run on this Windows machine; a forced `process.platform = 'darwin'` run of all three wrappers here — where `/bin/ps` does not exist — printed `REFUSED: could not list running processes …` and exited 69 with nothing started, which is the fail-closed path end to end. |
| **2026-09-25 — updates are manual: Help › Check for updates… opens the releases page in the user's browser** | Asked for by the owner: *"add the check for updates menu item."* The item sits under `About SGVue` in the Help menu on both platforms and calls `shell.openExternal(RELEASES_URL)`, where `RELEASES_URL` (`https://github.com/sgvue/releases/releases`) is one hard-coded constant in `src/main/about.ts`; no renderer input reaches it. **Why manual, not an auto-updater:** the source repository is private, so an updater on a user's machine could not read it without a token the app must never carry; the builds are unsigned, so there is no signature to verify a download against and the OS would warn on or refuse a silently swapped binary; and an updater is a network client, a download path and a third disk writer beside `settings.ts` and `sessions.ts`, which is a design change. So the app makes **no network call** for this: the OS opens the page and the user decides. `publish: null` in `electron-builder.yml` stays. `openExternal` never navigates the window, so the 2026-09-24 navigation lock and `setWindowOpenHandler` are unaffected. A native menu item, like About: no designed surface changes. **Tested:** `tests/unit/menu.test.ts` (the item is in Help; its click calls `openExternal` with exactly the constant; the constant is https on github.com) and a smoke e2e that replaces `shell.openExternal` in main and clicks the real item. Shipped as **1.0.1**. *Amended 2026-09-28: the item opens the product site, `https://sgvue.github.io/?v=<version>`, and `RELEASES_URL` is gone — see that row.* *Amended 2026-10-01: the app does make one network call of its own now — the launch-time update check, the owner's choice, in its own row below. It is a request for a version number, not an updater: no download path and no disk writer were added, and this menu item still makes no call.* |
| **2026-09-25 — a second window, Schedules, over a private MessagePort; main holds no model data** | Owner-approved: *"I want this ifcTable to be wired together with sgvue, but as separate window. The UI everything must be closely harmony with the sgvue design. Then the table i can interact with any element table row then it will show me in the 3d."* **Shape.** `main/schedules-window.ts` creates at most one `BrowserWindow` on demand — the toolbar button (`schedules:open`, no payload, zod-checked `z.undefined()`, accepted only from the main window's `webContents`) or Window › Schedules (Ctrl/⌘+Shift+T: `keys.ts` reads no T and it is no OS default). Same `webPreferences` as the main window, `lockNavigation` applied, the production CSP byte for byte in `renderer/schedule.html`. It closes when the main window closes; closing it leaves the main window. **The channel.** Main makes a `MessageChannelMain` pair on each page's `did-finish-load` (whichever loads last connects them; a reload makes a new pair) and posts one end to each `webContents` on `schedule:port`; each preload re-posts it into its page with `window.postMessage` — Electron's documented pattern, since a port cannot cross `contextBridge`. From then on main sees nothing: main window → Schedules `store` (the snapshot, on connect and on every federation change) and `theme`; Schedules → main window `select {ids, zoom}`, validated with zod in the receiving renderer and applied through the tree's own `select(ids, zoom)`, so undo, the property card and the viewer behave exactly as for a tree click. An empty post on the same channel tells the main window Schedules closed, and it stops building snapshots. **Measured on the way:** a page's own `isLoading()` and `isLoadingMainFrame()` both still read true inside its `did-finish-load`, so a loading-state guard kept the windows from ever connecting — the load events alone are the gate. **The Schedules preload exposes nothing and imports only `electron`:** a module both preloads import is hoisted by Rollup into `preload/chunks/`, which a sandboxed preload cannot `require` (both windows would silently lose their bridge); electron-vite's `isolatedEntries` would lift that but throws in 5.0.0 whenever stdout is not a TTY. The channel name is therefore written once more there, held equal to `CH_SCHEDULE_PORT` by `tests/unit/schedule/preload.test.ts`. **Not watched by `gpu-guard.ts`:** Chromium has one GPU process per app and the main window's watch already reads it; this page draws no WebGL. **Measured** on the 134 MB reference model (15 889 elements): adapter 110–125 ms, structured-clone 16.8 MB, zod validation 167 ms, indexing 21 ms, toolbar click → first table 1.0 s, row click → property card 154 ms, Schedules renderer JS heap 21 MB (working set 185 MB), main renderer JS heap unchanged at 52 MB. **Cost to the main window:** one 32 px toolbar button, so the centred toolbar is 16 px wider each side; at the default window the theme toggle sits further over the view cube's empty corner, which already passes a real click through (2026-09-24). |
| **2026-09-25 — ifcTable's pure engine is ported, its extractor is not** | `src/schedule/` keeps ifcTable's own `ifc/` (types, entities, units, store), `schedule/` (def, engine, formula, format, compare, computed, conditional, resolve, columns, presets, valueSearch, fonts), `export/` (rows, csv) and `history.ts`, with the file names and relative imports unchanged, and its hard rule — nothing there touches `document` or `window` (`tests/unit/schedule/boundary.test.ts`). Its tests are in `tests/unit/schedule/`: **284 of ifcTable's cases**; the 31 left out need ifcTable UI modules or ExcelJS that later phases port (the inspector, coverage, `ruleEffects`, `kindOf`/`typeOf`, `moveItem`, the XLSX exporter). Changes to the engine: a `model` core field (optional on `ElemCore`, `Model` in the picker), two user-visible strings that named ifcTable now name SGVue, and the unused worker protocol types removed. **The extractor (`extract.ts`, web-ifc 0.0.68) is replaced by `schedule/adapter.ts`**, a pure function over SGVue's federation: one `ElemCore` and one `Record<"Pset.Prop", Cell>` per element, in SI with ifcTable's own `measureKind`, shared property sets converted once and kept shared (a structured clone preserves the sharing), plus `rowIds`. **Field gaps**, left empty rather than guessed: `space` for anything but an `IfcSpace` (the index keeps neither space containment nor `IfcRelSpaceBoundary`); `objectType` is the index's type-first string, not the occurrence's raw attribute; `predefinedType` has `USERDEFINED` already resolved; `storey` is `Name` before `LongName` where ifcTable reads `LongName` first; `building` / `site` come from the storey's place in the spatial tree, so an element with no storey has neither; `IfcSite` / `IfcBuilding` / `IfcBuildingStorey` are not rows (not federation elements, so never selectable); `authoringTool` is the header's originating system. |
| **2026-09-25 — main names the main window** | Until now "the window" was found: `targetWindow()` was the focused window or `getAllWindows()[0]`, and `deep-link.ts` delivered links to `getAllWindows()[0]`. With Schedules able to hold focus, Preferences… would have been sent to a page with no Preferences, About would have attached to the wrong window, and a share link could have gone to the schedule. `window.ts` now keeps the main window by reference (`setMainWindow` on create, cleared on `closed`); `mainWindow()` is what `targetWindow()`, `deep-link.ts`, the `schedules:open` sender check and macOS `activate` use. |
| **2026-09-25 — the owner's plan for the Schedules port, and one future writer** | Phases: (1) plumbing, engine and a basic table whose row click shows the element in 3D — built; (2) the full ifcTable UI; (3) two-way selection and right-click Isolate / Hide / Show; (4) export. **Export will add ONE disk writer**, limited to paths the user picks in a native Save dialog — owner-approved, to be built in phase 4; until then `src/main/settings.ts` and `src/main/sessions.ts` remain the only two writers and the guard test is unchanged. Extras chosen by the owner: two-way selection, right-click Isolate / Hide / Show, multi-model schedules. |
| **2026-09-25 — the Windows installer gives SGVue.exe the high-performance GPU, unless the user has already chosen** | Reported on the owner's HP ZBook (Intel Arc integrated + NVIDIA RTX 1000 Ada): Windows ran SGVue on the integrated GPU, and orbiting a large or federated model was choppy. Windows' own per-app setting fixes it — Settings › System › Display › Graphics › SGVue.exe, *High performance* with *Optimizations for windowed games* — and that setting is one `REG_SZ` under `HKCU\Software\Microsoft\DirectX\UserGpuPreferences`, named by the exe's full path. **Now** `build/installer.nsh`, wired in by `nsis.include`: `customInstall` writes `GpuPreference=2;SwapEffectUpgradeEnable=1;` for `$INSTDIR\SGVue.exe` **only when no value exists**, so a value already there — power saving, or anything the user picked — is kept on a fresh install and on every update; `customUnInstall` deletes it only on a real uninstall (never while an update runs the old uninstaller with `--updated`, `${isUpdated}`) and only while its data is still exactly that string. **Installer-only and Windows-only**: no app or renderer code, no Chromium switch, nothing for macOS. **Invisible — no pixel differs**; only which adapter draws them. **Measured on the owner's machine**, `SGVue-1.0.2-setup.exe /S` over the installed 1.0.1: value deleted → the installer wrote it; value `GpuPreference=1;` → a second install kept it; the installed app, started through `safe-app.cjs` with a throwaway `--user-data-dir`, reads `UNMASKED_RENDERER_WEBGL` = `ANGLE (NVIDIA, NVIDIA RTX 1000 Ada Generation Laptop GPU …, D3D11)` with the value set, and `ANGLE (Intel, Intel(R) Graphics …)` with `GpuPreference=1;`. The uninstall path was **not run** on the owner's install; it was read in the compiled uninstaller (`--updated` test → `ReadRegStr` → `StrCmpS` against the exact string → `DeleteRegValue`). Shipped as **1.0.2**. |
| **2026-09-25 — the Schedules window's whole interface is ifcTable's, and every visual decision in it is SGVue's** | Owner's requirement: *"The UI everything must be closely harmony with the sgvue design."* Phase 2 ports the rest of ifcTable's `src/ui/` into `src/renderer/schedule-ui/` as vanilla DOM, handler for handler and string renderer for string renderer: one `state` / `update()` / `render()` (`state.ts`), delegated events from `#app` (`events.ts`), focus / caret / scroll kept across repaints (`preserve.ts`); the five-tab inspector (Fields, Filter with up to 8 rules, AND / OR and *Keeps N · removes M · show them*, Sort / group on 4 levels with header / footer / blank line, grand total and Itemise off → Count, Format with the conditional colours and *Colours N of M · show them*, Appearance), collapsible and resizable 340–560 px; the field browser with value search, the template gallery (14 general + IFC-SG Planting), My templates, the calculated-value editor; column resize / auto-fit / equal width, drag and keyboard reorder, heading selection, coverage ("0 values", bars, remap), the status line, undo / redo (ifcTable's `history.ts`, 60 steps, Ctrl/⌘+Z and Ctrl+Y / ⇧⌘Z in this window only), Escape, print. **Design mapping, once** (`schedule.css`'s header): surfaces `--card` / `--ground` / `--step-bg`, lines `--border` / `--border-strong`, the accent as `--sel-bg` + `--sel-ink` with an `--accent` edge; the section label (600 11 px uppercase), the 13 px row, the 11 px mono count; 10 px cards, 8 px fields and buttons, 6 px rows and icon buttons, 999 px pills; hover.css's own classes on the markup. A field is the Filter card's field with the `fv-field` focus; a secondary button is Preferences' Save; a primary one is the chat panel's send button; an *add* is the Filter card's `+ add filter step` link; a remove is its 22 px ×; a toggle chip is the sidebar's accent pill; a segmented control is the sidebar's Entity / Type pair; an inspector tab is a toolbar button on `--sel-bg`; glyphs are `icons.ts` in `icons.tsx`'s stroke idiom (paths copied where the main window has the idea: Filter, Cross, chevrons, Revert for undo). The one control SGVue has no idiom for, an on/off switch, is drawn from the tokens (`--border-strong` track, `--accent` on). Overlays are the Preferences scrim and card, with its keyboard contract (focus in to the search box, Tab trapped, focus back to the opener); they carry no entrance animation because a keystroke rebuilds them (ifcTable's rule), while the dialog and the toast, created once, take `fadein`. **A header bar replaces two ifcTable surfaces**: its centre head (the schedule's name, the template chip, Auto-fit, Equal width) is ported whole; its app bar is not, and the header adds the category chip, Templates, My templates, Save, Print, undo and redo as 30 px `hv-step` buttons with `data-tip`. **Dropped, and why:** the brand, the feedback `mailto:`, the theme toggle (the theme follows the main window), the start / loading / error screens, the drop zone, the backdrop and "Open model…" (the model comes from the main window); the Export menu (CSV, Excel, the every-saved-schedule workbook) and the `.schedule.json` export / import, because each writes or reads a file outside the two sanctioned writers — phase 4 brings export back behind a native Save dialog, so **no button for any of them is drawn**; the rail's Browse templates… / My templates… / Save / Export… / Import… buttons (the header carries the first three; the rail keeps the saved list); the one-time adoption of ifcTable's older storage key. **Changed, and why:** `confirm()` and `prompt()` are the window's own dialog (`dialog.ts`, the Preferences card) — Electron has no `prompt()` and its `confirm()` is an OS box; `/formula.html` is an in-window overlay with the same text, because a desktop window has no tab to open and may not navigate; its section links scroll rather than change the hash. **Kept from phase 1:** a data row carries its index in the result as `data-row` on every path (itemised, collapsed, grouped with header / footer / blank rows and a grand total); only data rows carry it, so group, footer, blank and grand rows select nothing; a collapsed row selects every element it stands for; the mark follows the clicked *element* (`state.picked`, a store row) through every re-sort and regroup. **Typed edits (review, the same day):** ifcTable debounced each text box with its own timer per selector and never flushed one, which lost or misplaced text three ways — typing in rule 1 then rule 2 inside the delay dropped rule 1; typing a heading then clicking another column renamed the new one (the target was resolved when the timer fired); typing the name then Save saved the old name. `pending.ts` holds **one** pending edit for the window, its target resolved when the key is pressed, and lands it when its delay runs out, synchronously when another box is typed in, when focus leaves its box, before any click or key action (window capture phase), and before a new snapshot replaces the definition. Landing changes state only; the repaint (`renderSoon`) waits for the pointer's release and a task, so a commit inside a pointerdown never rebuilds the element being clicked. Ctrl/⌘+Z with the caret in a text box is the box's own undo, not the schedule's. `pending.test.ts` drives the three loss paths (two of them proven to fail with the flush removed). **Shared, not copied:** `focusables` / `trapTab` moved unchanged from `app/focus.ts` into React-free `app/focus-trap.ts` (re-exported), so both windows trap Tab with the same code and the Schedules bundle carries no React. **Tested:** the 15 deferred ifcTable cases that needed these modules (kindOf / typeOf, coverage, moveItem, the inspector, ruleEffects) are restored — the 16 XLSX cases stay deferred to phase 4; ifcTable's `schedules.test.ts` (saved setups and the panel, less its three download-name cases) and `acts.test.ts` (every `data-act` wired both ways — it caught the header's `tool()` helper on its first run, and learned it); `window.test.ts` for the new renderers; `tests/e2e/schedules.spec.ts` for template → filter → group with Itemise off → a collapsed row selects both its walls in the main window → undo / redo → save → close → reopen → rename → load, and for column selection, auto-fit, equal width, a resize drag and its undo, keyboard reorder, coverage and the inspector's resize / collapse. |

The frame is `requestAnimationFrame` with a 100 ms `setTimeout` as its backstop — Chromium stops frames for an occluded window on Windows, and a selection the table made while the main window sat behind it must still come back. `quiet` is set when the change came from the table (a flag held around the store call, which zustand's synchronous subscription reads) or is a re-send after a new snapshot; the table then never scrolls. **The table marks rows in place**: a `Map` of each drawn `<tr>` by its result index, built once per table paint, and a `classList.toggle` per row — never a repaint, because a 4 082-row repaint costs 0.3–0.6 s. A selection made elsewhere scrolls its first marked row to the middle of the table, clear of the sticky heading. The dialog opens in Documents, or the folder the last export went to this session (never stored), on the page's `<name> — YYYY-MM-DD.<ext>` (ifcTable's `exportName`; its `safeName` keeps word characters, hyphens and spaces, so nothing Windows refuses reaches the dialog). The filter for a schedule file is `.json`, because a two-part extension in a filter is handled differently by each OS's dialog. Cancel writes nothing and says so. **The guard:** `FS_WRITER_ALLOW_LIST` names three modules and `tests/readonly-guard.test.ts` pins the list; `tests/unit/exports.test.ts` proves the rules against a mocked dialog — bytes land only at the dialog's path, nothing on cancel, the extension forced, the replace question and its Cancel, no temporary file left after a failed rename, the cap before any dialog, and the schema's refusals. |

| **2026-09-25 — on Windows SGVue chooses its own graphics adapter: NVIDIA, else AMD, else Windows decides** | Asked for by the owner: *"auto set the highest graphic card if available? Nvidia as first choice."* 1.0.2's installer value asks **Windows** for *High performance*, and on another PC the owner still saw SGVue on Intel; Windows' pick also follows whatever value the user or another tool already set, which the installer never overwrites. **Mechanism, measured on the RTX 3070 Ti + UHD 770 desktop:** Chromium's `--use-adapter-luid=<HighPart>,<LowPart>` (both decimal), appended before `ready`, makes ANGLE create its Direct3D 11 device on that adapter. The Intel LUID turned `UNMASKED_RENDERER_WEBGL` into `ANGLE (Intel, Intel(R) UHD Graphics 770 (0x00004680) Direct3D11 …, D3D11)` and the active `getGPUInfo` device into Intel; the NVIDIA LUID turned both back; `--force_low_power_gpu` alone lands on Intel, and adding the NVIDIA LUID wins over it. A LUID no adapter has is ignored (Windows' default is used); a value in any other form (`0x0,0x12a5e`) crashed the GPU process until Chromium turned the GPU off altogether (`GPU access not allowed … crashed too many times with software GL`, WebGL disabled) — so the value is only ever built from numbers (`luidSwitch`). `--force_high_performance_gpu` was not chosen: it defers to the same Windows ranking the owner does not trust. **Why no cache and no relaunch** (the brief proposed caching the adapter in `settings.json` and relaunching once on a mismatch): `app.getGPUInfo` carries no LUID, and a LUID is issued at boot — this boot NVIDIA `0x11524`, Intel `0x12a5e`, Basic Render `0x12a33` — so last boot's NVIDIA LUID can be this boot's Microsoft Basic Render Driver, which is software rendering. Instead every launch reads `HKLM\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\DirectX` with one bounded `reg query` (35–42 ms in the main process, not PowerShell): Windows' `DXGIAdapterCache` scheduled task (boot trigger, one-minute delay, plus adapter-change triggers) writes each adapter's `AdapterLuid`, `VendorId`, `DeviceId`, `Description`, `DedicatedVideoMemory` and `LastSeen` there, and **an entry last seen before this boot is refused**, so the first minute after a restart leaves the choice to Windows. Data read fresh cannot be stale, so there is nothing a relaunch would fix, and none was built. **The rule** (`chooseAdapter`, pure): NVIDIA `0x10DE`, else AMD `0x1002` — within a vendor the adapter with the most dedicated memory — else no switch; never the Basic Render Driver; only in the instance holding the single-instance lock. **After ready** main logs `[gpu] drawing on … (asked for …)`, and says `NOT the adapter asked for` when the active device's vendor and device id differ. **Off switch:** Preferences' `Prefer NVIDIA graphics when available` (`preferNvidia`, default on, shown on Windows only, read at the next launch). **Visible:** Help › About's Windows box ends with `Graphics: <active device>`; `debug().gpu` gives the harnesses `UNMASKED_RENDERER_WEBGL`, and `profile-frame.cjs` prints it. **The installer's value stays:** it is what Windows uses when the checkbox is off, when the registry read fails or predates this boot, and when no NVIDIA or AMD adapter is present. **Measured**, `profile-frame.cjs` orbit on the 134 MB model (15 706 elements, 4.8 M triangles, 1212 × 982): NVIDIA 16.7 ms median / 16.8 ms p95 — 60 fps, the display's cap; Intel 66.5 / 83.9 ms — 15.6 fps. **The dev guards** read the dedicated-memory counter summed over every LUID a pid holds, so the forced adapter is always counted: 691 MB peak on NVIDIA with the big model, 18 MB on Intel, whose memory is shared — on an integrated adapter only the working-set terms bound a run, as they always did when Windows put the app there. The shipped guard reads working set and is adapter-blind. macOS unchanged. Dev builds only: `SGVUE_GPU_VENDOR=<hex vendor id>` puts one vendor first, which is how `tests/e2e/gpu.spec.ts` proves NVIDIA → Intel → NVIDIA in the real app. Shipped as **1.0.3**. |
| **2026-09-25 — a GPU process that fails with the adapter switch on turns the switch off until the user retries** | Review of the row above, owner-facing safety: a driver that cannot run SGVue's device on the chosen adapter must not leave every later launch broken. **Now** `index.ts` (`watchGpuCrash`) listens, only on a launch that appended `--use-adapter-luid`, for `child-process-gone` with `type: 'GPU'` and a reason in `GPU_CRASH_REASONS` — `crashed`, `abnormal-exit`, `launch-failed` — **only while the app runs, never once `before-quit` has fired** (a GPU process that faults in driver teardown while the app quits says nothing about the adapter, and recording it would turn NVIDIA-first off after the first session on such a machine; `shouldRecordGpuCrash`, pure, unit-tested), and records **once** `gpuSwitchFailed: { at, vendorId, deviceId }` in `settings.json` (`setGpuSwitchFailed`; `settings.ts` is an allowed writer; main-only, never in `view()`). Not `oom` (a big model can run any adapter out of memory, and turning the discrete GPU off for that would be backwards), not `killed` (a guard, Task Manager), `clean-exit`, `integrity-failure` or `memory-eviction`. **The next launch** (`planGpu`, pure): a failure naming the adapter it would pick → no switch, and a `console.warn` saying why and how to retry; a failure naming a different adapter → the hardware changed, the failure is forgotten and the switch appended; nothing picked (the first minute after a boot) → nothing asked and the failure kept, because that is not a hardware change. **Retry:** any patch that sets `preferNvidia` — Preferences' checkbox off, or on — deletes the failure. **Cannot loop:** nothing relaunches; a launch that asked nothing has nothing to record. Measured in the real app (`gpu.spec.ts`): Chromium's own `chrome://gpucrash` in a hidden window recorded NVIDIA `0x10DE:0x2482`; the next launch had no switch; off-and-on in Preferences cleared it; the launch after asked again; three ordinary quits recorded nothing. **Two hardening changes in the same review:** `luidSwitch` returns `null` — no switch — when the LUID's `HighPart` is not 0 (every LUID seen had 0, and a form Chromium might not parse crashes the GPU process); and the registry parser reads a number only from a `REG_DWORD` / `REG_QWORD`, because `reg query` prints a `REG_BINARY` as bare hex digits that `BigInt` would read as decimal. The Preferences checkbox gains one muted 12 px line, `Takes effect the next time SGVue starts.`, and a mismatch between the adapter asked for and the one drawing is a `console.warn`. |
| **2026-09-28 — a property name the assistant sends is matched to the file's own spelling in `executeTool`, and only when that is certain** | Asked for by the owner in these words: *"Sometimes when i ask it check area with includesGFA, it never check the shared parameters Includes As GFA. Similar for others."* The file authors a Revit shared parameter `Includes As GFA` (a Yes/No, exported as `IfcBoolean`, which web-ifc hands over as a JS `true` / `false` — so the index, `matchFn` and the Filter card's value list all read `true` / `false`); the assistant guessed `includesGFA`; `attr()` is an exact, case-sensitive lookup — the design's, and the Filter card depends on it — so the rule matched nothing, and with `!=` or `absent` it matched **everything**. Three causes, each checked in code: the schema listed at most 150 names in the order the file first uses them, and a real export has more (197 on the reference model), the shared parameters last; every miss path answered with the first 40 of `propKeys` in that same order, never the names nearest the guess; and nothing mapped a typed "Yes" onto a stored `true`. **The fix is one pass in `executeTool`** (`executors/names.ts`, on the pure `shared/prop-names.ts`), before any executor runs, so no tool can forget it: a rule's `prop` in `rules` and in `steps[].rules`, `groupBy`, `property` and `attr` — every field in the catalogue that names a property, and no other (`name`, `text`, `model` are left alone). `resolveKey`: the exact key; else the **one** key equal after `normKey` (lower case, letters and digits only), so case, spaces, underscores and punctuation are forgiven; two keys that normalise alike → **neither** is chosen; a merely similar key is **never** chosen — `includesGFA` is not rewritten, because `as` is missing from it; `Pset.Key` / `Pset:Key` is tried whole first, then on what follows each separator, longest first, so a key that itself holds a dot (`Thickness.LowerBound`) is found whole. A rewrite goes into the input, so the Filter card, a saved filter set and a share link carry the file's own key, and the result gains `resolvedKeys` and one sentence. **Yes/no values**, on `=` / `!=` only: yes/no, true/false, y/n, 1/0, t/f, .t./.f. and ✓/✗, any case, become the one stored spelling that means the same — only when the value as written matches no stored value (case-insensitively, as `matchFn` compares, so `TRUE` is left alone), **every** stored value of that key is a yes/no word, and exactly one stored spelling has that meaning; the result gains `resolvedValues`. **A name that is still not a key** — on every tool, success included, because `!=` and `absent` over a missing key match everything — gets `valid_values.propKeys: { nearest: { <asked>: 8 names }, total, truncated }` and a sentence, in place of the first forty. The ranking (`rankKeys`, deterministic): exact; normalised; *contains* — the normalised name inside the key's, or every asked word among the key's words (words split at camelCase, acronyms, digits and punctuation; two words match when equal or when one begins the other and the shorter has three letters); *partial* — a shared word, or the key inside the asked name; then spelling alone. Within a class: more shared words, then edit distance, then the shorter key, then plain string order. So `includesGFA` puts `Includes As GFA` first, ahead of `GFA`. **A value that matched nothing** under a real key answers with that key's 15 commonest values and counts (`valid_values.topValues`, `truncated` when cut) on every rule-taking miss — `query_elements`, `summarize_elements`, `apply_visibility`, `set_filter_stack` (the steps that matched nothing), `select_elements`, `color_by_property`, `list_values`, `find_properties`. A **real** key that the elements asked about do not carry now says how many elements of the federation do (`carriedInFederation`) rather than listing keys. `attr()`, `matchFn`, the rule grammar and every designed surface are unchanged — the Filter card showing the file's key is the card it always was, so this is not a deviation; `validText` lost its last caller and was removed. |

The assistant could only use names the schema listed and guess the rest; this is the search it lacked. Input `{ text, rules?, limit? }` (1–50, default 20), strict, `additionalProperties: false`, the shared `RULES`; the name passes the read-only guard's mutating-name test. It ranks **names**, never values (`search` does values), with the ranking a miss uses, over the names the scoped elements carry — the seven attributes included — in two passes: which names the pool carries at all (string work only), then the numbers for the chosen names, each value read the way `attr()` reads it so every count is the count a rule would see. Per name: `match`, `elements` carrying a value, the sets it sits in (top 5 and `setCount`), `kind` (number / text / boolean / mixed), `measureTypes` from `psetMeta` when there are any, the 5 commonest values and `distinct`, and `truncated` when either list was cut; the answer's own `truncated` is true when names were cut **or** any name's lists were. When nothing in scope shares a word with the text, the 5 nearest by spelling, and with rules the names alike **outside** the scope (`outsideScope`), so a wrong scope cannot read as "the file has no such property". **Prompt:** one line placed beside the design's own psetKeys line, commented `// 2026-09-28 — owner: "it never check the shared parameters Includes As GFA"`, in sentence case (a test asserts no shouting outside the quoted example and `LIKE`): names are authored by people and exporters ("Includes As GFA", not includesGFA); when the user names a property that is not an exact key, call find_properties (or query_sql with LIKE over property.name) before concluding anything; never report a property as missing on the strength of one guessed spelling; `resolvedKeys` / `resolvedValues` mean describe it with the file's key and value. `EXTRA_TOOLS_PARAGRAPH` names find_properties beside list_values. | **2026-09-28 — property names get their own schema cap, `SCHEMA_KEY_CAP` = 1 000; every other category keeps 150** | At the shared 150 a real Revit export lost its tail: the reference model has 197 names, they are listed in the order the file first uses them, and the shared parameters a user adds come last — so the name the owner asked about could be one of the "… N more". The block is `system[1]`, a cache breakpoint: written once per federation and read at the cache price on every later turn, so on the largest export it costs about what the tools block does. |

Finish: "Open SGVue now" (electron-builder's StartApp) and "Create a desktop shortcut" — ticked on a new install, on an update or repair only if the shortcut existed; ticked creates a missing one, unticked removes it; `createDesktopShortcut` `always` → `true`, because `always` recreated a deleted shortcut on every update. Windows 10/11 has no supported way for an installer to pin to the taskbar, so the Finish text says how. English only (`multiLanguageInstaller: false`), like the app. Bitmaps `build/installerSidebar.bmp` (164×314, installer and uninstaller) and `build/installerHeader.bmp` (150×57), 24-bit, from `scripts/make-installer-images.py` (the app icon, the dark/light tokens, IBM Plex). Uninstaller: a wizard, its Welcome button reads Uninstall; a real uninstall removes the desktop shortcut; GPU value logic unchanged. **Updates from the one-click installs:** same uninstall key and `InstallLocation`; the new installer runs the old uninstaller with `/S --keep-shortcuts --updated` (`installUtil.nsh:198–224`), so the GPU value and the shortcuts stay. A fresh install's folder is `SGVue` (was `sgvue`; the same folder on NTFS). | **2026-09-28 — the assistant makes and reads schedules: `make_schedule` (view) and `get_schedule` (read), the engine run in the main renderer** | Asked for by the owner in these words: *"wire the schedules with the AI. Improve AI abilities."* — the first two of the four abilities they chose (export and colour-from-chat come separately). **Where the engine runs:** in the main window's renderer, because every tool runs there and main holds no model data. `schedule-link.ts`'s `scheduleStore()` builds the store the Schedules window builds — the adapter's own `snapshotOf` (the sidebar's model names as the Model field), indexed by ifcTable's own `StoreBuilder` — so what the model reads is what the table shows. It is built the first time `get_schedule` or `make_schedule` runs — **never by a chat turn**: the per-turn view state is the Schedules window's own report (`current` carries the def and the row count its table shows), so no store is built and the engine does not run for it — and not when the window opens (a real model's snapshot is tens of MB, and most sessions never ask). It is held in one slot keyed by the federation object and the model names, and released the moment the federation changes (the slot's own store subscription, window or no window), when the Schedules window closes, or when `make_schedule` gives up on a window that never joined. The `SGVUE_IFC` e2e logs its one-time build cost on a real model (none is in `samples/` here, so unmeasured). **The shape the model speaks** (`schedule/assistant.ts`, pure): `title?`, `category?` (IFC classes, unioned — the engine's own `entity: string[]`; there is no "all", which would put every class in the header), `columns?` `[{field, heading?, total?}]`, `filters?` `[{field, op, value?, values?}]` in the engine's thirteen operators, `sortBy?`, `groupBy?` (each a sort level with a heading row and a `titleCountTotals` footer when any column totals, else `count`, as the templates do), `grandTotals?`. **Field names:** a calculated column of the schedule being changed, by name; a core field by its label, its key (not `mark`, which is Revit's ElementId — "Mark" is the Revit parameter) or SGVue's attribute name (`IfcEntity`, `Level`, …); `Pset.Prop` exactly as the catalogue has it; else any property name through the same `resolveKey` the rule grammar uses, as `{pset:'*', prop}` — the engine's own wildcard, which matches names case-insensitively, so two keys that differ only by case are one column and are not ambiguous. An unknown or ambiguous name refuses **the whole call** with the nearest names (`valid_values.fields`); nothing half-built is sent. A new column is dressed as the field picker dresses it (`columnForProp` / `columnForCore`). **What comes back** (`readSchedule`): the definition in the input's own shape, headings, a page of rows as displayed text (default 20, cap 50, `offset`), every group's count and subtotals (cap 50), the grand totals, each measured column's display `unit` and the SI `filterUnit` its filters compare in (on `schedule.columns[]` — cells and totals are written in the first, filter numbers are in the second, so a model reading "900" under `mm` filters with 0.9), `rowCount` / `elementCount` / `categoryElements`, `filled` per column and `filterKeeps` per filter (what each keeps alone — "why is this empty?"); cells clipped at 120 characters, rows and groups together at 300 000, `truncated` whenever a row or group was left out. Chat UI: the chips every tool already has — one per top-level group, else one for everything listed. **Tested:** `tests/unit/schedule/assistant.test.ts` (28), `tests/unit/ai-schedule.test.ts` (10), and a guarded e2e that drives both tools over the real `ai:tool:exec` IPC. Measured on the mock: a grouped door schedule's result is 1 775 characters; the tools block grows 23 523 → 28 870 B (27 → 29 tools). |
| **2026-09-28 — the open schedule crosses the port as `current` and `define`, each checked twice** | Schedules → main window: `current {def, rowCount}` (a `null` def while it shows none) — posted at most once per 250 ms after a paint that changed the definition or its row count, and on every new port, carrying what is on screen at the moment the timer fires, so it always converges. `rowCount` is the painted result's own `totalDataRows`; only while a filter or colour-rule preview narrows the table does that window run the engine once more, without the preview. Main window → Schedules: `define {kind, def}` (`kind` `made` or `edited`), sent only after the snapshot on the same port, so it can never arrive before the store it is a schedule of. Both are model or user data, so both are untrusted on receipt: zod's `DefShape` bounds every array and string (200 columns, the engine's 8 filters and 4 sort levels, 64 classes, 100 calculated values, 200 000 characters in all) and types every field reference as one of the engine's three kinds (a core key from `CORE_KEYS`, a named property, a formula id), then `parseScheduleDef` — the file importer's own validator — reads it before anything uses it. The main window keeps the last one in a module holder (`openSchedule()`), cleared with the port and never in the view store, a session or a share link: it belongs to the Schedules window and lives exactly as long. `defineSchedule` also sets the holder at once, with the row count `make_schedule`'s own engine run found, so the next turn sees it before the echo arrives; the window drops any `current` queued for the old schedule on a `define` and always echoes the new one. In the Schedules window a `define` lands any pending typed edit, then goes through `update({def})` — the same path a template takes — so it is on the window's own undo history; it clears column marks and previews, marks the schedule unsaved (so replacing it asks first), keeps the saved-setup / template marks for an edit and clears them for a new one, and flashes the existing toast: `Schedule from Ask SGVue` / `Schedule changed by Ask SGVue` (the fidelity contract's Schedules entry names both). The view state gains one field, `schedule`, built from the holder alone (`scheduleBrief(def, rowCount)`, pure — the engine's own `visibleColumns` / `disambiguate` give the headings the table shows), so a chat turn costs no store and no engine run — `{title, category, columns, filters, rowCount}` or `null` — the headings capped at 40 of 60 characters and the filter text at 300 — sent every turn outside the cached prefix; `get_view_state` reports it too. Two prompt lines (2026-09-28): when to reach for `make_schedule`, and that "this schedule" is the view state's `schedule`, read with `get_schedule` before any total is stated. |
| **2026-09-28 — changing the open schedule is `make_schedule` with `base:"open"`, not a separate tool; bounds in words, enforced by zod** | The task offered `base:"open"` or an `edit_schedule`. A tool named `edit_…` fails the read-only guard's mutating-name test, and renaming it to dodge the guard would be the wrong lesson; one input shape for both also keeps the catalogue one tool smaller. With `base:"open"`: `columns` are added at the right — or, for a field the schedule already shows, re-headed and re-totalled (`total:"none"` takes a total away) rather than added twice; `removeColumns` takes columns away by heading or field and refuses one the schedule does not have, naming its columns; `filters` replaces the filter list when given (`[]` clears); a hidden column it is asked to add is shown again; `groupBy` replaces the grouping levels and keeps the plain sort, `sortBy` the reverse; `title` renames; `category` moves it. Everything the simplified shape cannot say is left exactly as it was — calculated columns, formats, colour rules, widths, alignment, appearance, itemise, the template note — asserted field by field. **Filters cap at 8, not the 20 the task named:** `parseScheduleDef` keeps only the engine's `MAX_FILTERS` (8), so a ninth would be dropped in silence. **No `maxItems` / `maxLength` in the schemas:** Anthropic strict tool use does not support array or string length keywords, and a refused schema costs every tool its strictness (the gateway's fallback); the bounds (40 columns, 8 filters, 4 sort-and-group fields, 12 classes) are stated in the descriptions and refused by zod before any name is resolved. **The window is opened — and so brought forward — only when it is closed** (then `make_schedule` waits up to 8 s for it to join, and says so if it does not): an open window is left where the user put it, so a change asked for in chat does not take the keyboard away from the chat. |

| **2026-09-28 — `export_schedule`: the one tool that may open a Save dialog, and the read-only guard amended for exactly it** | Owner-approved, in the words of the option they chose: *"Export from chat — 'Export this schedule to Excel' opens the normal Save dialog for you. You still pick where it goes."* **Shape:** view, strict, `{ format }` with `format` one of `xlsx`, `csv`, `all_saved`, `schedule_file` — the Export menu's four entries — and nothing else: no path, no file name, no content; zod re-checks it and a stray key is dropped before the executor. **Path:** the tool refuses when no Schedules window is joined or it shows no schedule (`openSchedule()`), then posts `export {n, format}` over the private port (`schedule/messages.ts`, `.strict()`: any other key refuses the message). The Schedules window runs **the Export menu's own action** — the menu now calls the same `EXPORT_ACTIONS[format]` table (`schedule-ui/exporting.ts`), so there is no second path — which builds the bytes, asks main for the native Save dialog attached to that window (`export:save`, unchanged) and shows the menu's own toast. Before it starts, it answers `exportAck {n, refused}` **at once**: `null` (started), or the menu's own reason — `busy` (an export of that window is in progress, i.e. its dialog may be open: `deliver`'s existing one-at-a-time flag — *amended 2026-10-02: or that window's own confirm or prompt is up; the refactor pass's row, below*), `no_schedule` (the header and its menu are not drawn), `nothing_saved` (the menu disables "All saved schedules" until one is saved). The main window keeps **one request at a time**: a second while the first awaits its answer is `busy` without being sent, a closed port answers `closed`, and after 3 s (`EXPORT_ACK_MS`) `timeout` — the wait is for the window's answer, never for the dialog, because the tool budget is 20 s and a person can take longer. On `opened` the executor raises the window through the toolbar button's own route (`window.sgvue.openSchedules()` → main's `openSchedules()`: restore + focus), no new IPC. **What the model is told:** that the Save dialog is opening in the Schedules window, that the user picks the name and folder, that nothing is saved unless they click Save, and that the tool is not told whether they did — never that a file was saved; no result of the tool names a path. One prompt line says the same. **Why an acknowledgement rather than a busy flag mirrored in the main window:** the refusal rules (busy, saved setups in that window's `localStorage`, a schedule on screen) all live in the Schedules window, so it decides them with the menu's own state and main never duplicates it; the request number keeps a late answer from being read as a newer request's. **The guard** (`tests/readonly-guard.test.ts`): the mutating-name test (`…|import|export`) exempts the exact name `export_schedule` — and asserts it is the only tool whose name says "export"; the file-system-input test now walks every nested property and array item and adds `content`, `contents`, `bytes`, `blob` and `data` to the forbidden keys, with cases proving a `path`, `fileName`, `file_name`, `folder`, `content` or `bytes` beside `format` — or a `path` nested in an item — is caught; a new case pins `export_schedule`'s input to exactly `{format}` in the four formats, strict and closed, and its executor to never naming `saveExport`, `showSaveDialog`, `export:save` or `writeFile`. Every other assertion is unchanged; `exports.ts` stays the third and last writer, writing only where its own Save dialog returned. |
| **2026-09-28 — `color_by_schedule_column`: the heading menu's "Colour 3D by this column", from chat — one grouping, one apply path** | Owner-approved, in the words of the option they chose: *"Colour 3D from chat — 'Colour the model by this schedule's Fire Rating column' — uses the same colour-by the table's right-click menu already has."* **Shape:** view, strict, `{ column: string \| null }` (1–200 characters). **Same grouping:** the menu's steps — run the open schedule uncapped, group the column's *displayed* text by `colourKey`, refuse more than `MAX_COLOUR_GROUPS` (100) or a column with no value, the legend title the table's own heading (`full.headings[vi]`), the column key `fieldKey(field)` — moved out of `schedule-ui/selection.ts` and `main.ts` into one pure function, `colourColumn` in `src/schedule/colour.ts` (with `columnGroups`, `tooManyColours`, `colourKey`), which the Schedules window's menu now calls over its store and the executor over `scheduleStore()` — the same adapter snapshot through the same `StoreBuilder`. `fieldKey` moved to the engine's `def.ts` for it (the Schedules window's `panels/shared.ts` re-exports it, so none of its five importers changed). **Same apply path:** the port's `colourBy` branch became `colourByColumn(label, key, groups)` in `schedule-link.ts` — `admitGroups`, then the legend's own `setColorByGroups` (whose `colorByGroups` gives `SCHEME` colours, biggest group first), with the column key kept so the table draws its swatches — and the executor calls it too.
