| **2026-09-28 — the snap takes the surface being looked at: a hidden or cut-away candidate is never taken, the hit face's plane comes first** | The same request. The design (`viewer-core.js` L337–359) takes any crease vertex of the hit element within 16 px and any crease segment within 11 px, nearest in pixels, with no depth at all — so in plan a slab's soffit corner, on the top corner's pixel, can win, and a near-vertical edge seen from above gives whatever z its screen foot lands on. **Now** (`snap.ts`, `snapPoint(…, depth)`, still pure): `SnapDepth` carries the eye (`null` for the orthographic camera), the view direction, `tol` and the section plane, all read by `createSnapper` from the camera's `matrixWorld` — the matrix the pick ray and `toPixels` read — and from `SnapContext` (`scale: stageScale`, `clip: pickOptions.clip`). **Hidden:** the hit face is its plane (the hit normal through the hit point; with no normal, the plane square to the ray there); a candidate is hidden when it lies more than `tol` beyond that plane from the camera. Along the candidate's own view ray that is its depth against the surface's depth there, so one rule serves perspective (the camera's side is the eye's) and orthographic (the side its rays come from), and for a face square to the view it is the hit point's own depth. The plane, not the hit point's depth, is what keeps the design's corner snap on a receding face: on a wall 20 m off seen at 60° (1 000 px, 45° field), a corner 15 px along the same face is about 0.4 m deeper than the hit point, far past any tolerance. **Cut away:** the design's own `kept` (`n·p + c >= 0`, L334) on the candidate. **Order:** the design's rule — a corner inside 16 px beats an edge inside 11 px, nearest in pixels — is run over the candidates on the hit face's plane (`|n·(p − hit)| <= tol`) first, and only if none is inside its radius over the visible ones off it. **An edge** keeps the design's point, the foot, whenever that point is visible; when it is hidden or cut away the edge gives the nearest-to-camera point that is neither, inside its 11 px (the stretch inside the radius, narrowed to the kept side and to not-behind the face — both linear along the edge — and its end with the smaller view depth; the foot's side where the edge is square to the view). So a vertical edge seen from above gives its top end, and a hidden edge with no visible stretch gives nothing. **Interpretation:** the task said "for an edge, take the nearest-to-camera visible point among those within the radius". Taken for every edge, that moves every snap on a receding edge in perspective up to 11 px towards the camera — about 0.35 m along an edge 30 m off at 45° (1 000 px, 45° field) — although nothing is hidden, which the same task forbids ("the design's own numbers must hold wherever no hidden candidate is involved"); so the nearest-to-camera choice is made only when the foot is hidden or cut. **The tolerance:** `SNAP_PLANE_TOL` = **1 mm × scale** (`radius / 27.5`, as every scene constant is): 1.0 mm on the design's mock (scale 1.001), about 11 mm on the 137.9 MB reference model (its whole box is 454 × 429 m). It has only to absorb float32 placement noise, ~1e-7 of the model's extent (3 µm on the mock, ~60 µm at 500 m), and stay under the thickness of the thinnest element whose two faces read different levels — the candidates are the hit element's own, so only one element's two faces ever compete (a 20 mm finish, a 40 mm leaf, a 100 mm slab). **Unchanged:** the radii, the axis and perpendicular locks (they read the winner as before), the `S` toggle (off, the face point untouched), the marker, the 64-element cache. Without `depth` `snapPoint` is the design's rule, and a test reruns every design case under a depth view in which nothing is hidden and gets the same result, field for field. **Tested** (`tests/unit/snap.test.ts`, real three.js cameras through `overlay.ts`'s `toPixels`): a plan slab whose top and soffit corners share a pixel → the top, orthographic and perspective (the design takes the soffit); a soffit corner nearer in pixels than the top → the top; a column's vertical edge seen from above → never a point down it (the design: z 0.5–2.5 of 3), and on an edge alone its top end; a wall's back corner behind the face under the cursor → refused; an on-face corner over a nearer off-face one, an on-face edge over a nearer off-face corner, an off-face one still taken when alone; a cut-away corner and a cut-away edge foot. **On the mock** both parity clicks that changed (`spot-C1` at 760, 505 and `markups-card`'s at 620, 470) land on a window sill's top face (normal +z, z 8.4 and 4.9) of a 200 mm facade wall: the design took the wall's bottom-back corner, 900 mm below that face and behind the wall (replayed offline from the live camera and crease edges: `up = −0.900`), the rule takes the sill's back corner on the face (`up = 0`). So the spot reads `110.900` not `110.000` and the second measurement starts 0.9 m higher (`Y 9 025 · Z 2 700` → `8 865 · 1 800` mm); `measure-M1` is unchanged. |

| **2026-09-28 — a section outlines everything it cuts: one instanced fat-line mesh in the accent, 2.5 px, depth-tested** | Asked for by the owner in these words: *"Also highlight and thicken the line of all geometry that are cut in cut section."* Of the options offered they chose *"Accent colour, thick — SGVue's teal accent, about 2.5 px, so the cut stands out clearly from the rest of the model in both themes."*; not a solid poché fill, not drawing-style black / white. **Why fat lines:** WebGL's `linewidth` is always 1, so a `LineSegments` cannot be 2.5 px. three 0.186 ships the node-pipeline answer, `Line2NodeMaterial` (in `three/webgpu` itself), which expands each segment to a screen-space ribbon with round caps from an `InstancedBufferGeometry` carrying `instanceStart` / `instanceEnd`. `examples/jsm/lines/webgpu/LineSegments2.js` wraps it, but its `LineSegmentsGeometry` imports the classic `three` entry, so the eight-vertex template and the interleaved instance pair are written out in `section.ts` (`cutGeometry`, ~20 lines) and drawn as a plain `Mesh` — nothing in the material checks for `LineSegments2`. **One draw for every segment** (instanced), never one object per element; `frustumCulled = false` (the template's own bounds are a unit quad at the origin), `castShadow` off, not in the picker (which walks the batches only). **Width** `CUT_LINE_PX` = 2.5 CSS px, screen-constant (the material's pixel mode divides by the viewport over the device pixel ratio). **Colour** the theme's accent, set in `materials.setTheme` beside `selEdge` and the plane outline, so a theme switch recolours it with no recompute. **Depth:** opaque (the material does not blend), writes depth, and is **not** clip-gated — every segment lies on the plane, where the cut's own `>= 0` would keep or drop pixels by rounding. `polygonOffset` factor −1, units −4 (both backends honour it on triangle lists: `gl.polygonOffset`, and WebGPU's `depthBias` / `depthBiasSlopeScale`) pulls it a few depth steps toward the camera, so a face lying exactly in the plane (a slab top at a cut at its level, measured on the mock at L2 + 0) does not fight it; from the kept side the model's own faces hide it, and from below grade the camera sees none of it (checked). Render order 5: after the batches (0), so it depth-tests against every solid, and before the ground veil (10), so below grade it is dimmed with the parts it outlines — the mock's at-grade site slab reads at 40 %, as its faces do. Glass (a transparent family) blends over it later, so a cut seen through a window is tinted, never hidden. **Both backends:** the phase-6 captures on WebGPU (the mock only, `SGVUE_BACKEND=webgpu` + `SGVUE_ALLOW_WEBGPU=1`) draw it with 16 168 exact-accent pixels in `section-grid-C` against WebGL2's 16 243, no console error. **Disposal:** every recompute builds a new geometry, assigns it and disposes the old one (three's render object re-reads a changed geometry id); `dispose()` releases the last one and the mesh. **Parity** (the phase-6 chain on the mock, 1440 × 860, each theme its own run; HEAD captured twice for its own noise; this build captured with the outline drawn and then hidden in the same frame): `section-grid-C` 37 653 / 37 648 outline pixels (dark / light), and with it hidden the frame is HEAD's byte for byte; `section-grid-C-flip-offset1500` 1 129 / 1 129 (the camera is on the kept side, so only what shows through the glazing), and hidden it is HEAD's within HEAD's own run-to-run noise; `section-level-L2` 32 294 / 32 277, hidden the same, the light run's 2 525 px outside that noise all in the DOM label rows that move as much in `bubble-click-opens-section`, which cuts nothing. The preview, `bubble-click-opens-section`, `grids-iso`, `grids-plan`, `grids-north` and `levels-iso` compute no segment. **Frame cost:** render plus a 1-pixel readback (so the GPU finishes), median of 40, outline drawn vs hidden: 2.4–2.5 vs 2.4 ms on the 5.58 M-triangle synthetic model below, 1.4–1.5 vs 1.4 ms on the 2.23 M one — within noise. |
| **2026-09-28 — the cut is computed from the batch store's own triangles, on the main thread, at most once a frame** | **Where the triangles come from:** the slots the picker already walks — each slot's merged `position` (Float32, the scene frame, near the origin because the whole-metre offset was taken off in Float64 upstream) and `index`, and each part's `partRange` — so no second copy of the model is kept. `viewer/section-cut.ts` is pure (a type import from `batches.ts`, nothing else): `sectionCut(store, plane, include)` skips an element whose box does not reach the plane (`boxStraddles`, within `ON_PLANE_EPS`), skips every `IfcSpace`, asks `include`, then cuts each part's triangles in Float64. **Which elements:** `include` is `drawsEdge` — the grey edges' own rule: visible (hidden, isolated, filtered, a model or storey off all arrive as `rec.visible`), not inert (activate), not ghosted by highlight mode, not fading. Hover is deliberately not part of it: hovering a ghost brings it back to its colour for a moment, and its outline does not flicker in with it. **Glass is cut** — the glass family is clipped by the same plane (`familyify`), so a glazing panel or a curtain wall reads cut in section; a 6–24 mm pane's two lines merge at 2.5 px. **The rules at the plane** (`ON_PLANE_EPS` = 0.1 mm, a tolerance for float32 representation, not a model size): a triangle across the plane gives one piece; one with a vertex on the plane and the others on opposite sides, vertex to opposite edge; one touching at a vertex, nothing; **a triangle lying in the plane, nothing**; **an edge lying in the plane is drawn by the triangle whose third vertex is kept** — a surface crossing the plane along a mesh edge has one triangle each side and gives one segment, a solid resting on the plane from the kept side is outlined where it touches, one on the removed side is not. A crossing is always computed from its edge's lower-indexed vertex, so the two triangles sharing an edge land on the bitwise-same point. Pieces that meet at a shared vertex or edge crossing and run straight on (turn sine < 1e-4) are joined — a flat face is two or more triangles, so a box cut across is 4 segments, not 8; a bend (a faceted pipe) stays separate. A zero-length segment is never emitted. **Known, not handled:** two coincident faces of different parts (the mock's abutting wall boxes) each give their own segment in the same place — the same pixels twice; and a solid touching the plane only along a ridge from the kept side gives that ridge twice. **When:** `cutPlane` is set by the section host's `setClip` and cleared by its `clearClip` — `section.ts` calls `clearClip` first on every apply, and `setClip` only while cutting, so a preview and a cleared section have no plane — and every `rebuildEdges` marks the cut dirty (visibility, a fade landing, highlight, activate, a model added or removed, the batch-end rebuild). The frame loop recomputes **once, before it draws**, from whatever the plane is then: the Section card's offset typed fast or the assistant's `set_section` costs one recompute a frame and the last value wins (by construction — a flag the frame loop clears; in the app, twenty `setSection` calls in one task and the next frame shows the twentieth offset's cut). A theme switch is colour only. Every mark calls `invalidate()`; `frame-triggers.cjs` still passes 24 / 24 and draws nothing at rest. **Budget, measured in the built app** (dev build, guarded, WebGL2, RTX 3070 Ti; warm = median of 11): a synthetic model added through `viewer.addModel` — ten storeys of 48-sided columns on a 6 m grid, walls on every grid line with their faces subdivided 12 × 6, a subdivided slab, 32-sided pipes and 12 × 12 spheres — **2.23 M triangles** (7 040 elements): level cut 5.6 ms (11.7 cold), 206 544 triangles walked, 12 280 segments; grid cut 3.9 ms, 205 200 walked; a grid cut exactly on 540 walls' end faces 4.8 ms, 301 920 walked. The same at 25 storeys, **5.58 M triangles** (17 600 elements, 4.10 M vertices — about the 137.9 MB reference model's 5.57 M triangles a frame): level 5.6 ms (13.2 cold), grid 7.0 ms (513 000 walked, 9 200 segments), on-plane grid 8.9 ms (754 800 walked, 11.8 cold). The mock: 1.1–3.2 ms. So the recompute stays on the main thread — far under the ~50 ms that would have moved it — and is neither chunked nor in a worker; a worker would also need the triangles copied, since without cross-origin isolation there is no `SharedArrayBuffer`. **What it cost to get there** (Node, the same shapes): a triangle wholly on one side costs 6.7 ns; the join first used a `Map` keyed by a number above 2^31, which allocated a heap number per key and made the whole cut 48 ns a triangle, and an open-addressing table in typed arrays reused from part to part (a generation stamp empties it) brought it to 21 ns. **Tests** (`tests/unit/section-cut.test.ts`, 10): a box across and at an angle → a closed rectangle of 4; a vertex on the plane; a plane through two corners and along a face diagonal; an edge on the plane from a crossing surface (1) and from a box resting on it (4 kept, 0 removed); a coplanar triangle, and one within the tolerance → nothing; a faceted prism → 8; box culling; `include` leaving one element out; a space never cut; glass cut; and the mock's `Core Wall W L1` at L1 + 1.2 m → exactly its 250 × 3 500 mm rectangle, 4 segments. |
| **2026-09-28 — Help › Check for updates… opens the product site, told this version: `https://sgvue.github.io/?v=<version>`** | The owner's decision, in these words: *"From the next version, should Help › Check for updates open the new page instead of the GitHub releases list?"* → *"Yes, open the page"*. The site (published separately, not in this repository) reads `?v=` — strict semver, no leading `v` — and says "You're up to date" or "Update available", which the releases list could not: it left the user to compare version numbers by eye. **What:** `src/main/about.ts` keeps one hard-coded https base, renamed `RELEASES_URL` → **`SITE_URL`** (`https://sgvue.github.io/`), because it is no longer the releases page; `updatesUrl(version)` appends `?v=` and `encodeURIComponent(version)` — a pre-release's `-` and `.` pass unchanged, build metadata's `+` is encoded so the site reads it back rather than a space. `menu.ts`'s click is `shell.openExternal(updatesUrl(__APP_VERSION__))`. **Which version:** the build-time `__APP_VERSION__` (`package.json`'s, written in by `electron.vite.config.ts`), the one About and the landing page show — not `app.getVersion()`, which in a dev or e2e launch reports Electron's own (the About e2e asserts exactly that) and would send the site `?v=44.…`; in a packaged build the two are the same string. **Nothing else used `RELEASES_URL`**: About never showed it, and nothing in the app links the releases list any more. **Unchanged:** the item's label and place, `openExternal` (it hands the URL to the OS and never navigates the window, so the navigation lock is untouched), no auto-updater, **no network call by the app**, `publish: null`. No designed surface changes. **Tested:** `tests/unit/menu.test.ts` clicks the real item under three versions and pins the literal URL each time — `1.1.0` → `…/?v=1.1.0`, `1.2.0-beta.1` → `…/?v=1.2.0-beta.1`, `1.2.0-beta.1+build.5` → `…/?v=1.2.0-beta.1%2Bbuild.5`, each reading back as the version through `URLSearchParams` — and holds `SITE_URL` to https on `sgvue.github.io` with no query or hash of its own; the smoke e2e clicks the real item in the built app and expects `SITE_URL` + `?v=` + `package.json`'s version. *Amended 2026-10-01: "no network call by the app" held until the launch-time update check — below; the item itself still makes none, and the landing page's notice opens this same URL.* |

The toolbar has no room for them either (next row). **What:** those buttons, with the same copy, `data-tip`s, style strings and handlers, are `ActionBar` (`app/StatusBar.tsx`, `data-role="actionbar"`): the status bar's own card, 3 px above it — one style string, `BAR`, for both, so the two cannot drift — holding up to three groups (history, measures, spots) with the status bar's `·` between two that are both there and none in front of the first. It renders only while `hasActionBar` (`selectors/status.ts`: `canUndo`, `canRedo`, `measureCount > 0` or `spotCount > 0`), and it does not read `stats`, so a frame-rate report never re-renders it. `StatusBar` is the five fields and its markup, untouched; its string gave its three positioning declarations to the row (below). **The lane:** `App.tsx` sets `--abar` on the stage beside `--rlane` — `'30px'` while the bar is drawn, else `'0px'` (`selectors/lanes.ts`: `abar()`, `ACTION_LANE` = 30, the status bar's height, **measured at 27 px**, plus the 3 px the design leaves between the status bar and the legend). Three consumers: the colour legend's `bottom:calc(42px + var(--abar, 0px))` and its `max-height`, and `max-height:calc(100% - 110px - var(--abar, 0px))` on the Markups and Spatial-structure cards — the only left-lane cards that stop above the status bar. Filter, Section, Coordinate system and Viewpoints have no height limit to adjust; a tall one overlays the bars as it always has (z 14). **With the bar absent every computed length is the design's**, read back from the running app: `--abar:0px`, the legend's `bottom:42px` and `max-height:calc(100% - 126px)`, the cards' `calc(100% - 110px)`. **Measured** (mock, 1280 × 820; a storey hidden, 3 measures, 2 spots, colour by Level, Markups open; the same in both themes): status bar `[312, 781, 286.91, 27]`, action bar `[312, 751, 284.05, 27]`, reset pill `[698.63, 770.5, 182.73, 35.5]`, legend `[312, 543.91, 230, 204.09]` (computed `bottom:72px`), Markups `[312, 66, 330, 275]` — no two intersect, and the Spatial-structure card keeps the 5 px it stood off by (`[312, 66, 340, 680]`). At the real default window (1 264 × 755 inside) the two bars end 92 and 95 px left of the pill. The captures and the whole table are in `tests/parity/2026-10-01-ui/README.md`. **The bottom row** (the orchestrator's ruling on what that left, the same day: *fix the class, not the pixel*). With the bar at `bottom:42px` the design's centre lane was still its own — the hint bar and the reset pill both `bottom:14px; left:50%`, the pill drawn over the hint, neither knowing the bars or the Ask pill (`right:12px; bottom:14px`). Measured that way with the laser tool's hint (522 px on one line), a storey hidden and a full action bar: at 1280 × 820 the status bar over the hint's left 70 px, the action bar's bottom edge over 67 px of its top by 4.8 px, the pill over 183 px of its middle; at 900 × 700 and 760 × 700 six pairs of the five met, and the pill alone lay 90 px and 160 px across the status bar. A bar at `bottom:50px`, or a nudged hint, clears one pair at one window. **What:** `app/BottomRow.tsx`, mounted where the design's hint bar and status bar are — `position:absolute;left:12px;right:12px;bottom:12px;display:grid;grid-template-columns:1fr minmax(0,auto) 1fr;align-items:end;pointer-events:none` — with three zones: left (`justify-self:start`, a column 3 px apart) the action bar above the status bar; centre (`column-reverse`, 6 px apart, `margin-bottom:2px` — the design's `bottom:14px` against the row's 12) the hint and, above it, the reset pill; right (`justify-self:end`, the same 2 px) the Ask pill. A side track never goes below its `nowrap` content and the centre takes its content's width while there is room, so the pill alone is centred on the stage exactly as designed, a long hint is pushed right of the bars instead of under them, and only then does it wrap. **Every piece keeps its markup, copy, class, handlers and style string, less `position:absolute`, its offsets and the centring transform**, plus `pointer-events:auto`: the row takes none, so the canvas keeps its clicks between the zones, and a `data-tip` still shows — its `::after` is at z 40 in the stacking context it was in, because the row has no `z-index`. `z-index` 6 and 12 still apply, to flex items. `VisibilityFrame` is the border alone, where it was; `ResetPill` beside it asks the same selector. The hint's `white-space:nowrap` is `white-space:normal;text-align:center;text-wrap:balance;max-width:100%`. **What a plain grid did not do, measured:** at 760 × 700 the bars leave the centre 62.5 px; the pill (182.73 px, `nowrap`) overflowed its track on to the status bar and the Ask pill, and the laser hint wrapped into a 62.5 px column of fifteen lines. So the row measures its zones — in a layout effect on every render of it, so before the paint, and from a `ResizeObserver` when the window or the status bar's own text changes a width — and asks one pure function, `centrePlace(row, left, right, need)` (`selectors/lanes.ts`): `between` the side zones, with `BOTTOM_GAP` = 10 px on either side, while they leave at least what the centre needs — the pill's own width, or `HINT_MIN` = 200 px for a hint, at which the longest is three lines — else `above` them, on a row of its own across the stage, 6 px clear; `empty` keeps no gaps, so with nothing in the centre the status bar and the Ask pill stand at the design's offsets down to a 678 px window, as they did. Every argument is a width that does not depend on the answer, so the layout cannot flip back and forth. **One departure from the ruling's own string:** between the side zones the centre is `align-items:flex-start`, not `center`. Centred over the 522 px hint, 39 px of the pill — its `reset` button — are under the open chat panel at the default window, and 29 px under a full-height property card: the panel stops 52 px above the stage's foot and the card 56 px, room for the row's one line and not for a second level. Over the hint's start the pill is 130 px clear of the panel. Alone, either piece is the zone's whole width, so the alignment moves nothing else. **Tab order:** the row is one place in the DOM, so `reset` (which followed the view cube, before the property card) and `Ask` (which followed the chat panel) are now the stage's last two stops, after the action bar's buttons. `tests/e2e/keyboard.spec.ts` pinned neither. **Measured** (mock; dark and light alike; `[x, y, w, h]`). At rest, against a build of `d16f393` driven the same way: the status bar (`[312, 781, 286.91, 27]`, `[312, 716, 286.91, 27]`) and the Ask pill (`[1201.42, 774, 66.58, 32]`, `[1185.42, 709, 66.58, 32]`) have the same rectangles at 1280 × 820 and at the real default 1264 × 755, and their regions differ by **0 px**. The pill alone is `[698.63, 770.5, 182.73, 35.5]` and `[690.63, 705.5, 182.73, 35.5]`, the rectangles it has on `d16f393`; inside them 571 / 452 px (dark / light) and 589 / 455 px differ, by at most 37 / 93 of 255 in a channel, every one in the rounded ends of the pill and of its `reset` button — the two outlines are drawn at a layout position and no longer through `translateX(-50%)`; the label and the word `reset` are pixel-identical. The laser state, all five on screen: 1280 × 820 — action bar `[312, 751, 284.05, 27]`, hint `[608.91, 773.2, 522.25, 32.8]` (10 px right of the status bar, on the design's own line), pill `[608.91, 731.7, 182.73, 35.5]`; 1264 × 755 — `[608.91, 708.2, 522.25, 32.8]` and `[608.91, 666.7, …]`; 900 × 700 — the hint on three lines, `[608.91, 619.61, 202.52, 66.39]`, the pill `[608.91, 578.11, …]`; 760 × 700 — the centre above the bars, the hint on two lines `[312, 575.41, 436, 49.59]`, the pill `[438.63, 533.91, …]`. **No two of the five intersect at any of the four sizes** — in that state, at rest, with the pill alone, or with the spot tool's 360 px hint, which alone at 1280 × 820 is still on the stage's centre line (`[609.84, …]`; 609.85 before). The status bar, the action bar and the Ask pill have, in every one of those states and sizes, the rectangle they had before the row. **Not solved, and recorded for the owner:** the chat panel, the property card and the colour legend are not zones of the row, and no lane is declared for its height. With an element selected, the chat panel open, a tool hint and something hidden — all four at once — the chat panel is in the card's lane (`[590, 283, 330, 420]` at 1264 × 755) and covers the whole pill above the hint; in a window of 900 px or less the open chat panel covers the pill and the top 28 px of a wrapped hint, and 38 px of a full action bar as it did before the row; and where the centre stands above the bars (760 px) it is where a colour legend is. A second declared lane — the row's measured height, read by the chat panel, the card and the legend the way `--abar` is read — would clear all three; that is a decision, and it is not built. Below a 678 px window with the sidebar open the status bar and the Ask pill do not fit side by side: the design overlapped them, the row lets the Ask pill run past the stage's edge (5.5 px at 660). **Tests:** `hasActionBar`, `abar` and `ACTION_LANE` in `tests/unit/selectors.test.ts`; the smoke e2e walks the bar on the demo building — five fields and no button in the status bar, no leading dot, the lane, the 3 px gap, no overlap with the pill, the Markups card's `max-height`, undo and redo, both `clear`s. `centrePlace`, `BOTTOM_GAP` and `HINT_MIN` are beside them in the unit file, and a second e2e case walks the row: at rest (the two design offsets, and a sweep of the gap between the zones that finds nothing of the row under the pointer), the pill alone (centred, at `bottom:14px`, a `data-tip` showing on hover), the laser state (the five pairwise clear, the hint 10 px right of the bars on one line, the pill 6 px above it, neither bar nor the Ask pill moved), then 900 × 700, 760 × 700 and back, and 690 × 700 with the centre empty. |
| **2026-10-01 — the toolbar is five groups with a divider between each two, Schedules alone and last; its width is a constraint, so the dividers were paid for out of the gaps** | Asked for by the owner in these words: *"Can you add organize, group and add divider for the toggles bar on the top of 3D view? Please separate the schedules toggle. Make it distinct from others."* **The groups**, in order: tools (Select, Laser meter, Spot coordinate, Snap) · panels (Section, Filter, Coordinate system) · show (Gridlines, Levels, Canvas grid, Shadows, Light / dark) · view (Saved viewpoints, 3D Plan N S E W, persp / ortho) · Schedules. Every button keeps its own markup, class, `data-tip`, handler and on / off colours; Shadows and the theme button left the view group for show, so they sit 2 px apart like the other icon buttons rather than 6. **The dividers** are `<span aria-hidden="true">`, 1 × 18 px, `flex:none`, `background:var(--border-strong)`, direct children of the toolbar between the group boxes. **Why the design had none, and the answer to it:** dividers strand on wrapped rows (`BUILD_PLAN.md` §5 "Toolbar"). So a divider whose previous and next siblings have different `offsetTop`s — it would end one row or start the next — is set `visibility:hidden` in a layout effect, re-run by a `ResizeObserver` on the toolbar and when the persp / ortho label changes. Never `display:none`: that changes the very layout the rule was read from and can oscillate; `visibility` costs a wrapped row 2.5 px of centring and nothing else. A group that wraps inside itself is alone on its row (flex line breaking), so comparing `offsetTop`s of two groups is exact. **Why the gap is 4 px:** at the default window the toolbar ends inside the view cube's 148 px canvas, over a corner where the cube draws nothing (2026-09-24's canvas-grid row), so it may not grow. Five groups are 126 + 94 + 158 + 279.03 + 30; with four 1 px dividers and `column-gap:4px` (eight gaps) the toolbar is **737.03 px**, against 741.03 before (four groups, three 10 px gaps). 5 px gaps would have made it 745.03 px — the task's limit of +4 exactly, with its right end 2 px nearer the cube than before instead of 2 px further. **Schedules** is the same 30 × 30 `hv-step` button with its icon and `data-tip="schedules"`, in `color:var(--accent-ink);border:1px solid var(--accent)` (the box is `border-box`, so it is still 30 px): it opens another window and is not a toggle, so it never takes the filled `--sel-bg` "on" look its neighbours use. **Measured against the cube** by asking the cube itself, one probe per pixel (`viewer/cube.ts` takes a mouse's events only where its zones, compass ring, north kite or `N` are drawn): at 1280 × 820 the Schedules button `[1121.52, 18, 30, 30]` has 0 cube pixels under it in the 3D view and in Plan, the toolbar's whole box 0 (before: 2 and 4), and the nearest drawn pixel is 7.43 / 8.83 px from the button (the theme button, last before: 6.49 / 7.14). At the real default window, 1 264 × 755 inside: 0 under the button in both views, 17 / 30 under the toolbar's bottom-right padding (before 25 / 45), nearest 4.27 / 2.49 px (before 3.90 / 1.11). That is the mock, whose true north is 0°; a file with a rotated true north, or an orbited view, can put the ring or the `N` under the toolbar's right end as it could before, 2 px further from it now. **Wrapping:** 900 × 700 and 760 × 700 give tools · panels · show / view · Schedules with the divider after show hidden; 640 × 700 gives four rows with three hidden. `cardTop` still follows the toolbar's measured height (`App.tsx`'s own observer; the e2e opens the Filter card under a wrapped toolbar). **Consequences elsewhere:** the button under the cube canvas's empty corner is Schedules now, not Light / dark — a mouse reaches it through the canvas's pass-through, a touch does not (Window › Schedules does); `tests/e2e/helpers.ts` gains `handClick` ("the pointer arrives, then clicks") and `openSchedulesWindow`, which `schedules.spec.ts` opens the window with, and the theme toggle is an ordinary click again. `openSchedulesWindow` makes the click again if no window came: one run of the new toolbar test in sixteen lost that click, straight after a window resize. The canvas steps aside when the pointer arrives and takes its events back on a pointer move anywhere else, and under test the page has a second pointer Playwright does not drive — the desktop's own cursor; that a move from it did this is **inferred, not observed** (no window opened, and nothing else gives the canvas its events back). A person has one pointer, so nothing in the app is retried. **Tests:** a smoke e2e reads the five groups and four dividers in order, one row, ≤ 745 px (737), the Schedules outline, the Tab order against the drawn order, 0 cube pixels under the button in 3D and Plan, the wrap rule at 900 × 700 and back, and opens the Schedules window with a hand's click. |
| **2026-10-01 — the property card's clipped texts carry their whole text as a native `title`** | Asked for by the owner in these words: *"Sometimes on the properties panel, the information on Predefinetype, objectype are too long and got cut off, can you do a hover and show full?"* Every text the card clips with `text-overflow:ellipsis` gets one: the four identity tiles' values (IfcEntity, PredefinedType, ObjectType, Level), each property set's header and each property's name. The header's title is the set's **own** name (`Pset_WallCommon`), which the card otherwise never shows — it draws the prettified `Wall Common`; a property's is the name as drawn. **One rule, pure:** `fullText` in `selectors/props.ts` — the text, or `undefined` for a blank and for `DASH`, because a tooltip on a placeholder is one more placeholder. **Native `title`, not `data-tip`:** the design's `[data-tip]::after` tooltip is positioned inside the element, and the card's body is `overflow:auto`, which would clip it; a native tooltip is the OS's and cannot be clipped. **No style string changed**, so nothing drawn moves: the card's rectangle differs from the capture before by 0 px (dark) and 3 px (light), which is exactly what two captures of one build differ by (0 and 3). **Tests:** `fullText` in `tests/unit/props.test.ts`; the smoke e2e selects a tree on the demo building (`IfcGeographicElement`, which its tile draws as `IfcGeographicEl…`) and reads the tiles, the three set headers and `Species Botanical Name` back, then asserts that no clipped span in the card is left without a title. |
| **2026-10-01 — the element tree is eye-first: the eye leads a row and the chevron trails it** | Asked for by the owner in these words: *"Can you swap the collapse expand icon and visibility eye icon on the element by entity? so it all consistent with the storeys."* A group row was `[chevron 14px][label][count][eye 24px]` (`SGVue.dc.html:179`) and is `[eye 24px][label][count][chevron 14px]`; an element row was `[name / meta][eye]` (`:191`) and is `[eye][name / meta]`, so the whole tree reads the way MODELS and STOREYS do. Both tree modes share the markup. **Only the order changed:** each piece keeps its markup, class and handler — the eye its `stopPropagation`, the chevron its rotation. **Padding:** the group row keeps `5px 4px 5px 6px`, because its 6 px on the tree's 10 px is exactly the storey row's 2 px edge + 4 px on the same 10 — so the group eye is on the storey eye's own x with nothing to adjust; the element row's side padding is mirrored with its columns (`3px 8px 3px 2px`), so the eye keeps its 2 px from the row's edge (now the selection edge) and the text its 8 px from the other, and the selection edge and backgrounds are untouched. **Measured** (mock, 1280 × 820): storey eye x 16, group eye x **16** (it was 251, at the row's right end), element eye x **31** (was 253), MODELS eye x 14 (the design's own, unchanged); the group row is still 269 × 34 px and an element row 252 × 36.89 px. **Unchanged:** `role="tree"`, the roving tabindex, `data-tree-key`, `aria-*`, `onTreeKey` (an eye is still a button whose Enter and Space are its own) and the row windowing, which measures the group's second child — still a row. A group row's `children[1]` and `children[2]` are still its label and count, which `scripts/shell-sanity.cjs` and `scripts/profile-frame.cjs` index. **Tests:** `tests/unit/tree-keys.test.ts` and the keyboard e2e pass unchanged; the smoke e2e reads each row's children in order, the two x's, and that the eye hides a group without opening it. |
| **2026-10-01 — a model's file path is its hover title: the library row, the Recent pill, the loaded model's name block** | Asked for by the owner in these words: *"The model library, can you show file path on the hover for each model?"* A native `title` carrying the full path on (a) each row of the sidebar's `library` popover and (b) each Recent pill of the landing page — both `LibraryFile.path`, which `libraryOf` fills from the recents list — and (c) a loaded model's row in MODELS, on its name / file text block only, so the row's buttons keep their own titles. **Where (c) comes from:** the renderer already keeps each loaded model's file in the federation controller (`federation-store.ts`, `files`, read through `sessionFiles()`, which is what the session and the share link record). `Sidebar` passes it to `modelRows` as `files`, and `ModelRow.path` is that model's path, `''` when it has none. It is read inside the memo keyed on `federation`: a file is recorded when its model joins, before the commit that changes `federation`, and removed with it. **No IPC, no store key, nothing new held.** **Never a placeholder:** a model with no file behind it — the demo building, the `#mock` federation — has `''`, and `title={md.path \|\| undefined}` writes no attribute. A file dropped from somewhere main would not admit keeps the spelling the drop gave it — or none, and then no title. Nothing drawn changes: the sidebar above the tree is 0 px different in all twelve before / after captures. **Tests:** `ModelRow.path` by model key, the no-file case and the popover's entries in `tests/unit/selectors.test.ts`, on synthetic paths (`C:\Models\Tower\Arch.ifc`); the smoke e2e opens the committed fixture and compares the three titles with the path the session recorded — read back at run time, so no path of any machine is in the repository — and checks the demo's rows carry none. |
| **2026-10-01 — the ground stands at the project frame's own zero, not at the height of the first placement streamed** | Reported by the owner in these words: *"I have an ifc model generated by Tekla. When federated model, it was aligned with my model and above canvas grid. But when i open it individually, the model is under the canvas grid. what could be the issue?"* **The cause:** the scene is the project frame less a whole-metre federation offset, and the offset — Z included — is rounded from the first placement web-ifc streams from the boot model (`worker/geometry-streamer.ts`, `offset ??= [round(x), round(y), round(z)]`). web-ifc streams by IFC class and then in file order, and puts each geometry's own centre in its placement, so "the first placement" is the middle of whichever product of the first class it walks comes first in the file. `viewer/scene.ts` drew the ground at scene z = 0, clamped into the box (`min(max(0, min z), max z)`), and called that "the federation datum" — which it is only when that first product happens to sit within half a metre of the file's zero. That held for the Revit exports this was built on and not for a Tekla steel-structure export, whose first part streamed was a member near the top: opened alone, its ground stood at **98 %** of the model's height with **92 %** of its elements wholly under it (and so, since 2026-09-24, dimmed under the veil); federated behind another model, that model's offset was used and the same file stood on the ground. **The offset is right and is not changed** — sessions, share links and viewpoints store cameras in scene coordinates, and every readout adds it back. **The rule:** the ground stands at the project frame's own zero — in scene coordinates `0 − offset z` — whenever the model's z range spans it, and otherwise at the **bottom** of the range; never at its top. It is one pure function, `groundLevel(datumZ, minZ, maxZ)` (`scene.ts`); `buildScene` takes the datum as its last argument, and `viewer-core.ts` builds the rig through one helper, `buildRig`, at construction and on every `restage`, so the two cannot disagree; a theme change rebuilds only the grid helper, at the same captured height. Everything that reads `rig.groundZ` follows by itself: the ground and its veil, the grid helper, the gridlines with their bubbles and dimensions (`annotations.ts`), and the orbit / dolly fallback pivot (`input.ts`). Nothing else read scene 0 as the ground (searched: `viewer/`, `shared/`, the model and state modules, the assistant's executors) — the level rings and the section planes use the storeys' own heights, and the spot tag and every readout add the offset. `debug()` reports `ground`. **What else moves:** a model wholly *below* its datum used to get the ground on its top (the clamp's upper end) and now gets it under its bottom; an empty box still reads −∞ — nothing is loaded and nothing is drawn — as the clamp did; and any file whose first placement is a metre or more from its zero has its ground moved to that zero — the reference model's by one metre, to its lowest storey's `+0` (its offset Z is −1, `tests/unit/annotate.test.ts`; not re-measured, there is no sample model on this machine). **Measured.** *The design's mock* (offset Z 0, box −1 … 15.5, ground 0 before and after): the `default` capture at 1280 × 820 is **byte-identical** to `2893aff`'s in both themes — of five dark captures of the fixed build four are byte-identical to one of `2893aff`'s, as are both light ones, and the fifth differs by about 8 100 px, all of them outlines of the overlay's bubbles and dimension labels, where five captures of `2893aff` itself differ from each other by 0–6 342 px of the same; with the stage's chrome and labels hidden, the boot view and Plan are byte-identical in both themes and the south elevation within 2 px (two captures of `2893aff`: 0, 0 and 2 px). *The committed fixture* (below): offset `[3, 0, 12]`, box −13 … 0.2 in scene coordinates; the ground at scene 0 before — the roof, 98.5 % up, four of the five elements under it — and at scene −12 after, the file's zero, with only the footing below. *The Tekla export*, through the app's own index builder and geometry streamer under Node (relative figures only; nothing of the file is recorded): first placement at 98 % of the model's height; the ground there before, with 92 % of the elements wholly below it; at the file's zero after, 42 % up the box, with 99.9 % of the elements wholly above it and none across it. It was not captured in the app: the one guarded run that loaded it tripped the dev guard (below) and was not repeated. **The fixture:** `scripts/make-tiny-ifc.py` writes a second deterministic file, `tests/fixtures/high-first.ifc` — a floor slab on the file's zero, two columns, a footing below zero and a strip of roof slab 12 m up, the roof written first. web-ifc streams `IfcSlab` before `IfcColumn`, `IfcFooting` and `IfcBeam` whatever their ids (probed: a beam written first is streamed last), so the high part had to be a slab, and the unit test pins the premise (first part `IfcSlab`, offset `[3, 0, 12]`). `tiny.ifc` regenerates byte-identical. **Tests:** `tests/unit/ground-level.test.ts` — the rule (spans, wholly above, wholly below, flat, empty, and equal to the old clamp wherever the box reaches zero or stands above it), `buildScene` with a non-zero datum (the ground, the veil and the helper, through a theme change, and scene 0 for the mock's box) and the real streamer on the fixture; the smoke e2e opens the fixture in the built app and, in Plan, toggles the canvas grid — the middle of the slab does not change, because the grid is under it, while the viewport does. On `2893aff` that middle changed by 576 px. The suite runs the production bundle, which has no `debug()`, so the ground is read off the canvas. **Not changed, and a limit:** `restage` still rebuilds the rig only when the scene scale moves by about 10 %, so on a federation whose box does *not* span its datum, a model added later that lowers the box's bottom by less than that leaves the ground where the first build put it — as it did before. **The guard trip:** loading the Tekla export in a guarded dev run (`safe-run.cjs`, default limits) read the GPU process at 242 MB when the load returned and 2 634 MB 1.7 s later, over the dev guard's 2 500 MB (renderer 2 121 MB); the run was ended by the guard, nothing survived, and it was not re-run with a raised limit. The app's own guard allows `max(3 072 MB, 40 % of RAM)`. **Also fixed on the way:** `scripts/shell-sanity.cjs` had not parsed since `5d942e1` — a comment inside the `BUBBLES` template literal quoted `declutter.after` in unescaped backticks, which ended the template; the two are escaped, as the line above it already was, and the script runs to its end on the mock again (every block, 92 s). |
| **2026-10-01 — the landing page says when a newer version is out, and the app asks GitHub for that once at every start — its one network call** | Asked for by the owner in these words: *"Can you show updates with link if there are one automatically on the upload page?"* ("upload page" is the landing page.) Asked how — *"Showing an update notice automatically means SGVue must contact the internet once each time it starts (it asks GitHub for the newest version number; nothing about you or your models is sent). Until now the app made no such call. How should it work?"* — the owner chose **"Check at every start"**: *"One small request to GitHub when the app opens. If a newer version exists, the home page shows 'Update available' with a link to the download page. Offline or blocked: it stays silent."* The variant with a Preferences switch was offered and not chosen, so there is no switch. **What this reverses:** 2026-09-25 and 2026-09-28 both recorded "no network call by the app … no background check". That is no longer true of the app; it is still true of the menu item. What those rows refused is still refused: there is **no auto-updater and no download**, `publish: null` stays, nothing new is written to disk, and the private source repository is never read — the request goes to the *public* releases repository the installers are published in. **The request** (`src/main/updates.ts`, main only): one `GET` to `LATEST_RELEASE_URL` — `https://api.github.com/repos/sgvue/releases/releases/latest`, a hard-coded constant that nothing builds or extends — through Electron's `net.fetch`, with `Accept: application/vnd.github+json`, `credentials: 'omit'`, `cache: 'no-store'`, `redirect: 'error'` and `AbortSignal.timeout(5000)`. No query string, no body, no cookie and no other header of ours: nothing about the user, the machine's files or the models. It is a web request, so GitHub sees what every server sees — the IP address and Chromium's standard headers (a user agent naming the engine, Electron and the OS, and in a packaged Electron app by default the app's name and version). **The answer:** the status must be 200; the body is read through its stream and abandoned the moment it passes 256 kB (GitHub's is about 5 kB), so a broken or hostile answer is never buffered whole; `JSON.parse`; `tag_name` must be a string `parseVersion` accepts, and is kept without its leading `v`. Everything else — offline, a proxy's page, a rate limit (403), a redirect, a timeout, an oversized or malformed answer, a tag that is not a version — is `null`, never a throw, with at most one `console.warn` line (the reason flattened to one line and cut at 200 characters, since a parse error quotes the answer it could not read). **The comparison** (`src/shared/version.ts`, pure): `parseVersion` reads `1.2.3` or `v1.2.3` with an optional pre-release and optional build metadata; it refuses anything over 64 characters before a pattern is run, a leading zero, and a number too large to compare exactly, and its one pattern has no ambiguous repetition. `isNewer` is semver precedence — numbers as numbers, a pre-release older than its release, numeric identifiers below alphanumeric ones and compared exactly at any length, the longer identifier list newer on a tie, build metadata ignored — and `false` when either side cannot be read. The current version is the build-time `__APP_VERSION__`, the one About and the landing page show, never `app.getVersion()`. **Once per run:** `checkForUpdate` keeps its promise, so a reloaded window asks again and no second request goes. **Never in a development build** — the `app.isPackaged` gate `SGVUE_OPEN_PATHS` uses: there no request is made, and `SGVUE_UPDATE_LATEST`, when set, stands in for GitHub's `tag_name` through the same validation and comparison, which is how the unit tests and the e2e suite see a notice without a network. A packaged build ignores the variable. **IPC:** two channels, in `IPC_CHANNELS`, both taking no payload (`z.undefined()`) and both answered for the main window's `webContents` only, as `schedules:open` is: `update:check` → `{ latest } \| null`, and `update:open`, on which main calls `shell.openExternal(updatesUrl(__APP_VERSION__))` — the page Help › Check for updates… opens. **The renderer never supplies a URL**, and learns nothing but a version string that has passed `parseVersion`. Two preload keys, `checkUpdate` and `openUpdatePage`; the Schedules preload has neither. **The notice** (`app/Landing.tsx`): asked once, when the page mounts, and kept in component state; with no bridge, no answer, a refusal or `null`, nothing is rendered. With an answer, one box in the 620 px column between the introduction and the drop zone, above the warning banner when both show — the designed warning banner's box in the accent tokens, `display:flex;align-items:center;gap:10px;padding:12px 14px;background:var(--sel-bg);border:1px solid var(--accent);border-radius:10px` — holding the text `SGVue <latest> is available — you have <current>.` in `--sel-ink` at 12.5 px and the page's own text button, `get the update →`, in `--accent-ink`. `data-role="update-notice"`; no icon and no dismiss. **Measured** (`tests/parity/2026-10-01-update-notice/README.md`; `#mock&landing`, 1280 × 820, both themes): with nothing newer the landing capture is byte-identical to `2893aff`'s (a pair in each theme; two captures of one build differ by up to 19 px, each by one level) and the page's `outerHTML` is the same 7 922 bytes; the notice is `[330, 241.2, 620, 44.75]`, 22 px under the introduction and 22 px over the drop zone. The column is centred, so it grows by 66.75 px about its middle: what is above the notice stands 33.4 px higher and what is below 33.4 px lower, as with the designed banner. At the real default window (1 264 × 755 inside) with two rows of Recent pills the page becomes 789 px tall and scrolls by 34 px. **Live:** the app's own `latestVersion()`, bundled alone and run once in a guarded Electron main process with the default `net.fetch`, was answered 200 and returned `1.1.0`; that first request took 2.8 s on this machine, inside the 5 s limit. **Tests:** `tests/unit/version.test.ts` (the grammar, the refusals, the cap and a megabyte refused on its length, the precedence table and semver's own example chain); `tests/unit/updates.test.ts` (every fetch injected or mocked: the URL pinned to https on `api.github.com` with no query, the request's exact `init`, seventeen bad answers, four failed requests, a reason with line breaks and control characters kept to one line, the 256 kB cap with an endless body cancelled, newer / same / older / pre-release, one request for three calls, none after a failure, the variable ignored when packaged, no request and the stand-in when not); the guard test pins the two preload keys, that neither takes an argument, that only `main/updates.ts` names the host, and each handler's parse, sender check and `updatesUrl`; the smoke e2e launches with `SGVUE_UPDATE_LATEST=99.0.0` and reads the copy, the box, the tokens in both themes and the URL the button opens (`shell.openExternal` replaced by a recorder), then launches with this version, an older one and nothing and finds no notice once the answer is in, then with a broken link and finds the notice above the banner. **Not tested here:** a packaged build's launch request end to end — `dist/` holds the 1.1.0 package, built before this. Once repackaged, `npm run test:packaged` makes that one request per launch, and its landing page shows the notice if a release newer than the build is out. |
| **2026-10-01 — two section planes, the gridline cut and the level cut: independent in the state, the card and the viewer** | Asked for by the owner in these words: *"Can you add the section cut along gridline as another cut also? same, i need offset, cut / flip, clear. and since we have multi plane section cut now, add a clear all somewhere. Can you also add divider on the gridlines under section panel for along x or y side, for example gridline 1 to 10 one section, Gridline A to E one section."* Asked *"Section cuts: how many should be possible at the same time?"*, the owner chose **"Two: gridline + level"** — *"The gridline cut and the level cut become independent. Each has its own offset, cut, flip side and Clear, plus one Clear all. The gridline chips are split by a divider into their two directions (A-E / 1-10)."* **Before:** one plane — `section: { kind: 'grid' \| 'level' \| null, name, offset, flip, cut }`, one clip uniform pair, and picking a level chip replaced a gridline cut. **State:** the shapes live in `src/shared/sections.ts`, because the codec, the selectors, the assistant's view state and the store all read them: `SecPlane = { name, offset, flip, cut }` — `name: ''` is "no plane", the offset is millimetres as the card has it — `Sections = { grid, level }`, the frozen `NO_PLANE` / `NO_SECTIONS`, and `sectionsLabel`, the one function that prints what is on (`grid C`, `level L2`, `grid C + level L2`, `null`). The store key `sections` replaces `section`. `setSec(kind, patch, open?)` spreads the patch over that plane and leaves the other object untouched; `clearSections()` writes `NO_SECTIONS`; `gridClick(name)` is the design's `:895` rule on the gridline plane alone (the bubble of the grid already sectioned clears it, any other previews it at offset 0 with `cut` off and opens the card) and never reads or writes the level plane. The chip rule (`chipPatch`, `:1875`) is the design's, per plane: the live chip gives `{ name: '' }`, another `{ name, offset: 0 (gridline) \| 1200 (level), cut: true }`, so `flip` is kept; a block's `Clear` writes `NO_PLANE`, the four values the single card's `Clear` wrote. Millimetres still become metres in one place, `sectionConfigOf(kind, plane)`, now once per plane. **The card** (`app/SectionCard.tsx`, the owner's own layout): the header gains `Clear all` left of the ×, in the `Clear` button's own style string; "Along a gridline" is its chips grouped by the file's own grid family (`sectionGridFamilies`: consecutive grids of one `GridAxisRecord.family` are one wrapping row — the federation's list is already family-first — with a 1 px `var(--border)` rule, `aria-hidden`, between two rows and none for one family), then the design's offset row, the plane's summary and its three buttons for the gridline plane; "At a level" is the level chips and the same for the level plane. The pieces are the design's markup and style strings, once per plane; a block is `display:flex;flex-direction:column;gap:6px` — the design's own block style — so the rows sit 6 px apart inside a block and blocks 12 px apart, and each block is a `role="group"` named by its label, which is how the tests and the dev scripts reach one plane's controls. **The summary row is the one place the design's two rows were re-arranged.** The design puts the summary and `cut` · `flip side` · `Clear` on one line (`:594–601`), where the three buttons leave the summary about 75 px of the 300 px card: it read `offset mm …`, the plane's name clipped away — on the single card too, and in the first capture of this change — and with two planes that line is the only place a plane's name, `cut` and `flipped` are read at all. So the summary is a line of its own (its own style string, `:595`, so it still ellipsises if it ever overflows; a native `title` with its whole text) and the three buttons are a row of their own after it, right-aligned (`display:flex;justify-content:flex-end;gap:6px`), each in its own style string, in the design's order. With no plane chosen a block's controls stay and its summary reads `offset mm · no section`. The card is 114 px taller on the mock (330 → 444), so it takes the Markups card's `max-height:calc(100% - 110px - var(--abar, 0px));overflow:auto`. The toolbar's Section button is lit while the card is open or either plane has a name. **The viewer** (`viewer/section.ts`, rewritten around two slots, `SECTION_SLOTS = ['grid', 'level']`): `Viewer.setSections({ grid, level })`, each a `SectionConfig \| null` with the old `kind: 'grid' \| 'storey'`, replaces `setSection`. Each slot owns one outline quad and one preview sheet (neither clip-gated, so each stays whole where the other plane cuts) and computes its plane with the same `planeFromSegment` / `planeFromLevel`; the host is told every slot's cutting plane in one call, `setClips([grid \| null, level \| null])` — `null` for a slot that is absent, previewing, or names a grid or storey the federation does not have. **The clip is the intersection of the kept sides:** `materials.ts` has a second uniform pair and `clipKeep` is `dot(p, n₁) + c₁ ≥ 0 AND dot(p, n₂) + c₂ ≥ 0`, on the same `opacityNode` + `alphaTest` path with the same cap shading; a slot that is not cutting is parked at the plane that keeps everything (`n = +z`, `c = 100 000`), so with one plane cutting each fragment is kept or dropped exactly as the single test decided. **Rays:** `PickerOptions.clip()` returns the planes that are cutting — none, one or two — and `clipSpan` intersects their `clipInterval`s; `pick` and `ray` (hover, select, the laser, the snap's face) are clamped to that span before the walk, inside `picking.ts`, so the brute-force reference shares it. **`anyHit`, the label-occlusion ray, is not clamped** — the brief for this change listed it with the other two, but it was never clamped by the one plane (`viewer-core.js` L720–721 tests the raw pick list, and the port kept that), and clamping it now would change which bubbles and tags are hidden in every one-plane frame, which the same brief's regression rule forbids; it is as it was. **Snap:** `SnapDepth.clips` is the same list — a candidate on the cut-away side of any plane is never taken, and an edge whose foot is hidden is narrowed to what every plane keeps (`keepWhere`, once per plane) before its nearest-to-camera point is taken; the rest of 2026-09-28's depth rule is unchanged. **Re-aim** is the design's L253 rule, per slot: a slot moved when it is cutting and it had no previous config, or its previous one was previewing, or its kind, name or flip differ. The first slot that moved, in slot order, is the one aimed at — so a restore that brings both re-aims once, at the gridline plane — and an offset, a clear, or anything the other slot does never re-aims; `refresh()` (the box or the grid set changed) passes the current config as its own predecessor, so nothing moved. One behaviour kept from the design and worth knowing: a gridline plane keeps the side holding the model's centre, so an offset that carries it past the centre swaps the kept side without a re-aim, exactly as with one plane. **Every path repaints** the way it did: `setSections` → `apply` → `host.setClips` → `markCut()` + `invalidate()`. **Dev surface:** `debug().section` is `{ grid, level }`; `debug().plane` became `debug().planes`, both uniform pairs; the dev hook `setSection(kind, name, cut)` sets that kind's plane beside whatever the other is, and `setSection(null)` clears both; the parity chain clears with `secClear()` — the card's `Clear all` on the app, `Clear` on the prototype — and `screenshot.cjs`, `frame-triggers.cjs` and `shell-sanity.cjs` reach a plane's controls through its block. **Measured — one plane is the build before** (the phase-6 chain on the mock, 1440 × 860, each theme its own run; HEAD `60ec62b` built with dev tools into a scratch directory and captured three times, plus six further light captures of the first four states; this build captured three times). *Canvas only* (every sibling of the viewport hidden, 1140 × 860): **every one of the 84 frames (14 states × 2 themes × 3 runs) is identical, pixel for pixel, to a HEAD capture.** HEAD's own captures are one frame per state except `levels-iso`, which comes in two — one pixel at (775, 823), green 104 or 105 — in both themes (1 of 3 dark captures, 2 of 9 light); each of this build's is one of the two. The cut statistics and draw counts are HEAD's in every state (`section-grid-C` 220 segments on 47 elements from 960 triangles, `section-grid-C-flip-offset1500` 248 / 46 / 2 052, `section-level-L2` 568 / 74 / 3 360). *Whole window:* the label readback — text and box of every label — is identical in all 28 state / themes; the card is `[312, 66, 300, 330]` → `[312, 66, 300, 444]`; with the card (grown 40 px for its shadow), the label boxes (3 px) and the status bar's live fps taken out, the worst pair of captures differs by 0–619 px a state for this build against HEAD and 0–517 px for HEAD against HEAD (sums 2 902 and 2 769) — DOM label antialiasing, which is why the canvas was captured alone — and by 0 px in the three `section-grid-C*` states either way. **Measured — two planes** (mock, grid C flipped + level L2; planes `[−1, 0, 0, 12]` and `[0, 0, −1, 5.2]`): the outline is 378 segments on 63 elements from 2 220 triangles, 25 draw calls in 3D and 24 in the east elevation; canvas-only at 1140 × 860, dark / light, its accent pixels are 10 469 / 9 771 in 3D and 8 457 / 8 294 in the elevation, against 12 231 / 11 837 for grid C alone (220 on 47) and 16 584 / 15 001 for level L2 alone (568 on 74); the level plane changes 13.7 % of the grid-only frame's pixels. `scripts/frame-triggers.cjs` gained the second plane's four changes — set beside a gridline cut (72.05 % of the pixels changed), its offset (7.86 %), its flip (72.03 %), its clear (42.11 %) — and passes **28 / 28**, with 0 scene renders in 2 s at rest. **Tests:** `tests/unit/section-planes.test.ts` (new: `createSection` against a recording host — the clips per slot, every re-aim case, previews, unknown names, refresh), two-plane cases in `picking`, `pick-grid` (grid against brute force), `space-pick`, `snap`, `annotation-store`, `markups` (families, per-plane chips, summaries, patches) and `selectors`; e2e — the card's layout (the rule, the summary on its own line and whole, with its `title`; the three buttons right-aligned under it), both cuts through the card, the offsets independent, flip one, preview one, a viewpoint named `grid C + level L2` saved and restored, Clear one, Clear all, the toolbar button. `tests/parity/2026-10-01-two-sections/README.md` has every table. |
| **2026-10-01 — a session, a share link and a viewpoint carry `sections` and the old `section` beside it; one normaliser reads either** | Nothing saved before may break, and nothing saved now may break an older build. **Writing:** `sessionPayload` and `viewpointOf` write **`sections: { grid, level }`** and, beside it, the legacy **`section`** in its old single-plane shape from `legacySection`: **the plane that is cutting**, when exactly one of the two set planes is; when neither cuts or both do, the gridline plane if it has a name, else the level plane; else the old "none" object (`{ kind: null, name: '', offset: 0, flip: false, cut: false }`). An older SGVue that opens a new session, link or viewpoint reads the one key it knows and restores one plane — the gridline cut when both were cutting — instead of meeting a shape it does not know; it ignores `sections`. *(The preference for the cutting plane is of later the same day, from the review of this change: until then the gridline plane came first whatever it was doing, so a previewed gridline beside a level cut reached an older build as the preview and the cut was lost.)* `section` is derived, never state: `SessionSource` omits it, so the two keys cannot disagree in what is written. **Reading:** one pure function, `sectionsOf(p)` in `src/shared/session-codec.ts`, used by `sessionPatch` (the session and the share link) and by `restoreView` (a viewpoint) — `sections` when it is an object, else the legacy `section` mapped onto the plane of its `kind` (`'grid'` → the gridline plane, `'level'` → the level plane, anything else → both empty), else `null`, which `sessionPatch` reads as "keep the live planes" and `restoreView` as "none". **It coerces untrusted input**, because a link is a string a person pasted and `localStorage` is editable: through `planeOf`, a name that is not a string becomes `''` — and so does one longer than 200 characters (`PLANE_NAME_MAX`, from the same review: a name is printed in the card's summary, a viewpoint's sub-line and the assistant's view state, and a grid tag or a storey name is a few characters) — an offset that is not a finite number becomes `0`, `flip` and `cut` are `true` only when exactly `true`, and a plane is rebuilt from those four values, so an unknown key never reaches the store; an array or a primitive where an object should be is an empty plane. A name the live federation does not have draws nothing, as before — the viewer looks it up and finds no plane. **Unchanged:** `RESTORE_ORDER` (section before camera — the restore step now pushes both planes in one `setSections`, and the camera that follows overrides the re-aim, as it did); the 4 MB cap; `frameKey`. `forgetDemoView`, `clearSections` and a viewpoint with neither key reset both planes. **Tests:** `session-codec.test.ts` — the legacy key is written (gridline first, then level, then none; the cutting plane when only one of the two cuts, each way round), a 201-character name is no plane in either shape and through a link, a new payload round-trips through the link codec, an old payload lands on its kind's plane, `sections` wins when both are present, a payload with neither keeps the live planes, hostile shapes are coerced (numbers and objects for names, `NaN` / `Infinity` / strings for offsets, `1` / `'true'` for booleans, arrays, `null`, extra keys); `visibility-store.test.ts` — a viewpoint writes both keys, an old viewpoint restores, a hostile one is coerced; e2e — the stored session carries both keys through every step, and a share link put back into the pre-2026-10-01 shape (one `section`, no `sections`) restores its level section, cutting, on a real file. |
| **2026-10-01 — each plane's cut outline is trimmed to the kept side of the other cutting plane** | With two planes cutting, what one removed is not drawn, so it must not be outlined by the other. **How:** `sectionCut(store, planes, include)` (`viewer/section-cut.ts`, still pure) takes the 0–2 cutting planes and runs 2026-09-28's cut once per plane, unchanged — the same on-plane rules, the same joining of collinear pieces — then hands each **joined** segment to `emit`, which tests its two ends against every other plane: both ends kept (within `ON_PLANE_EPS`) → pushed whole; both on the removed side, or one there and the other on the plane → dropped; one each side → cut at the crossing, `t = dₐ / (dₐ − d_b)`, in Float64, and the kept part pushed. Trimming after joining keeps a box 4 segments rather than 8 pieces and makes a straddling wall one shortened line. An element whose box does not reach the other plane's kept side (`boxReachesKept`) is skipped before any of its triangles is walked, which is most of what a second plane saves. **Not drawn, by decision:** the line where the two planes meet inside a solid — it is no surface triangle's edge, and the cap shading already shows that corner; drawing it would need the solid's interior, which the triangle walk does not have. With one plane `others` is empty, nothing is trimmed, and the result is the single-plane cut float for float (the one-plane cases of `section-cut.test.ts` are unchanged and pass). **Where:** still one instanced fat-line mesh and at most one recompute a frame — `setClips` calls `markCut()` where `setClip` / `clearClip` did, and every edge rebuild still does; `debug().cut` is `{ segments, elements, triangles, ms, planes }`, summed over both. **Measured** on what produced 2026-09-28's numbers — the 5.58 M-triangle synthetic model (17 600 elements), warm median of 11, this machine, seven runs of this build and four of HEAD: **both planes cutting 6.5 / 6.6 / 7.1 / 6.9 / 6.2 / 7.2 / 6.3 ms** (8 334 segments on 490 elements from 222 968 triangles; cold 8.0–12.0 ms), both with the gridline plane lying on a face 6.9 / 7.4 / 7.4 / 7.1 / 6.7 / 7.1 / 6.6 ms (8 510 / 671 / 277 040), one plane moving while the other stays 6.5 / 6.2 / 6.8 / 6.2 / 5.7 / 6.4 / 5.7 ms. One plane, this build against HEAD on the same machine in the same session: level 6.6 / 6.0 / 6.7 / 5.9 / 5.6 / 6.2 / 6.3 against 5.6 / 6.1 / 6.3 / 5.6, grid 7.2 / 7.4 / 7.5 / 7.5 / 7.4 / 7.3 / 7.5 against 6.6 / 7.2 / 7.3 / 6.8, the on-plane worst case 9.3 / 9.2 / 9.1 / 9.3 / 9.3 / 9.1 / 8.9 against 8.7 / 8.8 / 9.4 / 9.4 (HEAD cold 11.7–13.4) — so a few tenths of a millisecond for the extra box test and the call through `emit`, and two planes cost no more than one, because each plane's walk is cut short by the other's box test. Still no worker and no chunking. On the mock: 378 segments on 63 elements with grid C flipped + level L2, where grid C alone is 220 on 47 and level L2 alone 568 on 74. **Tests** (`section-cut.test.ts`): a segment that straddles the other plane, cut at the crossing; the untrimmed cut when the other plane removes nothing, and nothing when it removes everything; an end lying on the other plane kept, a segment only touching it dropped; trimming by each other plane in turn; `sectionCut` outlining both planes of one model in either order, an element out of the other plane's reach not walked (its triangles not counted); a flipped other plane; one plane alone equal to the single cut, float for float; and the mock's core wall under a level cut and a gridline cut. |
| **2026-10-01 — `set_section` sets one cut and leaves the other alone; one string names what is on** | What the tool's `kind` means follows the two planes. `kind:"grid"` sets the gridline cut and leaves the level cut alone; `kind:"level"` the reverse; **a `kind` with `name:""` or no name clears just that cut**; `kind:null` clears both; a name the federation does not have still changes nothing and returns the valid list (`valid_values`), as before. It acts through the card's own `setSec(kind, patch, open)`, so nothing about undo changes (a section is not undoable) and the card opens as it did. The result's message says when the other cut is on and untouched (`… The gridline section at C is unchanged.`), so one cut is never read as having replaced the other — the behaviour until today. The result's `section` and `get_view_state`'s `section` are one string from one function, `sectionsLabel` (`shared/sections.ts`, also the viewpoint sub-line): `grid C`, `level L2`, `grid C + level L2`, or `null`. The description in `shared/tool-schemas.ts` says exactly this in the catalogue's style, still opening with the design's own sentence. What a call writes to the plane it names — the defaults, the `cut` input, the clears — is the next row's. **Checked:** the read-only guard and the catalogue tests pass; `ai-executors.test.ts` covers independent planes, clear one, clear both, an unknown name and the view-state string; the evaluation suite still grades — oracle 36 / 36 and null 0 / 36, neither calling the API. **The runner on Windows:** `scripts/ai-eval.cjs` ended before its first case there, on HEAD and on this change alike — it destroys its probe window before opening the first case's, a window per case leaves none between two cases, and the real main quits on `window-all-closed` everywhere but macOS (the next `loadFile` failed with ERR_FAILED (-2), and the process exited 0). It now keeps one hidden, empty window open for the run on Windows and Linux — made after the strays are listed, loading nothing, in no case — and none on macOS, so nothing changes there; the documented commands are unchanged, and both scores above are from the runner itself, with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` unset. |
| **2026-10-01 — the assistant should be able to do whatever the user can do in the app; `set_section` is the first application, and it does what the user's click does** | The owner, asked whether an assistant-made section should actually cut — until then `set_section` wrote a name, an offset and a side and never `cut`, the design's own patch, so on a plane whose `cut` was off the assistant's section was a previewed sheet: *"yes. assistant should possess everything user can do on the app. that would be the best case scenario."* **The direction that sets:** whatever a user can do in the app, the assistant should be able to do through its tools — bounded, as the user is, by the viewer-only rule (no tool writes model data, a file or a path; a tool's `kind` stays `read` or `view`). It is a direction for the whole catalogue, applied here to one tool; the audit of the others against it is separate work. **`set_section` under it** (`ai/executors/view.ts`): (1) *Setting* a plane — a name the plane does not already have — is the chip's own patch, `planePatch(name, kind)` in `selectors/section.ts` (the set half of `chipPatch`, split out so the two cannot drift): that name, **cutting**, at the design's default offset for the kind — 0 for a gridline, `LEVEL_DEFAULT_OFFSET_MM` (1 200) for a level, one constant, moved to `shared/sections.ts` so that the tool's description states the same number rather than a copy of it — plus whatever the call passes; an omitted `flip` is not flipped, as before. (2) *Changing* the plane already set at that name changes only what the call passes: **an omitted `offset`, `flip` or `cut` keeps what the plane has — one rule, no exception** — the way the card's `−500` / `+500`, `flip side` and `cut` each change one thing, and its offset and flip controls never touch `cut`. So a plane the user positioned by hand is flipped, moved or previewed without being put back on its gridline, and a previewed plane that is moved or flipped is still a preview. The same kind and name again never clears the plane, which is what a second click on the live chip does. (3) One new optional input, **`cut`** (strict schema, zod, executor): `false` is the card's `cut` button off — the plane shown as a preview — and `true` is it on; omitted, a **new** plane cuts (the chip's patch) and a plane already set keeps its state. So `{kind, name}` on a new plane cuts; `{kind, name, cut:false}` previews it; `{kind, name, offset:500}` on that previewed plane moves it and leaves it a preview; `{kind, name, cut:true}` cuts it. The first build of this change had made an omitted `cut` cut on a change as well, and told the model to pass `cut:false` again to keep a preview; that exception was ruled out the same day — one rule is what the card does, and it is what the model can be told in one sentence ("an omitted offset, flip or cut keeps the plane's current state"). (4) The two clears are the card's own — `kind:null` is `Clear all` (`clearSections()`), a `kind` with no name is that block's `Clear` (`NO_PLANE`) — where they wrote the name alone before and left a nameless plane's offset, side and lit `cut` behind. (5) The result says what happened — `Section cut at grid C.` / `Section previewed at grid C.`, with `The level section at L2 is unchanged.` when the other plane is on — and `section` is the one string as before. **Everything a user can do to a plane is one call:** set (`kind`, `name`), re-offset (+ `offset`), flip (+ `flip`), preview ⇄ cut (+ `cut`), clear one (`name:""`), clear all (`kind:null`). **Not done, and it belongs to the audit:** the view state still says only which planes are on (`grid C + level L2`), not their offset, side or cut — so the assistant can change a hand-set plane without disturbing it, but cannot *report* where it stands. **Tests:** `ai-executors.test.ts` — the default cut and both default offsets (equal to what the two chips write), an explicit offset and side with zero as a value, `cut:false` and `cut:true`, a new name starting from the defaults whatever the plane had, the sequence new plane cuts → `cut:false` previews → `offset` moves the preview → `cut:true` cuts it, the same plane re-sent changing only `cut`, only `flip`, only `offset`, a bare re-send changing nothing and saying what the plane is, a hand-set plane previewed in place, a bubble-previewed plane left a preview until `cut:true`, a non-boolean `cut` refused; `ai-tools.test.ts` — the five inputs, the strict schema, the description's sentences with the constant in them, the zod side; `markups.test.ts` — `planePatch` equals the chip's set branch. The evaluation suite scores oracle 36 / 36 and null 0 / 36; its `op-section` oracle call, `{ kind: "grid", name: "C" }`, needed no change. |
| **2026-10-01 — the assistant is Vee, and its mascot is a pixel sprite: a pure core, a whole number of device pixels to a cell, one clock that renders nothing** | Asked for by the owner in these words: *"Can you help me update the ai assistant name and animations?"* — with a design handoff, "Ask Vee". **The handoff is kept unchanged in `design-reference/ask-vee/` and is the specification for the assistant's name, its mascot and its thinking-process motion** (`README.md` the spec, `ask-thinking-hex.jsx` the reference code, option 1a "Hex" the mascot); `design-reference/README.md` gains the one line that says so. Its stills and `img/ask.webp` show only the demo building. **Scale.** The handoff says its sizes are "app px (screenshot ÷ 1.2)". The screenshot was taken at 150 % display scaling: against the real panel (330 × 420, bubble text 12.5 px, send button 32 px) the jsx's 503.5 × 640 panel, 18 px text and 48 px button are 1.52, 1.44 and 1.5 times the app's — 1.5 within what a tracing gives, and nowhere near 1.2. So **jsx px ÷ 1.5 = app px** (README px ÷ 1.25), the panel in the handoff is today's panel, and every existing element keeps its style string; only what is new is sized from the handoff. **Two steps; this is the first:** the name and the mascot at rest (`idle`). The thinking trace, the motion, the stop control and the left-aligned "You" bubble are the second, and the busy row is untouched. **The name.** One constant, `ASSISTANT = 'Vee'`, behind `authorOf(message)` in `selectors/chat.ts` — the design writes `'SGVue'` three times (`SGVue.dc.html:1597`, `:2017`, `:2038`) for a message's label, the reply strip and the quote a reply carries to the model, and `ai/bridge.ts` now calls the same function. The title is `Ask Vee`, the pill `Ask Vee` (the design's `Ask`), the Schedules window's toasts `Schedule from Ask Vee` / `Schedule changed by Ask Vee`. `SGVue` stays wherever it names the **app**: About, the window title, the sidebar's brand, `EMPTY_COPY`'s "SGVue never writes to your model", the workbook's creator, the design's own instruction lines. Code comments that call the feature "Ask SGVue" were left: no user reads them. **The model is told**, in one added instruction line after the design's two opening lines (`Your name is Vee. You are the assistant inside SGVue, and SGVue is the app rather than you: when the user asks who you are or what to call you, say that you are Vee.`) — because a reply's quote now reaches it as `[replying to Vee: …]` and it had never been told the name is its own. No design line is removed or reworded; 28 → 29 lines. **The sprite's pure core** (`app/vee-grid.ts`, no DOM): `veeGrid(state, frame)` is the handoff's `hexGrid` ported verbatim — 16 × 16 cells, the 12 × 12 body at (2, 3), `PING` / `HOP` / `BOB`, the rim order `HEX_PERIM` — with all five states, though the app shows only `idle` yet. `tests/unit/vee-grid.test.ts` cuts `HPAL` … `hexGrid` out of the jsx, runs it, and requires the same 256 cells for every state at every frame of two whole 336-frame cycles; then asserts what the README says each state draws. Also there: the palette as **roles** (per theme, a token's name — read from the document when drawn, so it is the variable and never a copy of its hex — or a constant), the three slots and the frame offsets (both read back out of the jsx in the test), and the two rules below. **The device-pixel rule.** A sprite is crisp only if every cell is the same whole number of device pixels.
